# Contracts (locked in GRO-1961)

Source of truth for types: [`shared/types.ts`](../shared/types.ts). This file is the prose companion.

## Repo layout / scripts

```
package.json          npm workspaces: client, desktop. Root scripts:
                        dev            electron-vite dev (cwd desktop/): builds main + preload, serves the renderer with HMR, launches Electron
                        build          electron-vite build → desktop/out/{main,preload,renderer}
                        desktop:build  build, then electron-builder --mac → desktop/dist-app (.app + dmg, F1 — see "Packaging"); the root version is injected via extraMetadata
                        typecheck      tsc -p client && tsc -p shared && tsc -p desktop
                        test           vitest run  (FOUR projects: client=jsdom, desktop=node, tools=node, perf=jsdom — client/vitest.perf.config.ts, the budget tripwires kept off the main run's contended pool)
                        e2e            build, then playwright test --config desktop/e2e/playwright.config.ts (G1 — Playwright-Electron smoke suite)
                        e2e:only       the same Playwright suite on the existing desktop/out, no rebuild
                        perf           tools/perf/run.mjs: the local CDP perf harness on the packaged app (A/B with --vs, ABBA order; see tools/perf/README.md). Never in CI
                        perf:budget    tools/perf/measureBudget.mjs: the size and integrity gate on desktop/out + the packaged .app and DMG (run after desktop:build; see "Packaging")
                        perf:budget:ci the same gate on desktop/out alone (--out-only; after build) — what CI runs
client/               The renderer: React 19 + TS, @milkdown/crepe 7.22.x, plus ONE lazily-loaded dependency: `@excalidraw/excalidraw` PINNED to 0.18.1 (YAZ-878 — the module is imported only by `drawings/renderScene.ts`, and only through a dynamic `import()` on the first drawing preview or modal open, so it never enters the main chunk; its stylesheet is a second dynamic import, made by the modal's surface alone, YAZ-879). No scripts of its own — electron-vite (desktop/electron.vite.config.ts) builds it with ./index.html as the entry, and its renderer config `dedupe`s `react`/`react-dom` — 🔒 load-bearing since YAZ-879: npm hoists an OLDER react beside the Excalidraw tree at the repo root, and without the dedupe `<Excalidraw>` mounts against a second React and dies on a null dispatcher. Every entry below lives under client/src/:
  App.tsx                 composes this window's root + `useWorkspace` identity, window-local `sidebarCollapsed` (YAZ-1280), Sidebar, retained main editor layers, and the full-editor RightPanel; global settings/width still follow `storage.subscribe`, while the lens (window identity since YAZ-1628) and both panel visibility states persist independently per window. Owns shared watch/index/properties/sync feeds, menu/link/file lifecycle listeners, renderer ⌘B, the renderer ⌘X/⌘C/⌘V listener (asks the Sidebar's `clipboardRef`, YAZ-1674), the ⌘K uncollapse/focus handshake, rename/delete carry-before-remap ordering, external-rename notice, the one passive toast (`notify(text, kind)` — bottom-left, iconed, D10), title/hash, theme, and responsive split-vs-overlay derivation; a null root renders Welcome with no auto-dialog.
  Welcome.tsx             the Welcome screen (C2, GRO-2164): app name, recents as one-click rows (dead folder → "Folder not found" + MRU drop), Open folder… (+ test)
  api.ts                  `api` = window.yaseenDocs in `CONTRACT`'s shape, looked up at call time: invokes reject with BridgeRequestError, pushes and the specials pass through; the renderer's one door to main (+ test)
  bridge.d.ts             `window.yaseenDocs: YaseenDocsApi` (derived from `CONTRACT` in shared/ipc.ts; installed by desktop/src/preload)
  editor/                 Editor dispatches BEFORE mounting an owner: a path the Files tree shows as a FOLDER gets `views/FolderView` (YAZ-2290 D3, see "Folders are the pages"); otherwise by the shared file kind — Markdown gets the Crepe host + conflict bar (the scroller holds the page title, properties, Crepe, the comment stream — YAZ-1472, see "Comments" — "Linked mentions" and "Reviews"), while view-only kinds delegate to `viewers/` and never mount Crepe, autosave, frontmatter or backlinks owners. createCrepe (locked factory, see "Editor rules"), featureConfig (Crepe feature allowlist + guard test), drawingMenu (the Drawing slash item on Crepe's own BlockEdit menu, YAZ-877), drawing/drawingPreview + drawingPreview.css (decoration-only `![[x.excalidraw]]` previews, see rule 27; the editing MODAL a preview click opens is React beside the editor, `src/drawings/`, rule 28), image/ (YAZ-1656, rule 30: imageSrc — the `app://vault` URL builder + Obsidian's `alt|width` parse/format; imageView + .css — THE ONE node view, on the commonmark `image` node; insertImage — paste/drop bytes → `assets/images/`; imageOptions — the ctx slice that carries the host's options to the paste handler; ImageModal + .css — the lightbox), listItemRoundTrip (empty-item round trip), SaveIndicator — this list used to name a `frontmatter` module too; there has never been one here, only `frontmatter.test.ts`, which covers `shared/frontmatter.ts` (rule 1's split/re-prepend) from the surface that consumes it — TOMBSTONE: baseEmbed/ and baseCodeBlock/ (the `![[X.base]]` decoration plugin and the ```base node view) were deleted with the format in YAZ-844, see rules 20/21
  editor/marks/           underline (mark: Mod-u ↔ `<u>…</u>` inline HTML, $remark + $markSchema + $shortcut) (+ test), highlight (mark with an optional `color`, see rule 31) (+ test), htmlPairs (shared inline-HTML pair wrapper for underline + coloured highlight), markTestKit (the shared mark test harness)
  editor/outline/         outlineFolding ($prose plugin: every foldable bullet folds — parents and image bullets, YAZ-1709) + outlineFoldKeys + outlineFolding.css, listCommands (outliner keymap), hotkeys (Obsidian hotkeys), zoom + zoom.css (zoom into a bullet), guideLines + guideLines.css (click-to-fold guide lines), listNodes (shared helpers), bullets.css (depth glyphs) (+ tests)
  drawings/               createDrawing (YAZ-877): the empty-scene constant + the clock name + the never-overwrite create loop over `api.writeAsset`, the host half of the editor's Drawing slash item; drawingScene (YAZ-878: `isDrawingTarget` + the base64 → UTF-8 → JSON read over `api.readAsset`, throwing for everything that is not a scene — plus `openDrawing`, YAZ-879's load, which keeps the resolved path and mtime a save needs), renderScene (the ONE lazy `@excalidraw/excalidraw` module import + `exportToSvg`, and where `window.EXCALIDRAW_ASSET_PATH` / `EXCALIDRAW_EXPORT_SOURCE` are pinned before it), drawingFeed (the subscribe/poke refresh source), and the modal (YAZ-879, rule 28): DrawingModal + drawingModal.css (the chrome — overlay, Save, dirty, discard strip, error line), ExcalidrawSurface (🔒 THE ENGINE SEAM: the only `<Excalidraw>` mount and the only serializer), saveDrawing (the write + the canvas-wins CONFLICT retry) (+ tests)
  editor/wikilink/        wikilinkPlugin + wikilink.css (decoration-only [[wikilink]] rendering, see rule 23), wikilinkClick + createFromLink (Links C click navigation + create-on-click, rule 23), wikilinkPicker + wikilinkPicker.css (the `[[` completion popup + its keymap, see rule 24), wikilinkMenu (the right-click menu on a rendered id link — the note's name or `<folder> (folder)`, its id, Copy ID; YAZ-2293, see rule 23), WikilinkIndexBridge (the one Markdown index feed plus the separate tree-catalog navigation feed; root changes retire old view-only rows before either new feed can compose) (+ tests)
  hooks/                  useFile (Markdown + strict view-only text load; migration/frontmatter ownership remains Markdown-only), useViewOnlyCatalog (structural-watch Files-tree catalog, on the window's one tree feed `lib/treeFeed.ts`, YAZ-2191), useAutosave (debounce/flush/conflict; frontmatter-only external changes absorb silently, GRO-2186, and a frontmatter-only CONFLICT retries once, YAZ-2175), useWatch (one bridge watch() per root, fan-out), usePickFolder (native dialog)
  viewers/                TextViewer (selectable monospaced read-only text with two-axis overflow), PdfViewer (revoked Blob URL around Chromium's passive native PDF frame), and ImageViewer (exact binary bytes decoded once and painted as one static canvas frame) (+ tests)
  lib/                    pure logic with unit tests: autosave state machine, storage (bridge-backed in-memory cache of the main-owned app state + window identity; init() before the first render, subscribe() for live changes), treeState, paths, windowTitle, theme (resolve the Appearance setting to light/dark, K), urlHash (the #/abs/path.md mirror, rule 17), renameContinuity (Links E1 GRO-2194 / E1b GRO-2241: the per-path autosave handle map + dirty-buffer carry across a rename — per affected open path for a folder rename — see "Links": Rename; ALSO `retireDeletedPath`/`retireDeletedDir` for GRO-2272, which retire ONLY — a delete has no destination, so capturing or stashing a buffer would be a resurrection vector), dragSlot (YAZ-1471: `insertionSlot` / `dropIndex`, the insertion-slot arithmetic BOTH horizontal tab strips reorder by — the window's tab bar and a folder's view tabs — one spelling so the two cannot drift apart), pageLabel (YAZ-2290: `isFolderPath` / `useFolderPaths` — whether a tab path is a FOLDER, by the window's newest Files tree — and `pageLabel`, a tab path as a name: a file hides Markdown's extension, a folder keeps its whole name), selection (the sidebar multi-select reducer + `orderedSelection`, YAZ-1336/1338), windowChord (the ONE ownership boundary for renderer window chords), sidebarHotkey / copyPathHotkey / fileClipboardHotkey (⌘B YAZ-1280, ⌘⇧C YAZ-1338, ⌘X/⌘C/⌘V YAZ-1674 — each a key on that boundary), notice (`LINK_NOTICE_MS`, `NoticeKind`, `Notice` — the toast's shared type, YAZ-1674), leadingTrailing (the watcher-burst gate: the first event after a quiet spell reads at once, a burst coalesces into ONE trailing read, YAZ-2240), treeFeed (the window's ONE `fs:tree` feed per root, YAZ-2191), windowFlush (the close/quit flush registry for the writers below App, YAZ-2174)
  components/             ContextMenuSurface.tsx (action-free context-menu mechanics for menus whose commands stay domain-owned), NoticeIcon.tsx (the toast's per-kind stroke glyphs, D10)
  links/                  completion.ts — the ONE [[…]] completion matcher (Links B, GRO-2191): trailingLinkFragment / matchLinkCandidates (ends-trimmed, case-insensitive over the candidate name, ranked exact → prefix → substring, cap 8 applied after ranking — GRO-2197; TOMBSTONE, YAZ-2293: matchLinkNames, the same match over plain names, went when the cell editors began completing over candidates) / linkCandidates (LinkCandidate { name, insert, label } rows from index records: shortest unambiguous names + one alias row per frontmatter alias, Links E2 GRO-2214 — every row of a note that has an `id` INSERTS that id, a note without one its name or the piped alias form, YAZ-2293) / basenameCandidates (YAZ-2293: the index basenames as candidates, each inserting the note's `id` when it has one — what the cell editors complete over), shared by EditableCell's link editors and the editor's [[ picker (rule 24); renameLinks.ts — the automatic link updates after an in-app rename/move (Links E1 GRO-2194, folders + cross-dir moves E1b GRO-2241: maskCode / rewriteBodyLinks / rewriteNoteLinks / updateLinksAfterRename + countLinkReferences, the E1c dry-run referencing-set count — Markdown sources/targets use index records, while explicit view-only targets use the separately pinned catalog described under "Links": Rename); renameDetector.ts — the E1c external-rename hypothesis detector (GRO-2242: detectRenames with the locked guard rails, diffRecords for the while-running snapshot feed, preRenameRecords to synthesise the engine's pre-rename view); useExternalRenames.ts — the E1c queue/banner hook (both feeds → confirm-first banner state, session dismissed set, in-app-rename suppression, the Update repair+rewrite flow — see "Links": Rename → External renames); backlinks.ts — the Links D referencing set + context snippets (GRO-2193: `backlinksFor` over an index snapshot through the shared resolver, memoized per (records, path); `mentionSnippets` over one note's raw content, code-masked, for the expanded rows — one snippet per LINE, rendered as display text through `linkDisplayText` with every mention on it highlighted, GRO-2197), BacklinksSection.tsx + backlinks.css — the collapsible "Linked mentions (N)" block at the bottom of a note or a folder's page (see Editor rule 25); folderLinks.ts — links to FOLDERS (YAZ-2290 D10: `vaultDirs`, `folderResolver`, `linkResolver` — the path-level resolver every wikilink surface shares, a note first and a folder only when none answers — `folderLinkCandidates` for the `[[` picker, `pageResolver` for the views and the properties panel (a link to a folder reads as the folder's name) and `belongsToBasenames` for a link column narrowed to a folder; see "Links": Links to folders); shortcuts.ts — the one door to `also_in` and what a folder shows (YAZ-2290 D2/D4, see "Note shortcuts"); reservedKeys.ts — `RESERVED_KEYS`, the frontmatter keys the app owns (+ tests)
  comments/               the comment stream (YAZ-1472, see "Comments"): CommentsSection.tsx + comments.css (the block under a note's body and under a folder's views — the properties-panel header, one card per thread, bullet-style folds, the one composer in its three seats; a VIEW over CrepeHost's `disk`, no watcher and no read of its own), markdown.ts (`commentHtml` — the ONE Markdown→HTML door in the app, `marked` gfm+breaks → DOMPurify — and `commentSummary`, the folded row's first line); the model is `shared/comments.ts`, whose contract test sits here as comments.test.ts so vitest's client project collects it (+ tests)
  sidebar/                Sidebar, VaultSwitcher (the header's recent-vaults panel, YAZ-1767 — one-line rows with a hover ⓘ full path and vault display names since YAZ-1974 — and its right-click vault menu, YAZ-1798 — items as data in `vaultMenuSections.ts`; see "Multi-window": Open beside), Tree (the ONE tree both lenses draw; since YAZ-2290 a folder row opens the folder's tab, shows its note count and carries its shortcut rows — "Folders are the pages", "Note shortcuts"), ShortcutPicker ("Add note shortcut") and folderCounts (`folderCounts` → `{ counts, shortcuts }`, one pass per index snapshot). The lens row is "The sidebar lenses". The sidebar chrome is TWO rows since YAZ-847: ROW 1 the lens tablist (Files ⇄ Favorites; `DEFAULT_SIDEBAR_LENS` = Files since YAZ-1846 — a new window and a switch to a different vault land on it; the per-window `WindowEntry.sidebarLens` since YAZ-1628) — carrying, right-aligned at its end, the ⚡ YAZ-862 expand/collapse-all button: ONE double-chevron acting on the dirs the ACTIVE lens has ON SCREEN (`bodyDirs`), in a single `setAll` dispatch on the one `expanded` set both lenses share (a SESSION bucket since YAZ-1642: shared by every window on the vault, never restored by a launch); it collapses those dirs whenever ANY of them is open and expands them only when none is, and it is GONE rather than disabled while a query is typed and whenever the active lens has no folder to unfold — and ROW 2 the persistent search bar (YAZ-801) — ALWAYS visible, on BOTH lenses, loading/error/empty vault included (a bar that came and went with the tree would be a view, which is the rescinded design), Esc clears a typed query and only the SECOND press gives up focus, ⌘K focuses it (the App handshake above); while `query` is non-empty the body shows a FLAT ranked results list INSTEAD of the tree (🔒 flat-list ruling, YAZ-739) — and that list also carries the loaded tree's FOLDERS as rows (🔒 D1–D2, YAZ-1491 — fed from the Sidebar's own `dirs` memo, `folderCandidates` spliced ahead of `searchCandidates` so a folder sits first within a rank bucket; a note still never matches on its folder name, the folder itself is one row), choosing a folder row OPENS its page as a tab, exactly as a note row opens the note (one `activate` rule serves Enter, ⌘⏎ and click alike; ⌘ opens a background tab), and folder rows carry a glyph and a `, folder` aria-label suffix (🔒 D4), replacing the ACTIVE lens tab's body while the tabs row stays live and a lens switch mid-search keeps the query (🔒 D5, YAZ-847) — a conditional render, never a teardown, so every bit of tree state (data, expansion, pending create/rename, drag) is waiting untouched when it clears — fed by `client/src/search/` (ranking engine YAZ-802, `useSearchResults` + `SearchResults` YAZ-803; the hook's index feed is LAZY since YAZ-808: no `index(root)` read and no watch subscription until the first non-empty query, latched on for good after it, because the always-on per-window index feed is WikilinkIndexBridge's) and driven entirely from the input, which never loses focus: ↑/↓ clamp at both ends and never wrap (the `[[` picker's rule) and clamp against the RESULT COUNT too, so an index refresh that shrinks the list under the selection highlights the last row rather than nothing (YAZ-808); on a NOTE row ⏎ opens it in place, ⌘⏎ in a background tab, a row click opens and ⌘-click background-opens (I3) — the list stays up either way — and every one of those gestures on a FOLDER row opens the folder's page the same way; the blank-space context menu is the TREE's, so right-clicking the results' blank space offers nothing ("New note" there would have no target) — not even Electron's text menu, which the body's handler default-prevents (YAZ-2050) — while a result ROW opens that row's own tree menu through the same `openMenu` (YAZ-2050, `SearchResults.onRowContextMenu`; the right-click also highlights the row): 🔒 D1 it follows the FILES rules on every tab, a search row being a disk row — `MenuTargets.lens` pins `'files'` for a search row (the active lens otherwise) and every lens rule in the menu path reads it (`focusOn`, `startCreate`'s Favorites hop); 🔒 D2 the items that draw INTO the hidden tree (Rename, New note / dated note / folder / dated folder, Focus) first leave the search through `onRevealInFiles` (App flips the lens to Files and issues the same reveal request the tab menu uses; the Files reveal accepts a dir, which opens ITSELF too via the create menu's synthetic-child `expandTo`) — `MenuTargets.leaveSearchTo` names the row, `viaTree` calls `onRevealInFiles` with it, the reveal clears the query, ends a hiding focus, expands and flashes the row — and every other item acts in place with the query kept; multi-select never reaches this menu (the selection clears when a query starts); ⌘-click on a file row opens it in a BACKGROUND TAB of this window (the locked I3 ruling, GRO-2235); the file- and folder-row context menus carry New note / dated note / folder / dated folder ("New note" is born from the target folder's `.template.md`, YAZ-2290 E3; `EntryKind` is `file` | `dir`; TOMBSTONE: "New base" and its `base` `EntryKind` were deleted with the format in YAZ-844), on a FOLDER row Open (YAZ-2290 D3), "Review this folder" (YAZ-2322) and "Add note shortcut" (YAZ-2290 D2), on a note row the review toggle (YAZ-2322), Rename (files Links E1 GRO-2194, folders E1b GRO-2241 — inline input replacing the row label, prefilled name minus extension for files / raw name for folders, Enter commits / Esc-blur cancels; no hotkey v1 — F2 is a polish note), Copy path, Open in ▸ New window (D2, GRO-2168 — context menu only since I3; a flyout leaf since YAZ-1674) — TOMBSTONE: the type-conditional "New ▸ <type>" submenu and "New type…" (`NewTypeDialog`) were deleted with the type system in YAZ-836; Delete (GRO-2272 — opens ConfirmDelete, never deletes directly) and Open in ▸ Reveal in Finder (GRO-2274 — a flyout leaf since YAZ-1674; file rows, folder rows AND blank space, where it targets the vault ROOT); the menu is DATA → sections → render since YAZ-1674 (🔒 D8): `menuSections.ts` holds every gating rule as one small function returning a `MenuItem` or null and the pure `buildMenuSections(targets, handlers)`; `ContextMenu` takes `{ x, y, sections, onClose }` and only draws — a `role="group"` per NON-EMPTY section with a CSS rule between adjacent groups, one button per item whose only text child is the bare label (the ⌘X / ⌘C / ⌘V / ⌘⇧C hints are drawn from `data-hint`, so `textContent` and the accessible name stay what four test files pin); menu ORDER is SEVEN groups (🔒 D7 as amended; YAZ-2249 splits Create in two): [Open · Open N in new tabs · Focus on folder] · [Cut · Copy · Paste · Copy N paths · Copy path · Copy ID · Copy for Agent] · [New note · New folder] · [New dated note · New dated folder] · [Review this folder · Turn review on/off · Rename] · [Add to favorites · Add note shortcut · Open in ▸] · [Delete — or, on a shortcut row, Remove shortcut] — empty groups are skipped (a single file row starts with the clipboard group), "Open N in new tabs" still leads the whole menu while "Copy N paths" moved down beside "Copy path" (a deliberate loosening of YAZ-1337 🔒 D5), "Copy ID" (YAZ-2293 — the `copyId` leaf, see "Note ids": Copy ID) sits between "Copy path" and "Copy for Agent" on a NOTE row whose note has an `id` and on nothing else, and Delete is LAST and alone (GRO-2272 `C1a-`, destructive-last, pinned by a test in Sidebar.test.tsx); "Open in ▸" is the one PARENT item (`MenuParent.children`, one level deep): its flyout holds [New window · VS Code · Default app] · [Reveal in Finder] with every child's own gate intact, opens on hover, click, ArrowRight or Enter, one flyout at a time, closes when the pointer enters a different top-level item or on ArrowLeft / a first Escape, flips to the LEFT of the parent when it would spill off the window's right edge, and wears its chevron as a masked SVG in CSS (never text); blank space also carries Copy path targeting the vault root (GRO-2273); each menu item resolves its OWN target through `MenuTargets` — no item derives visibility from another's value (GRO-2296); ConfirmDelete is the in-app delete sheet (never a native dialog; focus starts on Cancel, `deleteConfirmMessage` owns the copy, counts come from the loaded tree + vault index so the delete path makes no extra fetch); menu and submenu clamp to the viewport edges (GRO-2204); RenameInline is the CreateInline-idiom input for Rename; dragging a FILE row onto a folder row (or the root header) MOVES it there (E1b — the TabBar HTML5-drag idiom, dropDir highlight, same rename pipeline; no multi-select, no folder dragging — Future; Cut ⌘X / Paste ⌘V, YAZ-1674, moves a multi-selection and folders through the same rename pipeline); hooks/ holds what YAZ-2202 moved out of Sidebar.tsx as-is (YAZ-2131 🔒 D10): useVaultTree (the tree and its watcher refresh, both expansions, the three Focus Mode lists, favorites, the missing-file checks), rowGestures (multi-select, the row drags, the file clipboard, inline create and rename) and useSidebarSearch (the search bar's query, results, highlighted row and ⌘K focus)
  workspace/              useWorkspace (the ONE renderer owner for main tabs + right-panel pages, retained mounts, both session histories, lifecycle repair, strict transfers, and one complete identity mirror) + the private pageDrag MIME codec (YAZ-966) (+ tests)
  tabs/                   TabBar + tabs.css: main tab semantics, Back/Forward, context actions, local reorder (on `lib/dragSlot` since YAZ-1471) and validated right→main insertion slots, the strip scrolling with no scrollbar through `app.css`'s shared `.scroll-strip`; durable ownership lives in workspace/, not here (+ tests)
  right-panel/            RightPanel + right-panel.css: presentational toolbar/header stack, resize/hide/overlay behavior, retained viewer layers, context action, and validated main/right insertion slots (+ tests)
  test-setup.ts           jsdom stubs (observers, Range rects) + the afterAll that cancels Milkdown's never-cleared readiness timeouts before jsdom is torn down (`test-timers.ts`, YAZ-2233; @milkdown/ctx 7.22.2 fixes it upstream) (+ test)
  views/                  viewSchema (1B: parseViews/serializeViews/updateViews over a yaml Document, unknown keys + comments preserved — since YAZ-844 the view SCHEMA a folder's `views` block round-trips through, no longer a file format; TOMBSTONE: BaseHost, the main-pane host for `.base`, died with the format), FolderView + folderView.css (YAZ-2290: a folder opened as a tab — title, the folder's own properties, the views over its notes, its comments, its linked mentions; the ONLY mount of ViewsPane, see "Folders are the pages"), ViewsPane (4A view chrome; 4B table for `type: table`, 4D board, 4E cards, 4F list, the outline document for `type: outline`, placeholder row list for unknown view types, see "View chrome"), folderSettings (YAZ-830: the one door to `folder_settings` — `folderSettings` over a folder's `.folder.md` record, the defaults, view-scoped typing, the whole-key writer and `writeFolderColumn`; see "Folder settings: `.folder.md`"), deleteColumn (YAZ-1513: the one function behind "Delete column…"), outlineDoc (YAZ-900: the outline document's grammar — parse / serialise / the block-start escape / the rename walk; TOMBSTONE (YAZ-2290): outlineSync and folderSync, which read belonging out of the outline and synced a disk folder into it), useIndex (2C: the vault index over the bridge, watch-driven refetch), useProperties + propertiesStub (YAZ-835: the vault-wide property declarations over the bridge, live via `properties:changed`, App-owned since YAZ-846, plus the in-memory test double that implements the same `YaseenDocsApi['properties']` shape — see "Properties"), testRecords + testFolderHost (the shared client-test fixtures — the 8-note index snapshot, and the `FolderHost` bundle every `ViewsPane` mount needs; they are two files because the main-process index test deep-equals the first one and desktop's tsconfig has no `jsx`), writeProperty (5B write path, GRO-2141), editorType (5B editor inference, GRO-2142), newNote (5D seed derivation + the `freeName` scheme, GRO-2144), scaffold (`createNote` — the ONE birth: a new note from its folder's `.template.md` and the seed, YAZ-2290 E1/E3 — and `ensureFolder`; TOMBSTONE: the type scaffold, per-type templates, starter bases and `createType` died with the type system in YAZ-836), view/ (toolbar, menus, keys, TableView, BoardView, CardsView, ListView, OutlineView + OutlineEditor (YAZ-901/903, the outline skin: the bullets-only Milkdown instance over one free-form document), EditableCell, groupDrag, cardWidth, GroupHeader (incl. `ViewFolder`, the context that tells the skins which folder a shortcut row is marked against), icons incl. `ShortcutIcon`), views.css (+ tests)
desktop/              Electron 43 shell (electron-vite 5, electron-builder): main + preload; the renderer is ../client. package.json's `build` block is the electron-builder config (F1); build/ holds icon.icns; dist-app/ is the packaged output (gitignored)
  electron.vite.config.ts     the three builds (main, preload, renderer rooted at ../client); @shared alias for all of them (+ electron.vite.config.test.ts: the renderer's hidden source maps move out of the build, YAZ-2183)
  src/contract.test.ts        every `CONTRACT` / `SPECIAL` door has exactly one main handler and one preload exposure, of the right kind (YAZ-2172, YAZ-2200)
  src/preload/index.ts        installs window.yaseenDocs over contextBridge: `buildBridge(CONTRACT)` plus the hand-written specials; unwraps envelopes into BridgeError rejections (+ surface.test.ts: the byte-identical surface pin; bridge.test.ts: the specials; copy.test.ts)
  src/main/index.ts           bootstrap: app:// protocol serving out/renderer (host `yaseen`) and vault images (host `vault` → `vaultProtocol.ts`, YAZ-1658), single-instance lock, yaseendocs:// claim + open-url/open-file/second-instance routing (E1/E2), the application-menu apply layer (B3), the store (userData/yaseendocs.json), registerIpc(store), the window manager's restoreAll(), store.flush() on quit (+ index.startup.test.ts: startup and quit order with Electron stubbed, YAZ-2172)

Import from shared: `import type { TreeResponse } from '@shared/types'`.

## Supported file capabilities (YAZ-1289, locked)

`shared/fileKind.ts` is the one case-insensitive classifier. The final filename suffix decides the kind; extensionless names, leading-dot-only names such as `.json`, compound names ending in an unknown suffix such as `data.json.gz`, and every unlisted extension are unsupported. Existing dot-entry and `node_modules` exclusions apply before classification.

| Kind | Extensions | Files view / open | Read | Write / create | Semantic index | Markdown link |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `markdown` | `.md`, `.markdown` | Visible; editable Markdown editor | `readFile`, 10 MiB maximum; preserves the existing Node UTF-8 replacement-character and leading-BOM behavior | Yes: create, edit, autosave, frontmatter and Markdown-only feature writes | Yes: the only `IndexRecord` kind and therefore the only content-search, folder-row, properties, views, aliases and backlinks source | Semantic wikilinks; existing missing-note create-on-click behavior applies |
| `text` | `.txt`, `.log`, `.csv`, `.tsv`, `.json`, `.jsonc`, `.jsonl`, `.ndjson`, `.yaml`, `.yml`, `.toml`, `.ini`, `.cfg`, `.conf`, `.xml`, `.env`, `.properties`, `.lock`, `.js`, `.jsx`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.py`, `.rb`, `.go`, `.rs`, `.java`, `.kt`, `.kts`, `.c`, `.h`, `.cc`, `.cpp`, `.hpp`, `.cs`, `.swift`, `.php`, `.sh`, `.bash`, `.zsh`, `.fish`, `.ps1`, `.sql`, `.html`, `.htm`, `.css`, `.scss`, `.sass`, `.less`, `.vue`, `.svelte`, `.graphql`, `.gql`, `.mdx`, `.rst`, `.tex` | Visible; selectable, copyable, read-only monospaced viewer with exact whitespace and two-axis overflow | `readFile`, 10 MiB maximum; fatal UTF-8 decode, UTF-8 BOM preserved, any malformed UTF-8 or NUL rejected passively | Never | Never | Navigation-only, explicit extension required; no embeds, headings/blocks, aliases, create-on-click or semantic record |
| `pdf` | `.pdf` | Visible; Chromium's native PDF viewer in a read-only frame | Dedicated `readPdf` returns `Uint8Array`, 50 MiB maximum; never text, base64 or the asset pipe | Never | Never | Navigation-only, explicit extension required; the same no-semantic/no-create rules as text |
| `image` | `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif`, `.webp`, `.avif`, `.bmp` | Visible with its full filename; centered, read-only static canvas viewer | Dedicated exact-path `readImage` returns `Uint8Array` + allowlisted MIME, 50 MiB maximum; never base64, fuzzy lookup or the asset pipe | Never | Never | Navigation-only, explicit extension required; the same no-semantic/no-create rules as text/PDF |
| unsupported | every other suffix, including `.svg` | Hidden from Files and refused by supported-file deep links | Never through `readFile`, `readPdf` or `readImage` | Never through the note mutation boundaries | Never | None |

Every supported text/PDF/image target can use the normal tab, background-tab, restore, reveal, delete, open-in-new-window and open-in-VS-Code paths. Rename/move and external-rename repair must preserve what the existing bytes actually encode: Markdown and text retain their same-`FileKind` behavior, PDF stays `.pdf`, images keep the same extension case-insensitively except that `.jpg` and `.jpeg` are equivalent spellings. A rename never transcodes, so `.png` → `.jpg` and every other cross-encoding transition fail before disk mutation. Markdown remains the only creatable or writable kind.

Text, PDF and image transport uses one bounded regular-file snapshot: pre-read `fstat`, reject an oversized or non-regular file before allocation, allocate/read at most the initial size plus one byte (never more than the applicable cap plus one), then post-read `fstat`. A size/mtime drift or a byte count inconsistent with the snapshot fails `IO_ERROR` rather than returning mismatched bytes and metadata. Text above 10 MiB and PDF/image above 50 MiB fail `TOO_LARGE`.

PDF rendering uses an `application/pdf` Blob URL over the dedicated bytes. The viewer revokes the owned URL exactly once on replacement, path change and unmount. Electron enables Chromium PDF plugins while retaining `contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, and `nodeIntegration: false`; there is no PDF.js, annotation/editor layer, custom pagination, base64 conversion or asset-pipe reuse.

Raster rendering gives the dedicated bytes and allowlisted MIME to `createImageBitmap` once, sizes a canvas to the decoded dimensions, paints only the default frame, and immediately closes the bitmap exactly once. Animated GIF/WebP therefore display their static default/first frame; no timer or animation loop exists. Same-path external refresh keeps the last good canvas until replacement paints, while a path change hides old pixels immediately and stale decode completions can never paint. Decode/bridge failures remain passive. SVG is deliberately deferred: it is structured XML that can carry script and external references, so support needs an explicit sanitization and no-network policy rather than entry through this raster boundary.

View-only wikilinks are backed by a lightweight catalog flattened from the Files tree, never by `IndexRecord[]`. The catalog refreshes only for supported-file/directory structural watch events, and reads the tree itself only when no other consumer of the window's one tree feed (`lib/treeFeed.ts`: same-turn calls share a request, later ones share one trailing request, every answer reaches every listener — YAZ-2191) has read it since; content changes reload an already-open viewer without rebuilding semantic state. Resolution uses exact root-relative paths or case-insensitive basenames, with the shallowest path and then deterministic path order winning duplicates; autocomplete emits the shortest unambiguous spelling with the real extension. Recognized explicit-extension targets always take this navigation-only branch—even before the catalog is ready or when the target is missing—so they can never fall through to Markdown creation. Missing/removed targets and view-only heading/block suffixes remain unresolved and produce passive notices. `|display text` changes presentation only. View-only files produce no backlinks, properties, aliases, searchable content, folder-view rows, semantic records or writes; rename-reference repair reads/writes only Markdown source notes using the pre-rename view-only catalog.

A root switch synchronously clears the old navigation resolver, catalog targets and merged picker rows while the new tree is pending. The semantic source object keeps its identity, but an old vault's catalog can never compose with the new vault's semantic snapshot.

## Bridge API (`window.yaseenDocs`, locked in GRO-2153 — Desktop A1)

**The table** (YAZ-2131 🔒 D9, YAZ-2200): every door below is one line of `CONTRACT` in `shared/ipc.ts` — channel, argument and answer types, arity. The preload builds `window.yaseenDocs` from it, main registers `handle(CONTRACT.x.y, fn)` (typed from the table), and `client/src/api.ts` wraps it, so a new door is one table line plus its handler, and a missing or mistyped one fails to compile. The specials stay hand-written in the preload (`SPECIAL`): `watch` (multiplexed by subscription id), `window.onFlush` (the `app:flush` / `app:flushed` handshake) and `menu.onCopyAs` / `onPasteAs` (with their `menu:copy-text` / `menu:paste-text-fallback` fallback invokes); `state.setFolds` / `setBaseGroups` send a copy of their key list and `properties.onChange` hands its listener the payload's `properties`, as they always did. `desktop/src/preload/surface.test.ts` pins the whole surface byte for byte; `desktop/src/contract.test.ts` pins that every door is wired exactly once. The renderer reaches main only through `api`, except `clipboardPaste.ts` / `clipboardCopyOut.ts`, which subscribe with optional chaining so they also run outside Electron.

The desktop app has no HTTP server (GRO-2095 D2): the renderer is sandboxed and calls the typed bridge the preload installs; `ipcMain.handle` answers from the main process (GRO-2157 deleted the server and every HTTP path). All paths are absolute POSIX paths; no jail — any absolute path is allowed. Every method rejects with a plain `BridgeError { code, message, path?, mtime? }` (`BridgeErrorCode` in `shared/types.ts`, plus `CONFLICT` which carries the disk mtime); the client's `api.ts` wraps it in `BridgeRequestError` (same fields) so the renderer can `instanceof` it.

| Bridge | Notes |
|---|---|
| `tree(root)` | `TreeResponse` — recursive; every regular file appears (YAZ-1577 D1) carrying its preview `kind` — exact `FileKind` for Markdown/text/PDF/image, `null` for a file with no in-app viewer, which the renderer hands to the OS default app; every dir shows, files or not (GRO-2022); dot-entries, `node_modules` and crash-left atomic-write tmps (`isAtomicTmp`: `<name>.tmp-<12 hex>`, never deleted, YAZ-2179) are skipped; dirs before files, each sorted case-insensitive. Missing / not a dir → `NOT_FOUND` / `NOT_A_DIRECTORY`. ONE walk per root at a time in main (YAZ-2191): a call during a walk joins the single trailing walk queued behind it, so every answer post-dates its call; the sidebar and search read at once on the first structural event after a quiet spell, then coalesce a burst into ONE trailing read 100 ms after its last event (`lib/leadingTrailing.ts`, YAZ-2240); `ready` reads at once |
| `readFile(path)` | `FileResponse` — Markdown or approved view-only text, 10 MiB maximum. Markdown preserves the legacy Node UTF-8 decode behavior; view-only text uses fatal UTF-8 decoding, preserves a UTF-8 BOM, and rejects NUL. PDF, image and unsupported extensions → `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION` |
| `readPdf(path)` | `PdfResponse { path, data: Uint8Array, mtime, size }` — `.pdf` only, 50 MiB maximum, dedicated binary IPC for the native viewer; never base64 and never routed through `readFile`/`readAsset` |
| `readImage(path)` | `ImageResponse { path, data: Uint8Array, mime, mtime, size }` — exact absolute path only; approved raster extensions only; MIME comes from the single extension allowlist; 50 MiB maximum; never fuzzy, base64 or routed through `readFile`/`readAsset` |
| `writeFile({ path, content, expectedMtime? })` | `FileWriteResponse { path, mtime, size }` — `.md`/`.markdown` only; atomic write (`<name>.tmp-<rand>`, fsynced, + `rename`, YAZ-2177); parent dir must exist; if `expectedMtime` is given and the disk mtime differs → rejects `CONFLICT` with the disk `mtime` and nothing is written |
| `createDir(path)` / `createFile(path \| { path, content?, id? })` | `CreateDirResponse { path }` / `CreateFileResponse { path, mtime, size, id? }` — parent must exist (else `NOT_FOUND`); `.md`/`.markdown` only (else `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`; TOMBSTONE: the `.base` branch, which seeded a minimal table view, went with the format in YAZ-844); the object form's `content` (Bible B, GRO-2202) is the file's initial content and lands in the same write — content-at-create for a note born in a folder from its template and seed (YAZ-2290 E3), no create-then-write race. 🔒 EVERY note is BORN with its id (YAZ-2293 D3 — see "Note ids"): this is the one door all creation goes through, so the frontmatter `id` is set here, in that same write — the request's `id` (for a caller that had to know it before the file existed; anything that is not a note id → `BAD_REQUEST`, nothing created) or a freshly minted one — appended after the seed's own keys, and an `id` already in the seed is REPLACED (a template's is never copied). A create with no `content` therefore holds only its id block (`---`, `id: <id>`, `---`) — never zero bytes — and `size` is the created file's real byte length; the response's `id` is the one written. A seed whose frontmatter does not parse is created exactly as given, with no id in the file and none in the response: the id never blocks a creation, and the index gives the note one once the YAML is fixed ("Note ids": The sweep). Durable create (fsynced `<name>.tmp-<rand>` + `link` to claim the name, then the same bytes renamed in over it — YAZ-2380: macOS reports nothing when a file left on a hard-linked inode is later deleted, so the note would stay in the tree and the index; or a fsynced `wx` write where the volume has no hard links, YAZ-2177), never overwrites: exists → `ALREADY_EXISTS`. 🔒 A FOLDER is born with its id too (D13): `createDir` — the one door "New folder", "New dated folder" and `ensureFolder` go through — makes the directory, then durably creates `<folder>/.folder.md` holding only a freshly minted `id` (`---`, `id: <id>`, `---`); a folder whose file could not be written stands without one, and the sweep gives it one. Both handlers adopt the vault ("Note ids": The sweep) |
| `pickFolder()` | `PickFolderResponse` — Electron `dialog.showOpenDialog` (`openDirectory` + `createDirectory`) parented to the calling window (`desktop/src/main/ipc/dialog.ts`). Picked → `{ path }` (no trailing `/`); dismissed → `{ cancelled: true }`; dialog failure → `PICKER_FAILED` |
| `watch(root, listener) → unsubscribe` | `WatchEvent`s: first `ready` (at once for late joiners of an already-watched root), then `add` / `change` / `unlink` (+ `mtime`) for every regular file, viewer or not (YAZ-1577 D1), and `addDir` / `unlinkDir`; dot-entries, `node_modules` and atomic-write tmps (YAZ-2179) are ignored — with ONE exception, a folder's settings file `.folder.md` (YAZ-2290 D8, `fs/watchers.ts` `ignored`), which is reported unless a folder above it is skipped. Renderer consumers filter by kind themselves (the index and rename detection take Markdown only, the view-only catalog takes `isViewOnly` only), so an event for a file with no viewer refreshes the tree and nothing else. Image events refresh Files/view-only navigation and an open viewer but never trigger an index scan. A bad root yields one `error` event and no subscription; an unsubscribe that lands before the root check finishes cancels the subscribe (YAZ-2178). One watcher per root in main shared by every window (`fs/treeWatcher.ts`, YAZ-2192: one recursive `fs.watch`, each path announced once it has been quiet 100 ms, nothing announced for what was there at the start; chokidar polling with the former `awaitWriteFinish: { stabilityThreshold: 200 }` on a network volume or where `fs.watch` cannot serve); a window going away drops its subscriptions |
| `index(root)` | `IndexResponse { root, records, folders, generatedAt }` (GRO-2129; `folders` since YAZ-2290 D8 — every folder's `.folder.md` as an `IndexRecord`, sorted by path and never among `records`, see "Folder settings: `.folder.md`"; TOMBSTONE ⚡ YAZ-815: `types?` — the `.obsidian/types.json` assignments, read per call for cell-editor inference (5B, GRO-2142) — rode here for a typing rung nothing ever fed, and the read went with the field) — every markdown note under `root` as an `IndexRecord` (path/stat fields, the note's `id` (YAZ-2293), frontmatter `properties`, `aliases` (E2, GRO-2214), `tags`/`links`/`embeds` incl. body scan — D7), sorted by path; markdown only — nothing else has ever been a record. Full scan on first call per root (`desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/`), watcher-incremental after, evicted after ~10 min idle. Client: `useIndex(root, watch)` fetches once per root and refetches (300 ms debounce) on markdown `add`/`change`/`unlink`, `unlinkDir` and `ready` events |
| `coldDiff(root)` | `ColdStartDiffResponse { root, scannedAt, cacheStatus, added, removed, changed } \| null` (Links E1c, GRO-2242) — the cold-start reconcile diff the persistent index cache computed for `root` (see "Property index"): `added` = on disk but not in the cache (ON-DISK stats), `removed` = in the cache but gone from disk (CACHED stats), `changed` = stat drifted (re-scanned). Null before the first `index(root)` build for the root and again after idle eviction — the E1c detector reads it AFTER the first index snapshot. Honest miss semantics: trust the three lists only when `cacheStatus === 'hit'` — on `miss`/`corrupt`/`version-mismatch` they are empty and the status says why |
| `readAsset(root, ref)` | `AssetResponse { path, mime, data, size, mtime }` (GRO-2139 amended by D10: a bridge method, never a route; `mtime` added by YAZ-879 — the read is the only honest source of `writeAsset`'s `expectedMtime` guard) — a local image under `root` as base64 + mime (from the extension) for a `data:` URL. `ref` is a wikilink target or path (`\|alias` / `#heading` stripped like the index's link extraction): it is tried root-relative first (any ref, since YAZ-1658 — `resolveAsset`, which the `app://vault` protocol also uses with a note-relative step ahead of it); else/fallback Obsidian's shortest-path rule — case-insensitive basename match anywhere under root, first hit in a breadth-first walk (a shallower match always wins) with each directory's entries sorted case-insensitively, dot-entries and `node_modules` skipped. Images (png/jpg/jpeg/gif/webp/svg/avif/bmp, `IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`) and drawings (`.excalidraw` → `application/json`, `DRAWING_EXTENSIONS`, YAZ-876) only — anything else `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`; no match → `NOT_FOUND`; above 10 MiB → `TOO_LARGE` (`desktop/src/main/fs/assets.ts`). This legacy asset/card/embed pipe may read SVG, but that does not make SVG a supported Files-viewer kind; full-file image viewing uses the separate exact-path `readImage` allowlist above. |
| `writeAsset({ root, path, content, expectedMtime?, create? })` | `AssetWriteResponse { path, mtime, size }` (YAZ-876, first build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852) — the write half of the ASSET pipe: a `.excalidraw` scene-JSON sidecar (UTF-8 STRING body, `DRAWING_EXTENSIONS`) or — since YAZ-1661 (D5, reversing YAZ-876's deliberate narrowing) — an image (`Uint8Array` body, `IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`; the bytes ride Electron's structured clone untouched and a `subarray` writes exactly its own range). The body's KIND picks the allowed extensions: a mismatch is `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`, any other body `BAD_REQUEST`. **Never fuzzy** — `path` is an EXPLICIT vault-relative path (an absolute one under `root` is accepted too); `readAsset`'s basename search has no counterpart here, and a target resolving outside `root` is refused `BAD_REQUEST`. Parent folders are created on the way (`mkdir -p`, so the first drawing or pasted image in a vault makes `assets/drawings/` / `assets/images/` itself — the one deliberate divergence from `writeFile`, whose parent must exist). Otherwise `file.ts`'s semantics exactly: atomic write (`<name>.tmp-<rand>` + `rename`); `expectedMtime` given and the disk mtime differs → `CONFLICT` with the disk `mtime` and nothing written; `create: true` uses `createFile`'s durable create and **never overwrites** — an existing target is `ALREADY_EXISTS`. Content above 10 MiB → `TOO_LARGE`. Deliberately NOT a supported file: `.excalidraw` is absent from the shared classifier, so a drawing has no in-app viewer and stays invisible to the Markdown index — it lists in the tree with `kind: null` like any other file (YAZ-1577 D4) and opens in the OS default app; the handler still needs no store repair and no broadcast, since nothing (tab, recents, index record) can point at one (`desktop/src/main/fs/assets.ts`) |
| `state.get/setSettings/setSidebarCollapsed/setSidebarWidth/pushRecent/removeRecent/setFolder/setFolds/setBaseGroups/onChange` | `AppState` in the main-owned `yaseendocs.json` (GRO-2159, D9; replaces the retired renderer web-storage `mdapp.*` keys); targeted mutators so windows never clobber each other; `removeRecent` drops a dead folder from the MRU (C2, GRO-2164); the lens left this surface for `window.setIdentity` in YAZ-1628; `setFolder`'s patch (`FolderPatch`) is `expanded` / `lastFile` / `name`, each type-checked at the IPC boundary and `name` cleaned main-side by `cleanVaultName` (YAZ-1974; the `favorites` key of YAZ-1766 left this surface for `.yaseendocs/favorites.json` in 6A/D15); `setBaseGroups` replaces one view's collapsed group keys, keyed `<pagePath>::<viewName>` — the page being a FOLDER's directory path since YAZ-2290 (4C, GRO-2137) |
| `window.identity/setIdentity/open/openRecent/duplicate/onFlush` | window identity from `?win=<id>` (GRO-2159, see "App state"); `setIdentity`'s patch carries, beside `root` / `file` / `tabs` / `rightPanel`, the per-window chrome — `sidebarCollapsed` (YAZ-1280), `sidebarLens` (YAZ-1628; `files` or `favorites`, anything else `BAD_REQUEST`) and `focusDirs` / `focusFavorites` (YAZ-1628, YAZ-1766) — each absent = untouched; `tabs` and `file` may name a DIRECTORY, a folder tab (YAZ-2290 D3); `open({ root, file })` = an independent new window (GRO-2160 — the sidebar context menu's "Open in new window", D2 GRO-2168; ⌘-click opens a background tab instead since I3, GRO-2235); `duplicate()` = same folder + file + tabs as the calling window, cascaded bounds (the ⌘⇧N gesture, GRO-2167); `openRecent(path)` → `boolean` (YAZ-1767 🔒 D1, THE open-a-recent-vault door — the sidebar's vault switcher, and App's `openVault` for a vault window's Open Folder… and File › Open Recent (YAZ-1914), all use it): an absolute directory path in; a dead folder is pruned from the MRU and nothing opens (`false`); a live one is bumped to the top of the MRU and then either every live window already on that root is RAISED, least recently focused first so the most recent ends on top, minimized ones restored, nothing new opened (🔒 D9), or, with none open, a new window opens on the vault's `folders[root].lastFile` (D2) — `true` either way; `onFlush(listener)` = the close/quit flush handshake — main holds the close until every listener settled, hard 5 s cap (GRO-2160, see "Multi-window") |
| `menu.onOpenFolder/onOpenRoot/onSwitchVault` | pushes from the application menu to the focused window's renderer (B3, GRO-2161): File › Open Folder… (⌘⇧O) → run the pick-folder flow; File › Open Recent chose `path` → App's `openVault` (YAZ-1914: Welcome switches in place, a vault window hands it to `window.openRecent`); File › Switch Vault… (⌘O, YAZ-1767) → toggle the sidebar header's vault switcher, un-collapsing the sidebar first (see "Menus & shortcuts") |
| `menu.onSettings` | push from Yaseen Docs › Settings… (⌘,) to the focused window's renderer (`menu:settings`, YAZ-1679): open the settings dialog — the same one the sidebar cog opens (see "App state": `settings`) |
| `link.onOpenFile/onNotice` | pushes from deep-link routing (E1, GRO-2171): `onOpenFile(path)` = a link resolved to this window (the path is guaranteed inside its root); `onNotice(message)` = a link could not be opened — show it unobtrusively, never a dialog (see "Links") |
| `file.rename({ oldPath, newPath })` / `file.onRenamed` | In-app file/folder rename or move (Links E1/E1b): validates the target, never overwrites, and for files requires a transition that cannot misrepresent unchanged bytes (same kind for Markdown/text/PDF; same image extension case-insensitively, with `.jpg`↔`.jpeg` allowed). It repairs every stored main/right path plus roots/recents/folder state in one commit, then broadcasts `{ oldPath, newPath, kind }`. Each renderer carries dirty Markdown editor buffers and retires old controllers before `useWorkspace` remaps current paths, retained mounts and histories. The normal watcher unlink+add echo heals the appropriate index/catalog feed; no extra push or watcher exists. |
| `file.repairRename({ oldPath, newPath })` | Store/tab repair for a rename that ALREADY happened on disk (Links E1c, GRO-2242 — the confirmed-banner path, see "Links": Rename → External renames). There is no fs work: the handler validates the claim — `newPath` must EXIST (its stat derives `kind`), `oldPath` must NOT (`BAD_REQUEST` — a live old path means the hypothesis was wrong; refuse, never guess), the same encoding-preserving and dot-dir rules mirror `rename` — then runs the SAME `store.renamePath` repair and pushes the SAME `file:renamed { oldPath, newPath, kind }` to every window, so tabs, viewer continuity and title/hash reuse the E1 downstream unchanged. No vault-root guard (nothing moves; a window rooted at an externally renamed folder is exactly what the repair heals) |
| `file.delete({ path })` / `file.onDeleted` | Sends the file/folder to system Trash only; no permanent fallback. Validation rejects hidden infrastructure and the calling window's vault root. One store repair removes affected main/right paths and related recents/folder state while leaving `window.root` for the existing root-missing probe, then broadcasts `{ path, kind }`. Each renderer retires matching autosave controllers before `useWorkspace` removes ownership, preventing a dirty unmount from recreating the trashed file. Referencing notes remain byte-identical. |
| `file.clip({ paths, op })` / `file.clipState()` / `file.paste({ targetDir })` / `file.onClipChanged` | Cut / Copy / Paste of files and folders (YAZ-1674). ONE app-wide clipboard lives in main (`fileClip.ts`, 🔒 D1): session-only, never persisted, so a paste in ANY window takes what ANY window clipped — within a vault or across two. `clip` replaces it with the ORDERED selection (absolute, de-duplicated) and the verb (`copy` \| `cut`, 🔒 D2); nothing on disk is touched or stat'ed, a stale entry is reported per entry BY the paste. Every change is pushed to every window as `clip:changed` (`{ count, op }` or null — a menu label needs no more), and `clipState` is the catch-up read for a window that mounted after the clip. `paste` lands the clipboard INTO `targetDir` (must exist, never created — `NOT_FOUND` / `NOT_A_DIRECTORY`; empty clipboard → `BAD_REQUEST`), per entry in clipboard order, one bad entry never stopping the rest (`PasteResponse { pasted[], failed[] }`, 🔒 D3): a COPY is `fs.cp` (`fs/copy.ts`: recursive, `errorOnExist`, timestamps kept — folders whole, hidden dirs inside included, 🔒 D4) under Finder's next free name (`Note.md` → `Note copy.md` → `Note copy 2.md`, counting on from an existing ` copy N`, last-extension split, folders keep the whole name) with NO store repair and NO push (nothing moved — the watcher's add echo fills the tree); a CUT is the rename pipeline above, verbatim, per entry (`renameFile` → `store.renamePath` → `file:renamed`), so a clash is `ALREADY_EXISTS`, an entry already in `targetDir` is skipped silently, and `EXDEV` (another volume) fails `IO_ERROR` "cannot move across disks; copy it instead" rather than a copy-then-delete nobody asked for. A cut pastes ONCE (the clipboard clears when ≥1 entry landed; an all-failed cut keeps it); a copy pastes again and again. Guards borrowed from rename/remove where they transfer: a hidden source (`isSkipped`) and a folder into itself are `BAD_REQUEST`. No vault-root guard: Cut/Copy are only ever offered on rows. NOT in v1: the OS/Finder clipboard (Future YAZ-1707) and carrying a note's assets across vaults (Future YAZ-1708) |
| `shell.reveal({ path })` | Reveal in Finder (GRO-2274 — **VS Code parity is the locked reference for this issue**). `shell.showItemInFolder`: the entry is selected **in its parent**, for files, folders and the vault root alike — deliberately NOT `shell.openPath`, which would open a folder's contents; the verb is "Reveal", and VS Code and Obsidian both behave this way. The path is stat'd first so a stale row (deleted or moved externally) rejects `NOT_FOUND` — `showItemInFolder` returns `void` and silently does nothing for a missing path, which reads to the user as a broken menu item. Read-only, so unlike `file.delete` / `file.rename` there is deliberately **no dot-entry guard and no extension guard**: any absolute path the app can already show is fair game, matching the "no jail" posture above. No store repair and no broadcast — nothing changes — but still enveloped so the `NOT_FOUND` reaches the renderer as a passive notice |
| `shell.openVsCode({ path })` | Open in VS Code (YAZ-963). `shell.openExternal('vscode://file/<path>')`, every segment percent-encoded — the OS deep link, **never a spawned process** (no install-name or PATH coupling; the OS picks which VS Code answers). Same read-only posture and the same stat: a stale path rejects `NOT_FOUND` for the passive notice. Offered on every sidebar row, blank space (= the vault root) and the tab's menu. |
| `shell.openLink({ href, sourcePath? })` | Standard Markdown link routing (YAZ-1309). Main is the only trust boundary: `http`/`https`/`mailto`/`tel`/`ftp` are canonicalized and sent to `shell.openExternal`; absolute and note-relative `file:` targets resolve from `sourcePath`, lose URL query/fragment, and go to `shell.openPath`, so the OS default app decides. Fragment-only links stay renderer-local. Empty/malformed requests, non-local file URLs, and every other protocol reject before an OS side effect. Every `window.open` is denied by `setWindowOpenHandler`; allowlisted external popup URLs are rerouted through this same function, so Electron never creates a child browser. |
| `vaultConfig.read/write/onChange` | Vault-local config in `<root>/.yaseendocs/` (Desktop J, GRO-2188) — the `.obsidian/` analogue: travels with the vault. `read(root, name)` → parsed JSON, or `null` for a missing folder/file; malformed JSON rejects `INVALID_CONFIG`, so a caller can tell the two apart and never writes over a file it could not read; reading NEVER creates the folder. `write(root, name, value)` `mkdir -p`s the dotfolder lazily and writes pretty-printed JSON atomically (tmp + rename); `name` must be a plain `<stem>.json` basename (separators / `..` → `BAD_REQUEST`, other extensions → `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`). The shared vault watcher never sees dot-entries, so main runs one tiny watch per open-vault root scoped to the dotfolder (`desktop/src/main/vaultConfig.ts`, subscribed per `AppState.windows` root, dropped with the last window); own writes notify synchronously and their watcher echo is deduped by mtime, external edits (sync tools) are debounced — either way every window gets `vaultConfig:changed { root, name }` and filters by its own root. `.yaseendocs` never appears in the tree/sidebar, the vault index, or a folder's views |
| `favorites.get/set/onChanged` | The Favorites tab's list in `<root>/.yaseendocs/favorites.json` (YAZ-1766 6A, 🔒 D11–D15) — `{ version: 1, favorites: string[] }` of VAULT-RELATIVE POSIX paths in the user's order (append on add, rewritten by drag-to-reorder), so the list travels with the vault and GitHub sync carries it between machines. The API speaks ABSOLUTE paths: `get(root)` → `string[]` (absent or malformed → `[]`, never creates anything; unsafe entries — empty, leading `/`, a `..` segment, NUL — and duplicates are dropped on read, capped at `MAX_FAVORITES`); `set(root, paths)` replaces the list — every path must be `root` or under it (→ `BAD_REQUEST`), the dotfolder is created on the first write, and DEAD ENTRIES are dropped on the way (D14: a path that no longer exists on disk leaves the list; the renderer never prunes). Corrupt policy (D12, properties' R2.5): unparsable JSON or a wrong shape/version reads as `[]` and every `set` rejects `INVALID_CONFIG` — the file is never overwritten or moved aside. Repair (D13): the fs handlers in `desktop/src/main/ipc/fs.ts` (rename, delete, repair-rename, paste-as-cut) call `favorites.renamePath` / `removePath` right after `store.renamePath` / `removePath`, on the LONGEST open root (`AppState.windows`) that owns the path — a renamed folder carries its descendants, a path moved OUT of the vault is dropped, nothing is written when nothing changed, and a repair failure is `console.warn`ed without failing the file op or skipping its broadcast. Writes and repairs are serialised per root (properties' chain). Main subscribes one name-filtered `subscribeConfig` per open root and broadcasts `favorites:changed { root }` to every window on an own write or an external edit (sync); renderers filter by root and re-read. Module `desktop/src/main/favorites.ts` (Electron-free), IPC `desktop/src/main/ipc/favorites.ts` |
| `properties.get/setProperty/removeProperty/onChange` | Vault-wide property declarations in `<root>/.yaseendocs/properties.json` (YAZ-835, succeeding the Bible A registry of GRO-2201) — the ONE surviving registry concept: what editor a frontmatter key gets and what a link column points at, for the whole vault. Read/written through vaultConfig (`desktop/src/main/properties/`, Electron-free like the index). `get(root)` → `PropertiesResponse { root, version, properties, error? }` — see "Properties" under "View modules" for the full rules (version semantics, lazy creation, validation grammar, the `types.json` clean break). Any change — in-app mutation or external edit (the vaultConfig dotfolder watcher) — broadcasts `properties:changed { root, properties }` to every window; client: `useProperties(root)` (`client/src/views/`) → `{ status, properties, error }`, live-replaced on each broadcast (a corrupt properties.json is `status: 'ready'` with `properties.error` set, not a fetch error) |
| `github.status/syncNow/setEnabled/onStatus` | Per-vault GitHub sync (YAZ-1081) — plain `git` against the vault's own repo, no GitHub API and no token of ours. **OFF BY DEFAULT and per vault**: the switch is `<root>/.yaseendocs/github.json`, read as `{ enabled: true }` EXACTLY (absent, malformed or `"true"` all mean off), so it travels with the folder and a corrupt file fails closed rather than starting background git work nobody asked for; a disabled root is completely silent — no watcher, no timers, no passes. The repo is AUTO-DETECTED, never configured here: a root with no `.git` at its own level, or no `origin`, is `off` (not an error), and `status` on an off root still reports read-only `repo { remoteUrl, branch }` for a settings panel. Every method answers the same `GithubSyncStatus { root, state, attention?, message?, repo? }` the push carries — `off` / `synced` / `pending` / `syncing` / `attention` (`no-git` / `no-identity` / `auth` / `conflict` / `error`) — so a mutation never needs a follow-up read; `status` answers from the last broadcast without touching git. Passes are EVENT-DRIVEN (`desktop/src/main/git/manager.ts`): opening or enabling a vault pulls, edits mark it `pending` at once then commit after a 30s quiet period, an offline pass arms ONE 2-min retry, and window focus / OS wake (`powerMonitor` `resume` + `unlock-screen`, wired in `main/index.ts`) pull behind a 10s cooldown. One pass at a time per root, a burst collapsing into exactly one follow-up. A pass is commit → fetch → rebase → push (the commit never stages an atomic-write tmp, YAZ-2179), and the **lossless rule** is absolute: nothing is ever stashed, and a rebase that conflicts is `--abort`ed — the working tree comes back byte-for-byte and the vault reports `attention/conflict` for a human. On quit the renderers flush FIRST, then `flushForQuit` runs the final pass, so the last edit is in the last commit. `github:status-changed` goes to EVERY window on every transition; renderers filter by `status.root` |

Bases (GRO-2097) adds its methods to `CONTRACT` additively (`index(root)` and `readAsset(root, ref)` above), never routes; the drawing embed (YAZ-852) extends the same pipe the same way with `writeAsset(req)`.

Notes
- Folder picking (client, `usePickFolder`): the vault switcher's last row "Open folder…" (the sidebar header, YAZ-1767 D4) and the Welcome screen's Open folder… call `pickFolder()`; `{ path }` → set root + push to recents, `{ cancelled }` or a rejection → nothing. No dialog ever opens by itself — a window with no folder shows the Welcome screen (C2, GRO-2164). One dialog in flight at a time; the trigger buttons are disabled meanwhile. There is no in-app folder browser.
- Main-process writes trigger `change` events on the watcher; the client must ignore events for a path whose mtime equals the mtime it just received from its own `writeFile` (echo suppression).
- Auto-save: client debounces 500 ms after last `markdownUpdated`, also flushes on file switch / the close flush handshake (`window.onFlush` — GRO-2158/2160 — is what guarantees the last save; `beforeunload` is best effort). Only content that differs from the last loaded/saved markdown is saved (Crepe's first serialisation is a normalised rewrite and is never written on its own). Frontmatter-only external changes absorb silently (GRO-2186): an incoming `change` whose BODY matches the last loaded/saved body just replaces the autosave baseline — no reload, no conflict bar — so unsaved body edits and the caret survive a cell edit landing on that note's frontmatter from a folder's view (see "Cell editing"). The same rule covers a save that loses that race (YAZ-2175): a `CONFLICT` whose re-read shows the body unchanged adopts the new frontmatter and retries ONCE, so the app's own property/comment write never raises the bar; a body that changed still does.
- Desktop tests write right after `ready`: on macOS libuv starts the FSEvents stream asynchronously, so the watcher engine announces `ready` only once a probe watch has heard the stream (YAZ-2192), and walks the tree again then, so nothing that landed in between is missed.

## App state (`yaseendocs.json`, main-owned — GRO-2159, D9)

One user-global JSON file owned by the main process: `app.getPath('userData')/yaseendocs.json` = `~/Library/Application Support/Yaseen Docs/yaseendocs.json` (`app.setName('Yaseen Docs')` runs before anything reads userData; `--user-data-dir=<dir>` relocates it, which is how the Playwright checks seed state). Renderer web storage is not used at all. The renderer never touches the file: it reads a cache (`storage.ts` in client lib, filled by `storage.init()` before the first render) and writes through the targeted `state.*` mutators; `desktop/src/main/store.ts` owns the file. Nothing here is ever written into the vault or the markdown.

| Key | Shape | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| `version` | `1` | anything else → the file is treated as corrupt (see below) |
| `settings` | `SettingsState` `{ lineSpacing, blockGap, bulletThreading, threadWidth, threadColor, theme, contentWidth, newNoteLocation, newNoteFolder, confirmDelete, commentsOrder }` | **Edited in the settings dialog** (YAZ-1679, `client/src/settings/SettingsDialog.tsx`): an Obsidian-style centred modal — ONE scrolling page of every settings section (Appearance · Editor · Files & Links · Sync) with grouped rows on the right, the section titles down the left as nav anchors (click = scroll to the section; the active one follows the scroll) under a search box, and below a nav divider the `standalone` sections that are their own page inside the dialog (Hotkeys — a reference, not settings) — opened by the sidebar-footer cog, `⌘,` or Yaseen Docs › Settings… (`menu:settings`), all three the ONE dialog App mounts. Every page, row and search hit reads from the one registry, `client/src/settings/registry.tsx` (`SETTINGS_SECTIONS`): a setting is declared once — id, label, hint, keywords, render — inside a section's GROUP (a titled sub-heading, optionally hinted, or plain rows), and each row carries `data-setting="<id>"` (the `SettingsState` field name for the eleven fields here, `githubSync` for the per-vault switch), which is how tests and e2e specs address a row. Search (`searchSettings.ts`) ranks with the `[[` picker's `matchLinkCandidates` over label + hint + keywords + group title + section title, and shows hits under a "Section › Group" breadcrumb. "Settings cog" below means this dialog. Global: one value for every folder and window; validated field by field on load (`threadWidth` ∈ 1/2/3, `threadColor` `#rrggbb` or null, `theme` ∈ system/light/dark — default `system`, so a pre-K file just gains it; `contentWidth` ∈ narrow/medium/full — default `narrow`, so older files and junk safely preserve the existing 1040px behavior; `newNoteLocation` ∈ root/current/folder — default `current` (YAZ-1643) — and `newNoteFolder` a root-relative path per `isValidNewNoteFolder` (`''` = the vault root; no leading/trailing `/`, and every segment must be one create-on-click would accept — so no empty segment, nothing starting with `.` (which subsumes `.`/`..`), no NUL: GRO-2197, because the panel used to accept `.archive` and then EVERY unresolved-link click failed on the per-segment `validateEntryName`; `shared/types.ts` holds the rule and `sidebar/createEntry.ts` is the sibling that must stay in step) — defaults, so a pre-C2 file just gains them; `confirmDelete` a boolean defaulting **true**; `commentsOrder` ∈ oldest/newest — default `oldest`, so a pre-1515 file just gains it; it is the settings-cog **Comments** control, `Oldest first` / `Newest first`, above the Files & Links section, and the comment block's own header toggle writes the same field — YAZ-1515), an invalid field falls back to `DEFAULT_SETTINGS`; `setSettings` takes a complete valid object (else `BAD_REQUEST`). `contentWidth` (YAZ-1176) is the settings-cog Narrow/Medium/Full control: App stamps it as `data-content-width` on `.app`, where one `--content-max-width` maps it to 1040px/1440px/fluid; page header, editor, a folder's views, comments, and backlinks consume that one token and another window's change lands live through the existing `storage.subscribe` path. `newNoteLocation`/`newNoteFolder` (Files & Links, Links C2- — GRO-2240) are the settings-cog "Default location for new notes" control (Obsidian's three options; the folder input shows only for `folder` and validates on commit — invalid keeps the stored value) driving where a bare unresolved `[[link]]` creates its page (rule 23); the setting is GLOBAL but `newNoteFolder` is interpreted per-vault against each window's root. `confirmDelete` (GRO-2272 — VS Code's `explorer.confirmDelete`) gates the delete confirm sheet: it defaults ON and should stay that way, because the sheet is the ONLY guard delete has — `shell.trashItem` offers no programmatic un-trash, so there is no in-app restore behind it. Cleared by the sheet's own "Don't ask me again" and re-enabled from the settings cog (both halves are required: a one-way switch would leave hand-editing `yaseendocs.json` as the only way back). `theme` (Appearance, Desktop K — GRO-2218) is the settings-cog System/Light/Dark control: main mirrors it into `nativeTheme.themeSource` before the first window exists and on every change (`desktop/src/main/theme.ts`; new windows also get a matching `backgroundColor`, so no white flash on dark launches), and each renderer resolves it (`system` follows `prefers-color-scheme` live) to `data-theme="light"\|"dark"` on `<html>` — app.css's `[data-theme='dark']` token block — while swapping Crepe's frame/frame-dark var blocks in one managed `<style>` (`client/src/editor/crepeTheme.ts`, CSS-only: the open editor never remounts) |
| `sidebarCollapsed` (legacy top-level) | `boolean` | retired by YAZ-1280. On load it seeds only window entries that lack their own boolean; a per-window boolean wins; missing/junk → `false`. The sanitized snapshot and next persisted write omit this top-level key. |
| `sidebarWidth` | `number` | global; clamped to [180, 520] on load and on write; non-finite → 260 (YAZ-738) |
| `recents` | `RecentRoots` `[{ path, lastOpened }]` | most-recent first, de-duplicated by path, max 10 (`pushRecent(path)`, absolute path); wrong shape → `[]` |
| `windows` | `WindowEntry[]` `{ id, root, file, tabs, sidebarCollapsed, sidebarLens, focusDirs, focusFavorites, bounds: { x, y, width, height } }` | one entry per open window, reopened on relaunch with bounds clamped to a live display (see "Multi-window"); malformed or duplicate-id entries dropped on load; `upsertWindow` replaces by id or appends; an entry is removed when its window closes — unless the app is quitting (⌘Q, or the last window closing, which quits), so relaunch restores the same windows. `tabs` (GRO-2232) = absolute paths left→right, with `file` doubling as the ACTIVE tab; a path may be a file or — since YAZ-2290 D3 — a DIRECTORY, a folder tab ("Folders are the pages"), and the store does not tell them apart; `normalizeTabs` repairs both on load and every `setIdentity`. `sidebarCollapsed` (YAZ-1280) is an independent boolean per window: new/first/deep-link windows start open, a duplicate inherits then diverges, and legacy entries migrate from the retired top-level value as described above. `sidebarLens` (YAZ-847, per window since YAZ-1628) is `'files' \| 'favorites'` (Favorites since YAZ-1766) on the same rule: new/first/deep-link windows start on `DEFAULT_SIDEBAR_LENS` (Files since YAZ-1846), a duplicate inherits then diverges, a root CHANGE lands it on Files in `setRoot`'s one write (🔒 D2, YAZ-1846 — reversing the old "root change keeps it"; re-setting the same root still keeps it), `window.setIdentity` refuses anything but the two lenses (`BAD_REQUEST`), and legacy entries migrate exactly as `sidebarCollapsed` does — a v1 file's retired global `sidebarLens` seeds every window without a valid lens of its own, junk or missing (a PRE-847 file) reading as the default (`files`), and the next write drops the old key. `focusDirs` / `focusFavorites` (YAZ-1605, per window since YAZ-1628; the second since YAZ-1766) are Focus Mode's two path lists — the Files tree's focused DIRS and the Favorites tab's focused favorited DIRS, empty meaning no focus, uncapped — independent per window exactly as `sidebarCollapsed` is: new/first/deep-link windows start unfocused, a duplicate inherits both lists by value then diverges, a root change clears them (`setRoot`), and `renamePath` / `removePath` repair them as they repair `tabs`; on load they read with `tabs`' rule (relative or junk elements drop, a missing or junk list is no focus), and a pre-1628 file's per-vault `folders[root].focusDirs` is simply not read (no migration — the bucket could not say which window was focused; the next write drops it). |
| `folders[root]` | `FolderState` `{ expanded: string[], lastFile: string \| null, folds: { [file]: string[] }, baseGroups: { [pagePath::viewName]: string[] }, name: string \| null }` | per folder; `name` (YAZ-1974 D3) is the vault's DISPLAY NAME — `null` = its folder name; trimmed and capped at `MAX_VAULT_NAME` (80) code points by `cleanVaultName` on load and on every patch; a name equal to the folder's basename is stored as `null` (`storage.setVaultName`); persisted, per machine; it survives Remove from recent vaults (recents never prune `folders`) and follows the vault through `renamePath` (a Finder rename outside the app drops it, like recents); `setFolder(root, { expanded?, lastFile?, name? })` merges and creates the entry with defaults when missing (other keys ignored); `expanded` is a SESSION list since YAZ-1642 — held in the in-memory store and shared by every window on the vault, stripped from the file on every write (`toDisk`) and read as `[]` on load, so every launch starts the tree collapsed (the Sidebar likewise leaves the ancestors of the file it MOUNTS with closed; a file opened later still reveals) — while `lastFile`, `folds` and `baseGroups` persist (the Favorites list is NOT here since YAZ-1766 6A/D15 — it lives in the vault as `.yaseendocs/favorites.json`, see the `favorites.*` bridge row); `setFolds(root, file, keys)` replaces one file's collapsed outline fold keys (max 500; an empty list removes the file entry, the folder entry stays); `setBaseGroups(root, key, collapsed)` does the same for one view's collapsed group keys (max 200, 4C — GRO-2137). The `baseGroups` KEY is `<pagePath>::<viewName>` and since YAZ-2290 that path is a FOLDER's directory — the folder tab's own identity — and it stays session chrome that is never written into the folder's `.folder.md` (the name kept its `base` spelling because the store's rename/delete repair, `remapBaseGroupKey` / `baseGroupGone`, is pinned to it by tests); invalid lists dropped on load |

Write semantics: every mutation updates memory, notifies `onChange` listeners synchronously and schedules one debounced (150 ms) atomic write (`<file>.tmp-<rand>` + rename, pretty-printed JSON, parent dir created on demand), skipped when its text equals the last write's — a folder expand / collapse is session-only and changes no byte (YAZ-2198); `flush()` writes at once and `before-quit` holds the quit until it lands (`event.preventDefault()` + `app.exit(0)`), so nothing is lost on ⌘Q. A file that is unparsable, not an object or not `version: 1` is moved to `yaseendocs.json.corrupt-<epoch>` and the defaults are used — the bad file is never overwritten silently. Unknown top-level keys are dropped on load.

Live broadcast: after any change — from any window, or from main itself (window upserts) — every live `BrowserWindow` gets `state:changed` with the whole new state; the preload surfaces it as `state.onChange`, the client cache replaces itself and `storage.subscribe` listeners fire (`App` re-reads global settings and sidebar width). Per-window `sidebarCollapsed` — and `sidebarLens` since YAZ-1628 — is read from the boot identity and written optimistically through `window.setIdentity`, so another window's state echo cannot change it.

Per-window identity: main loads each window as `<renderer>?win=<id>` and keeps a `webContents.id → window id` lookup (`desktop/src/main/windows.ts`). `window.identity()` answers `{ id, root, file, tabs, sidebarCollapsed }` from `windows[]` for the calling window (`BAD_REQUEST` for an unregistered sender, `NOT_FOUND` once the entry is gone); `window.setIdentity({ root?, file?, tabs?, sidebarCollapsed? })` validates and merges into that entry (`id` and `bounds` cannot be changed this way). On the client, `storage.getRoot()` and `storage.getSidebarCollapsed()` are identity reads; root/file/tab helpers and the sidebar toggle write back through that same targeted identity path.

## Menus & shortcuts (B3 — GRO-2161)

The menu bar is a pure template (`desktop/src/main/menu.ts`, `buildMenuTemplate(inputs, handlers)`) so structure, accelerators and roles unit-test without Electron; `main/index.ts` applies it (`Menu.buildFromTemplate` + `setApplicationMenu`) and rebuilds it ONLY when `AppState.recents` actually changed (`subscribeMenuRebuild` compares by reference — store snapshots reuse untouched sub-objects). Item ids are stable (`menu.file.new-window`, `menu.file.open-recent.<i>`, …) so a live check (Playwright) can drive them via `Menu.getApplicationMenu().getMenuItemById(...)`. `buildContextMenuTemplate(params, actions)` is the same idiom for the right-click menu (spell suggestions → Add to Dictionary → cut/copy/paste per `editFlags`; on an `<img>` — `params.mediaType === 'image'`, which Chromium sets only for elements laid out as images, never inline `<svg>`, so sidebar icons keep the text rows — exactly two rows instead: Copy Image through the native `webContents.copyImageAt`, and Reveal in Finder through `revealVaultImage` → `revealItem`, a silent no-op for a non-vault src such as a card cover, YAZ-1666); every window's factory wires it in `main/index.ts` (YAZ-672).

"The focused window" in the table below means `pickMenuTargetWindow` (`menu.ts`, GRO-2197), not a bare `BrowserWindow.getFocusedWindow()`: the focused window when there IS one, else the window focused most recently, else any live window. macOS reports NO focused window whenever the app is not the frontmost application — a detached DevTools window, a native panel, or a programmatic `menuItem.click()` from the e2e harness — and every one of these items used to become a silent no-op in that state (measured: `tabs.spec.ts`'s ⌃Tab step failed 4 runs in 5 under CPU load because the `menu:next-tab` message was never sent at all).
