A menu item that quietly does nothing is the worst available answer, so the fallback IS the contract. `main/index.ts` tracks the last-focused id from `app.on('browser-window-focus')`; the picker itself is Electron-free and unit-tested.

| Item | Keys | Does |
|---|---|---|
| Yaseen Docs › Settings… | `⌘,` | sends `menu.onSettings` to the focused window's renderer, which opens the settings dialog (YAZ-1679; the renderer owns the dialog, so the gesture never touches it from main); no focused renderer → no-op |
| File › New Window | `⌘⇧N` | duplicates the FOCUSED window — same folder, same file, cascaded bounds (`window.duplicate` semantics, GRO-2167); no focused window → no-op |
| File › Open Folder… | `⌘⇧O` | sends `menu.onOpenFolder` to the focused window's renderer, which runs its pick-folder flow; the picked folder goes through App's `openVault` (YAZ-1914 🔒 D1): on Welcome the window becomes that vault in place, in a vault window it goes to main's `openRecentBeside` door — the vault's windows raised or a NEW window on its last file. A window that already shows a vault never changes vault |
| File › Switch Vault… | `⌘O` | sends `menu.onSwitchVault` to the focused window's renderer, which TOGGLES the sidebar header's vault switcher — un-collapsing the sidebar first when hidden; a no-op on the Welcome screen (YAZ-1767 D8; see "Multi-window": Open beside) |
| File › Open Recent › `<path>` | — | every click (⌥ no longer differs — YAZ-1914 🔒 D2) sends `menu.onOpenRoot(path)` to the focused renderer, which applies App's `openVault`: on Welcome it switches in place (MRU bumped by the renderer); in a vault window it goes to main's `openRecentBeside` door (YAZ-1767 D1): the directory is probed first (a dead folder is pruned from the MRU and opens nothing, GRO-2211), the MRU is bumped, and the vault's open windows are raised or a new window opens on its last file. Empty MRU → one disabled "No Recent Folders" item |
| File › Search Vault | `⌘K` | sends `menu.onSearch` to the focused window's renderer, which focuses its sidebar search bar — un-collapsing the sidebar first when it is hidden (YAZ-804; the renderer owns the bar, so the gesture never touches it from main). The bar is ROW 2 of the chrome and lives on BOTH lenses since YAZ-847, so ⌘K behaves identically whichever tab is showing and never switches the lens; the results then replace that ACTIVE tab's body (🔒 D5 — see "The sidebar lenses") |
| File › Close Tab | `⌘W` | sends `menu.onCloseTab` to the focused window's renderer — the ⌘W ladder (see "Multi-window": Tabs): close the active tab; with zero tabs open (Welcome included) close the WINDOW through the real close path (`window.closeSelf`) |
| File › Close Window | `⌘⇧W` | role `close` — goes through the flush handshake like any close (see "Multi-window") |
| Window › Next / Previous Tab | `⌃Tab` / `⌃⇧Tab` | sends `menu.onNextTab` / `menu.onPrevTab` to the focused window's renderer — wraparound cycle over the strip's left→right order; hidden menu duplicates carry the `⌘⇧]` / `⌘⇧[` equivalents (`acceleratorWorksWhenHidden`) |
| View › Toggle Sidebar | — | sends `menu.onToggleSidebar` to the focused window's renderer, which runs the same local toggle as every other sidebar gesture; no focused renderer → no-op |
| App chrome | `⌘B` | renderer-owned sidebar toggle (YAZ-1280): only plain Cmd+B outside input/textarea/select/contenteditable and while no aria-modal tool is open. Milkdown keeps Cmd+B for bold; Yaseendraw/modal tools keep their own gesture. There is deliberately no Electron accelerator or global shortcut. |
| App chrome | `⌘X` / `⌘C` / `⌘V` | renderer-owned file clipboard (YAZ-1674, D6): the same `ownsWindowChord` boundary as ⌘B / ⌘⇧C (a field, the editor or a modal keeps the key — text copy/paste is untouched); App asks the mounted Sidebar's `clipboardRef` handle and swallows the key ONLY when it acted (Cut/Copy need a selection ≥1, Paste a non-empty clipboard; an open context menu owns the verbs). Hand-confirmed on macOS in the demo that the renderer wins over the Edit-menu role. |
| View › Actual Size / Zoom In / Zoom Out | `⌘0` / `⌘+` (also `⌘=`) / `⌘−` | NOT the stock roles (YAZ-1710): each sends `menu.onZoom(step)` (−1 / 0 / +1) to the focused window's renderer, which routes it — `requestZoom` (`editor/zoomRequest.ts`) bubbles a cancelable `yaseendocs:zoom` event from the focused element; the note whose `section.editor` contains the focus claims it and steps its own `documentZoom` (`stepZoomByKey`: the pill ladder to 200, then 25 at a time to 400; ⌘0 → 100%; a wall is a no-op); unclaimed (blank sidebar, a dialog, the search box, Welcome) → `window.zoom(step)`, and main sets the sender's zoom level to `0` or `± 0.5`, exactly what the roles did, persistence included |
| View › Reload, Edit roles, Window roles | — | stock Electron roles; dev builds add View › Toggle Developer Tools; the Window submenu has `role: 'window'` so macOS appends the window list |
| Help › Yaseen Docs on GitHub | — | `shell.openExternal(HELP_URL)` |

## Multi-window (D3/D6 — GRO-2160/2167/2168/2169)

The window manager (`desktop/src/main/windows.ts`) is Electron-free — `main/index.ts` injects window construction and display geometry as a `WindowHost`, so everything below runs under vitest with fakes.

- **Restore** (D3): on launch, one window per `AppState.windows` entry; an empty state seeds a single Welcome window (`root: null`). Restored bounds are clamped fully inside the display work area they overlap most (fully off-screen → the nearest one; oversized → shrunk to fit) — so a window from an unplugged display always lands somewhere visible. Bounds changes (`move`/`resize`) are committed to the state debounced (250 ms per burst — one `state:changed` per drag).
- **Duplicate** (GRO-2167): `⌘⇧N` / `window.duplicate()` — same folder, same file, same TABS (the copy carries the whole set), fresh id, bounds cascaded +24 px and clamped at the display edge. A Welcome window duplicates to another Welcome window.
- **Open beside** (D2, GRO-2168): a file row's context-menu "Open in new window" → `window.open({ root, file })` — an independent window on the same folder with that file open. Deep-link routing uses the same `openWindow` path (default bounds, clamped). **The vault switcher** (YAZ-1767, `sidebar/VaultSwitcher.tsx`): the sidebar header's top-left button — bold vault name + the Octicon triangle chevron (D6) — drops, on click or ⌘O, a `ContextMenuSurface` panel hung off the header (`ctx-menu--panel`, D5) — at least the header's width (`minWidth`) and growing to fit its longest name up to 480px, past which a name wraps, so no name is ever cut (YAZ-1974 D7): the filter input first and autofocused (D7), every `AppState.recents` vault as ONE line — display name, relative time (YAZ-1974 D2) — the current vault `aria-current` (D3), "Open folder…" last (the folder picker, D4 — it opens beside since YAZ-1914). The path is one hover away (YAZ-1974 D2): an ⓘ between name and time shows while its row is hovered (visibility, so nothing shifts; a dead row too) and hovering it draws the WHOLE path — wrapped at each `/`, never truncated — in a `role="tooltip"` portalled to `<body>` with `position: fixed` at the row (below it, or above when there is more room there), so the panel's scroll box never clips it; mouse-only by design; clicking the ⓘ is the row's click. **Display names** (YAZ-1974 D3–D6): `storage.vaultName(root)` (`folders[root].name ?? basename(root)`, live through `useVaultName`) is what the trigger, the rows, the OS window title (`windowTitle(vault, file)` takes the NAME), Welcome's recents and "Copy vault name" show; File › Open Recent keeps full paths and the Reveal / VS Code "no longer there" notices keep the FOLDER name on purpose. Ranking is `matchLinkCandidates` over each row's display name AND folder name (one candidate per name, the `[[` picker's alias idea — each row once, at its best rank); the highlight starts on the first NON-current row so ⌘O ⏎ jumps to the last-used other vault; ↑/↓ clamp, hover moves it, ⏎ activates, **⇧⏎ / ⇧-click open the vault IN this window** — the menu's "Open in this window" (YAZ-1974 D8; a plain ⏎ on the current vault) — and while ⇧ is held the highlighted row's time slot reads "Open here" in the accent colour (D9; never on the current vault, a dead row or Open folder…); Esc closes on the first press, a second ⌘O closes. Every activation goes through `window.openRecent(path)` — raise the vault's open windows (🔒 D9) or open a new one on its last file (D2); `false` (dead folder, MRU pruned) disables the row with "Folder not found" and keeps the panel open. App's `openVault` is the same door for a vault window's Open Folder… and File › Open Recent (YAZ-1914). **The vault menu** (YAZ-1798): right-clicking the trigger (= the current vault) or any live row opens the sidebar's own `ContextMenu` with `buildVaultMenuSections({ path, name, isCurrent, renamed })` (`sidebar/vaultMenuSections.ts`, pure) — another vault gets [Open in this window `⇧⏎`] · [Set display name · Reset to folder name] · [Copy vault name · Copy path] · [Reveal in Finder · Open in VS Code] · [Remove from recent vaults]; the current vault the middle three groups; "Reset to folder name" only while a display name is set (YAZ-1974 D5 — never labelled "Rename", which renames on disk in the file menu). "Set display name" turns the name into an inline `TextField` WHERE it was right-clicked — the header or the row, a `<div>` in place of the `<button>` meanwhile — the name selected, the folder name as placeholder: ⏎ or blur saves, Esc cancels, an empty field is the folder name again; the field is the top layer: Esc and click-away end only it (the panel stays), and a mousedown on another row ends it (the blur saves) and swallows that click, so a vault never opens by accident. "Open in this window" is App's `openRoot` (in place — besides Welcome, the ONE deliberate in-place switch since YAZ-1914 made every other open go beside, D8/D11; it lands on Files like any vault switch, YAZ-1846; `false` greys the row like a click's); Reveal / VS Code are the Sidebar's own verbs (any absolute path, `NOT_FOUND` notice); the copies confirm through the sidebar notice; Remove is `storage.removeRecentRoot` with no confirm (the folder is untouched) and drops the row at once. The menu is the top layer while it stands: Esc and click-away close only it, and the filter ignores ↑/↓/⏎/Esc meanwhile. Every right-click swallows Electron's native text menu; a "Folder not found" row gets no menu. ⌘-click on a sidebar file row is NOT this gesture any more: since I3 (GRO-2235, locked) it opens a background tab in the SAME window — see Tabs below.
- **Tabs** (GRO-2232/2234/2235): `WindowEntry.tabs` is the ordered main-pane set and `file` is its active path. A tab is a FILE or — since YAZ-2290 D3, overturning 🔒 D3 of YAZ-1578 — a FOLDER, whose path is the directory's ("Folders are the pages"); its label is the folder's whole name (`lib/pageLabel.ts`), and its menu offers Copy path and Copy for Agent like a note's. `normalizeTabs` repairs legacy/malformed identity; `tabs: []` iff `file: null`, the active path belongs to the set, and paths are unique. The renderer model now lives inside `client/src/workspace/useWorkspace.ts` with the right panel; it mirrors one complete workspace identity per logical change, while `folders[root].lastFile` changes only when the active main file changes. Sidebar/deep-link opens replace the current tab, ⌘-click appends in background, activation lazy-mounts one retained editor layer, drag reorders, middle-click/✕ closes, and ⌘W follows right-neighbour then left-neighbour then empty-window then close-window. Per-tab Back/Forward history remains renderer-session-only. While the sidebar is collapsed the strip's nav row carries a THIRD button FIRST, **Show sidebar** (`aria-label="Show sidebar"`, the same `.tabbar-nav__btn` dress as ◀ ▶), rendered by TabBar from App's `onShowSidebar` (absent while the sidebar is open) — it replaced the floating `.sidebar-reopen` button that used to sit over Back (YAZ-1759), so nothing overlaps ◀. **The strip** (`client/src/tabs/`, `.tabbar`): tabs shrink to a ~120 px floor first and then the strip SCROLLS, Obsidian-like, with the ACTIVE tab `scrollIntoView`n on every activation. Since YAZ-1471 neither the scrolling nor the reordering arithmetic is spelled here alone. The scroll is the shared `.scroll-strip` utility in `app.css`, worn beside `.tabbar` and by a folder's view tabs too ("View chrome": Toolbar): `overflow-x: auto` with NO scrollbar (`scrollbar-width: none` is the whole hiding rule on Chromium ≥ 121, so the old `::-webkit-scrollbar` rules are gone), plus a `@keyframes strip-fade` mask driven by `animation-timeline: scroll(self inline)` — a scroll-driven fade marking whichever edge still hides tabs, and a strip that does not overflow has no timeline and so no mask at all. The insertion-slot idiom this strip invented is now `client/src/lib/dragSlot.ts` — `insertionSlot(e, i)` (the target's own horizontal midpoint) and `dropIndex(from, slot)` (the grabbed item leaves the list before it re-enters, so a slot past the grab point shifts one left) — ONE spelling for both horizontal strips, so the window's tabs and a folder's view tabs cannot drift apart; the validated right→main insertion slots above run on it, and so does the view tabs' own reorder.
- **Right panel** (YAZ-966): `client/src/workspace/useWorkspace.ts` is the renderer's ONE owner for main tabs, right headers, both session histories, retained mounts, and lifecycle repair. The durable per-window identity is `{ open, width, items, expanded }` beside `{ tabs, file }`; legacy entries gain the default, width is clamped to 320–720 px (440 default), and normalization enforces absolute unique paths plus global-in-window exclusive ownership. Every logical mutation mirrors ONE complete `{ tabs, file, rightPanel }` identity. The panel reuses the full `Editor` and the window's existing watch/index/properties/sync feeds; one right header is expanded at most, visited expanded headers retain hidden editor layers, and plain/⌘ link navigation plus Back/Forward stay right-local. Ownership transfers call `carryEditorAcrossPane` before the source unmounts or the identity mirrors, so dirty text crosses without a second editor/cache. Entry gestures are the shared Table/Board `PageContextMenu` (`Open in right panel` LAST since YAZ-1556 — the pane-specific open sits below the page actions every surface shares), ⌥-click or ⌥⏎ on a board card or a table name (YAZ-1557 🔒 D1 — ONE rule in `client/src/lib/openGesture.ts` for both views, mouse and keyboard: plain = current tab, ⌘ = background tab (I3), ⌥ = right panel, ⇧/⌃ = nothing, because ⇧ is the selection gesture everywhere; a plain click on a board card no longer opens anything — it SELECTS the card, 🔒 D2 below under "Views"; secondary-click, group drag, and preview remain separate), tab/header move commands, and native drag under private MIME `application/x-yaseen-workspace-page`; only validated absolute `{ path, owner: 'main' | 'right' }` payloads enter midpoint/end insertion slots, stale/wrong/external data no-op. The divider previews locally and persists only on commit; below 192 px hides without losing width, while narrow available space derives an overlay without rewriting identity. Rename/delete/root repair covers current paths, retained mounts, expanded choice, and histories; delete retires autosave before workspace removal, rename/transfer carries before remap, and duplicate windows deep-copy identity so later changes are independent. Automated ownership/drag/shell/lifecycle proof lives in `client/src/workspace/*.test.*`, `client/src/right-panel/RightPanel.test.tsx`, `client/src/tabs/TabBar.test.tsx`, `client/src/App.test.tsx`, and the desktop store/window/IPC tests. Since YAZ-2171 one pointed Playwright spec, `desktop/e2e/rightPanel.spec.ts`, pins move-in, resize, hide/show and the relaunch restore; final acceptance still uses the isolated Electron profile and focused demo vault before merge.
- **Single instance** (GRO-2160): `requestSingleInstanceLock` — a second launch quits itself; the first instance gets `second-instance`, routes any `yaseendocs://` URLs in its argv (E1), else focuses a window.
- **Close/quit flush handshake** (GRO-2160): `close` is ALWAYS intercepted — main commits the bounds, sends `app:flush`, the renderer flushes its autosaves (`window.onFlush` listeners: the note editor's own, and App's `flushWindow`, which flushes the outline editor from its live document and then awaits every in-flight `transformFile` frontmatter write, YAZ-2174) and acks `app:flushed`, then main `destroy()`s the window; a hung save can never block close (hard 5 s cap). A user close removes the window's state entry; the LAST window closing quits (`window-all-closed`), and quits — like ⌘Q's flush of every window, all at once under one 5 s cap (YAZ-2198) — keep the entries so relaunch restores them. ⌘Q is `runQuitSequence` (`main/quitSequence.ts`, YAZ-2174): the windows first, then `allSettled` of the state file, the index cache and the last sync pass, then `app.exit(0)`; a rejected step skips none of the others.
- **Same file in N windows** (GRO-2169 — D6/D9/A4): no dedicated sync channel — the disk-change path IS the sync. A window's save lands on disk → the shared watcher emits `change` to every window → each other window reloads silently when clean, or shows the conflict bar (Reload / Keep mine) when dirty. Echo suppression is per window: each window's own `Autosave` controller holds the mtime from ITS last `writeFile` response and only drops events matching that mtime (client `editor/Editor.tsx` — the one host left since YAZ-844 retired `BaseHost`), so window A's save is never mistaken for window B's echo. Folds are shared per file through the app state (`folders[root].folds`) — last writer wins, applied the next time the file is opened; a live window never adopts ANOTHER window's keys. ⚡ AMENDED by YAZ-1342: a window's OWN silent reload does re-fold, from its LIVE session keys — both fold plugins take `seedCollapsedKeys`, a getter read on every state init rather than a set frozen at mount — so folds on unchanged lines survive an external (AI) edit to the open file and the rebuilt plugin can no longer report an empty set and wipe the persisted keys. ⚡ AMENDED by YAZ-1347: that silent reload is no longer a rebuild — `applyExternalMarkdown` (`editor/external/`) parses the new body, structurally diffs it against the live doc (`diffDocs.ts`) and dispatches ONE `addToHistory: false` transaction stamped `external-edit`, so folds, caret, zoom and scroll ride ProseMirror's position mapping through ANY reword of a collapsed line, and the persisted keys re-derive from the live labels afterwards. The content-hash keys (rule 5) now serve cold start and the fallback only: a whole-document rewrite (one range ≥ 80% of the doc), an unparsable body, or a diff that fails to reproduce the parsed doc exactly falls back to the `setMarkdown` rebuild and its key-based reseed, so no edit ever behaves worse than v0.9.1. Enumeration prefixes (`12) `, `3. `, `X) `) are stripped from key labels (YAZ-1353), so a renumber done while the app is closed keeps cold-start folds too. Zoom is per window and in-memory only, never persisted. One watcher per folder in main, shared by every window and subscription (`desktop/src/main/fs/watchers.ts`); it closes when the last subscriber leaves.

## Links (E1/E2/E3 — GRO-2171/2172/2173)

- **Format** (E1, locked D7): a note's shareable URL is its `yaseendocs://` file link — path-only, absolute, percent-encoded (`yaseendocs:///Users/me/vault/My%20note.md`), no vault id; an optional `?root=` (also a percent-encoded absolute path) overrides which folder it opens under. `shared/links.ts` (`fileLink`/`parseFileLink`) is the ONE encoding, shared by main's parser and its own `open-file` re-encode (E2); `encodeURI` plus `#` and `?` encoded on top, so either never truncates the path.
- **Copy link** — TOMBSTONE (YAZ-1554): the sidebar row menu's "Copy link" (E3 GRO-2173, a `yaseendocs://` URL; a `[[wikilink]]` since YAZ-957) was deleted. It sat directly under "Copy path" and the two were easy to confuse, and `[[` already links from inside a note. Everything that existed only for it went with it: `MenuTargets.copyLinkText`, the sidebar's `viewOnlyLinks` prop, and `linkName` on the view-only source and catalog. Main's `yaseendocs://` handler and `shared/links.ts` are untouched.
- **Routing** (E1): a link — clicked anywhere (`open-url`), handed over by Finder's Open With (`open-file`, E2 — the plain path is re-encoded through `fileLink` and rides the same pipeline), or in a second launch's argv — routes identically (`resolveLinkTarget`): (1) the open window whose root contains the file — most specific root wins, Welcome windows never match — is focused and switches to it (`link.onOpenFile`); (2) else a new window opens on the most recent recents folder containing it (`?root=` pins the effective root instead when it contains the path); (3) else on the file's parent directory. Links queue until the windows are restored on cold start (`linkQueue.ts` — macOS fires `open-url` before `ready`), so hot and cold behave the same.
- **Failure surface** (E1): an unsupported, missing or non-regular-file target — or an unparsable link — focuses a live window and shows the passive in-window notice (`link.onNotice`), never a dialog. Every supported Markdown/text/PDF/image file may route through the existing window/new-window pipeline.
- **In-window wiki-link navigation** (Links C, GRO-2192) is RENDERER-LOCAL: clicking a `[[wikilink]]` in the editor drives this window's own tabs API (`openCurrent` / `openBackground`, Editor rule 23) — no IPC, no main-process involvement; the manager's `routeToFile` (the Routing bullet above, `windows.ts`) stays the ONLY path that focuses another window or creates one.
- **Aliases** (Links E2, GRO-2214): a note's frontmatter `aliases` are extra NAMES it answers to across the whole app — the index carries them (`IndexRecord.aliases`, table below), the ONE resolver consults them LAST (see "Link resolution"), so `[[CAC]]` renders resolved (Editor rule 23) and clicking it opens the aliased page, and the `[[` picker offers the page under each alias, inserting the piped `[[Name|Alias]]` — or, for a note that has an `id`, the plain `[[<id>]]` (rule 24, YAZ-2293). Two consequences pinned elsewhere: alias values are never outgoing `links`, and a rename never rewrites an alias-form link (Rename, below).
- **Note ids** (YAZ-2293 — see "Note ids"): a link whose target is a note's frontmatter `id` — `[[k3m9x2pq7abc]]` — names the NOTE, not its name or its place. The ONE resolver tries the id FIRST (see "Link resolution"); the app WRITES a link that way whenever the note has an id (the `[[` picker, rule 24; a view's link editors, "Cell editing"); the editor, the backlinks snippets and every view show the note's current title in the id's place (rules 23 and 25; "Note ids": What a link reads as); and no rename ever rewrites one (Rename, below). A link written by name keeps working exactly as it did, and a note with no id is still linked by name.
- **Rename** (E1, GRO-2194 — decision E of GRO-2096, LOCKED): an in-app rename updates links AUTOMATICALLY, no prompt (Obsidian's default). ⚡ AMENDED by YAZ-888 for NAME changes only: a changed NAME now asks first, once, in one sheet — the links still ALWAYS update, and a MOVE still never asks, so everything below is unchanged and runs on confirm ("The page title + rename confirm"). Scope since E1b (GRO-2241): FILE rename and cross-directory move, and FOLDER rename — the extension kind stays unchanged for files; E1b lifted E1's same-parent and files-only guards. Gestures: the sidebar file- OR folder-row context menu's "Rename" → inline input over the row (files: prefilled name minus extension, `validateEntryName` + the `entryPath` extension re-append via `renamedPath`; folders: raw name, no extension logic; same name = no-op; no hotkey v1); and drag a FILE row onto a folder row or the root header → move there (same pipeline; no multi-select, no folder dragging — Future; Cut ⌘X / Paste ⌘V, YAZ-1674, moves a multi-selection and folders through the same rename pipeline). Flow, in order: (1) the originating window FLUSHES its own autosave for the file, so the buffer travels with it; (2) it snapshots the vault index BEFORE renaming (afterwards the old name no longer resolves); (3) `file.rename` renames on disk, repairs the app state and broadcasts `file:renamed` (bridge table above); (4) every window's renderer — BEFORE remapping its tab — captures a DIRTY old-path buffer, RETIRES the old autosave controller (its unmount/close flushes become no-ops: the old path can never be resurrected) and stashes the buffer under the new path, where the freshly mounted editor applies it as an UNSAVED change (`lib/renameContinuity.ts`; title/hash follow the active tab's existing effects); (5) the originating window rewrites every referencing note (`links/renameLinks.ts`): the referencing set is the index records whose `links`/`embeds` resolve — through THE shared resolver (`resolverFor`) — to the old path; per file read → rewrite → write with `expectedMtime`, one CONFLICT re-read-retry, a second conflict skips the file. Rewrites preserve `|alias` and `#heading`/`#^block`, preserve link FORM (bare→new bare name, pathed→new root-relative, explicit extension stays), cover `![[embeds]]`, skip fenced/inline code (a length-preserving mirror of the index's `stripCode`), and touch frontmatter only as whole-value exact `[[…]]` strings via `setFrontmatterProperty`. ONE key is walked DEEPER than whole-value (YAZ-864): `folder_settings`, the single app-owned key with app-defined link semantics (🔒 Q1 — "Folder settings: `.folder.md`"; since YAZ-2290 it lives in the folders' `.folder.md` files, which ride the rename as referencing files beside the notes — `UpdateLinksOptions.folders`, rewritten like notes, never resolved to), whose `views[].order` entries, outline LINES that are exactly a wikilink (YAZ-900, `mapOutlineLinks` — a link inside prose is not a leaf) and `columns.<name>.target` strings are links the index never extracted and a rename therefore used to leave dangling; they rewrite exactly as a top-level entry does (same `resolves`/`newTarget`, so the bare-vs-pathed and alias rules cannot drift), the whole key goes back through `setFrontmatterProperty` as the one door's own write shape, and the referencing-set probe learned the SAME leaves — a page whose only reference lives in there has no `links` entry to be found by, so probe and rewrite stay one construction. The key's own module owns both the key name and the list of link-bearing leaves (`folderSettings.ts` `mapFolderSettingsLinks` / `folderSettingsLinks`), read from the RAW value rather than the tolerant read, so a hand-written unusable shape is never normalised away by a rename; a stale entry that never resolved to the renamed page stays byte-identical, as it always did. Outcomes are PASSIVE (never a dialog): one summary notice "Updated links in N notes" (+ "; M skipped (unsaved changes)"), `ALREADY_EXISTS` and other failures land in the same link-notice; another window's dirty referencing editor falls back to the normal conflict bar — EXCEPT for a referencing note that the rename itself remaps (a folder rename moving an open dirty note), whose editor remounts, re-reads from disk and adopts the post-rewrite baseline, so the conflict never surfaces (GRO-2197 audit, deferred). Accepted residual race: a save already in flight over IPC when the rename lands can still recreate the old path — the systematic paths (debounce, unmount flush, close flush) are all covered by the retire step. ALIAS-form links are never rewritten (E2, GRO-2214): `[[CAC]]` reaching the moved file through its frontmatter `aliases` keeps resolving afterwards — the alias travels with the file — so the referencing-set probe resolves BY NAME ONLY (`resolverFor(records, root, { aliases: false, ids: false })`); a note referencing the moved file only by alias is never even read. ID-form links are never rewritten either (YAZ-2293 D5): `[[k3m9x2pq7abc]]` reaches the moved file through the `id` in that file's own frontmatter, which travels with it too, so the same probe resolves no ids (`ids: false`) — an id link is never counted, stays BYTE-IDENTICAL beside a name link that is rewritten, and a note referencing the moved file only by id is never even read. KNOWN GAP (GRO-2197 audit, deferred): the banner's N and the number of notes actually written can still disagree on a pure MOVE — N counts every note whose links RESOLVE to the old path, while the rewrite writes nothing for a bare link that stays bare, and in a pure move every bare link stays bare, so "update N links?" can be followed by "Updated links in 0 notes". Closing it needs N to become link-FORM aware, not just resolution aware. E1b bare-vs-pathed rules, LOCKED: a FOLDER rename rewrites only PATHED links/embeds to the notes inside it (to the new root-relative path) — bare-name links keep resolving (names unchanged) and stay BYTE-IDENTICAL; links to the FOLDER ITSELF (🔒 D10, YAZ-2290 — "Links to folders" below) follow the FILE rule — a bare `[[Projects]]` becomes the new name, a pathed one the new path, and a bare one stays untouched while the name still reaches the folder (a move), resolved over the PRE-rename folders (`UpdateLinksOptions.dirs`); a FILE move keeps a bare link bare only when the bare form still resolves to the moved file AFTERWARDS (checked through the resolver against a POST-move record set — the shallowest rule can hand the name to a duplicate), else it escalates to the pathed form (a kept bare form is never spliced, padding included). TOMBSTONE (YAZ-844): the base-embed half of this rule is gone — `resolveBasePath`, the PRE-rename TREE snapshot the rewrite used to take beside the index one, and the `tree` argument of `updateLinksAfterRename` / `countLinkReferences` all died with `![[X.base]]`. The retired engine `tree` argument stays gone: Markdown targets resolve through index records, while explicit text/PDF/image targets use the separate catalog; App may fetch `api.tree` only before the rename gate to pin that catalog. Refusals (all passive notices): existing target → `ALREADY_EXISTS`; missing target parent → `NOT_FOUND` (never a mkdir); dot-dirs and the calling window's own vault ROOT → `BAD_REQUEST` (root identity is a recents/vault question, out of E1b scope). A window rooted AT (or under) the renamed folder follows it live: its tabs prefix-remap and its local root state remaps without an identity write (main's store repair already moved `WindowEntry.root`).
- **External renames** (E1c, GRO-2242 — LOCKED ruling): a rename/move done OUTSIDE the app (Finder, terminal, sync tools) is detected and repaired too — CONFIRM-FIRST, never automatically, never a dialog (ahead of Obsidian, which does not repair external renames at all — verified). ONE hypothesis detector (`client/src/links/renameDetector.ts` `detectRenames`), TWO feeds: (a) COLD-START — after the first index snapshot the client reads `coldDiff(root)` (bridge table above) and joins `removed` × `added`; trusted only on `cacheStatus === 'hit'`; (b) WHILE-RUNNING — consecutive `useIndex` snapshots (`WikilinkIndexBridge.onSnapshot` — no new watchers, no polling), diffed into the same removed/added shape, with UNMATCHED removals and additions carried forward exactly ONE snapshot generation. That carry is load-bearing, not belt-and-braces (GRO-2197): the watcher settles each path on its own (100 ms; chokidar's `awaitWriteFinish` ~200 ms on the polling fallback), so an `add` can trail its `unlink`, and the index applies `unlink` synchronously but `add` behind an async `scanFile`, so the index genuinely passes through a ~100 ms window showing the removal WITHOUT the addition. The 300 ms refetch debounce usually straddles both — but when it does not, the pair splits across two generations, `detectRenames` never sees it, and the hypothesis was previously lost forever (measured: `externalRename.spec.ts` steps 1 and 3 failed ~50-100% of runs under CPU load, 0/9 idle). Carried entries are re-validated against the new snapshot first (a removal whose path came back was a save, not a rename; an addition that vanished again is dropped) and only the CURRENT generation's leftovers are carried on, so the memory is bounded to one generation. Guard rails, firm: markdown files only; join key = EXACT (size, mtime) equality (macOS rename/move preserves both); zero-byte files skip; only UNAMBIGUOUS 1:1 pairs — a signature carried by several removed or several added files skips entirely (no guessing); equal basenames (a pure move) still hypothesize — pathed links may need rewriting. Either feed lands in ONE passive app-level banner (`useExternalRenames` — the link-notice idiom with two actions, one hypothesis at a time, oldest first): "Looks like `X` became `Y` — update N links?" [Update] [Dismiss]. N is the rewrite engine's own referencing-set count (`countLinkReferences`, the dry-run twin of `updateLinksAfterRename`, over a PRE-rename snapshot synthesised by re-pathing the moved record back — `preRenameRecords`); N === 0 → no banner, nothing happens at all. Update → (1) `file.repairRename` (bridge table above — store/tabs/editors follow via the existing `file:renamed` downstream; a refused claim, e.g. the old path came back, lands as a passive notice), (2) the E1/E1b rewrite engine old→new over fresh index/tree snapshots re-pathed pre-rename, (3) the usual summary notice — always shown here (the user explicitly asked). Dismiss → the pair joins a SESSION-level dismissed set and is never re-offered (the next refetch re-detects the same diff; the set is what keeps it quiet); a handled (Updated) pair joins the same set. In-app renames echo through the watcher as identical unlink+add pairs, so every `file:renamed` push also feeds the suppression set (exact pair for files; prefix mapping for dirs, since a folder rename echoes as one pair per moved note) — own renames never banner. The DIR prefix mappings are cleared on a root switch (GRO-2197): they exist only to swallow the current vault's echoes, and left standing they sat in the per-hypothesis linear scan for the rest of the session, across vaults. (The dismissed PAIR set is session-level by ruling and deliberately survives a root switch — its keys are absolute paths.)
- **Backlinks** (Links D, GRO-2193 — decision D of GRO-2096, LOCKED): the reverse direction is RENDERER-LOCAL too. "Linked mentions (N)" is a collapsible block in the note's own scroll — and in a folder's page (YAZ-2290 D10) — computed per render from the index snapshot the window already has through the ONE resolver (so alias-form and id-form links count) — no reverse map in main, no new IPC, no index field, no new watcher. Context snippets are read on demand for the rows actually shown. Full rules: Editor rule 25.
- **Links to folders** (🔒 D10, YAZ-2290 — `client/src/links/folderLinks.ts`): a folder is a page, so it is linkable. `linkResolver(records, root, dirs, folders)` is THE path-level resolver every wikilink surface shares (`ResolveLink`: target → absolute path or null), built by `WikilinkIndexBridge` per snapshot: a NOTE first — id, path, name, alias, through `resolverFor` — and a FOLDER only when none answers, so no link that resolved before means anything new and a note or alias of the same name always wins. `folderResolver` tries the id of the folder's `.folder.md`, then its root-relative path, then its bare name — duplicates to the SHALLOWEST, equal depth to the first in tree order, case-insensitive, `[[…]]` / `|alias` / `#heading` stripped. Which folders exist is the Files tree's to say (`vaultDirs`, off the window's one tree feed), so empty folders count and the vault ROOT is not linkable. A folder has no index record: what its link resolves to is the DIRECTORY path — a folder tab's identity — so a click opens the folder tab, ⌘-click a background one, and the backlinks pass compares it like any path ("Linked mentions" on a folder's page lists the notes and the folders that link to it; a folder's settings are a mention source, Editor rule 25). `[[` completion offers folders (`folderLinkCandidates`) after the note rows, each reading `<name> (folder)` — display text only, never typed or written. A folder whose `.folder.md` holds an id is inserted by that ID, under the folders' own shortest name, so a note of the same name is simply a second row. A folder with no id is inserted by NAME — or by root-relative path when the bare name belongs to something else — and gets no row when neither spelling reaches it. The picker never writes a `.folder.md`. A link to a folder READS as the folder's name in view cells and in the properties panel (`pageResolver`, over `FolderHost.resolveLink` in a view), and sorts and groups under that name. Renaming a folder rewrites the links to it (Rename, above). A link column's `target: "[[Folder]]"` narrows its picker to what that folder shows — the notes that live in it and its shortcuts (`belongsToBasenames`, 🔒 Q2 of YAZ-815): resolved like a click, so a note of that name wins and narrows nothing; when the target is no folder, or the folder holds none, the picker offers ALL notes (report-don't-block: it narrows when it can and never goes empty). Candidates are offered by basename and written by `id` (YAZ-2293). Tests: `links/folderLinks.test.ts`, `links/renameLinks.test.ts`, `views/FolderView.page.test.tsx`.
- The in-app `#/abs/path.md` hash (Editor rule 17) is separate and unchanged.

**YAZ-1289 rename amendment (supersedes the tombstone's final `api.tree` sentence):** the removed base-embed tree argument stays dead, and semantic Markdown targets still resolve only through index records. For a text/PDF/image file rename, App reuses a ready catalog only when it proves that exact target; a directory rename always obtains one fresh lightweight `api.tree(root)` snapshot so newly arrived descendants cannot be missed. A required snapshot failure stops before confirmation or mutation, the accepted root/generation is checked after the await, and the same pre-rename catalog is pinned through confirmation into count/rewrite. Switching roots cancels an old request silently and clears any open rename sheet. Explicit-extension links resolve through that catalog, while the referencing and written files remain Markdown records only. Post-move catalog resolution decides whether a bare duplicate spelling can remain bare or must escalate to a root-relative path. View-only target files are never read or written by link repair.

## Note ids (YAZ-2293 — decisions D1-D8)

- **The id** (🔒 D2, `shared/noteId.ts`): every Markdown note carries a permanent identity in its frontmatter — `id: k3m9x2pq7abc`, the key spelled `NOTE_ID_KEY`. It is given once and never derived again from the file's name or place, so a rename or a move by ANYTHING — the app, Finder, a sync tool, an agent — keeps every `[[<id>]]` link pointing at the note. Shape (`isNoteId`): exactly 12 characters of lowercase Crockford base32 (`0123456789abcdefghjkmnpqrstvwxyz` — no `i l o u`), 60 bits, because nothing can check a fresh id against the other device's; lowercase because link targets resolve case-insensitively; and ALWAYS at least one digit, so no word a person might name a note is an id. A frontmatter `id` of any other shape is someone else's value — never an id to the index, the resolver or any menu. `mintNoteId()` draws a fresh random one (`crypto.getRandomValues`) for a note being created; `noteIdFrom(draw)` builds one out of any byte source and draws again for the roughly 1% of draws that come out all letters.
- **Where a note gets it**: BORN with it — every creation goes through `createFile`, which sets it in the creating write (bridge table) — or, for a note that ARRIVED without one, given it by the sweep below. The index carries it as `IndexRecord.id` ("Property index"), and that field is what the whole renderer reads: no surface parses a file to learn an id.
- **The sweep** (D3, D4, D13 — `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/idSweep.ts` `sweepIds(root, records, among, knew, dirs)`): a note the app did not create arrives with no id, and a copied note arrives carrying its original's. The sweep runs in main on the live index (`live.ts`): after EACH watch-event scan (`add` / `change`), over that one record, and once after the cold-start reconcile, over every record — NOT awaited, so a vault of id-less notes never holds up its first index. Each note in its batch that has NO id is given one. 🔒 **Every folder holds its id** (D13): each of `dirs` whose `.folder.md` the index does not hold is given one holding only its `id` — at the cold start every folder the index walk went into (`scan.ts` `walk`'s `dirs`: what the tree shows, so no dot-folder, no `node_modules`, nothing inside one, and never the vault root), on a watch `addDir` that one folder, and on adoption a fresh walk (`sweepIndexed`). An empty folder and one holding only images get it too. A `.folder.md` deleted while the app runs, from a folder that is still there, is created again holding the id it had (`restoreFolderId`, on the watch `unlink`). Of the indexed notes SHARING an id only the KEEPER keeps it — the note the index already knew to hold it (at cold start, per the persisted cache; on a live event, every other indexed note is known to hold what it holds and the scanned one what it held before the event), else the first in path order, which is the same answer on every device — and any other note in the batch is given a fresh one. So a copy gets its own id, and every link to the original stays the original's. 🔒 **Only in an ADOPTED vault** — `<root>/.yaseendocs/` exists (`isAdopted`): the app never writes behind the user's back into a folder it has merely been pointed at (the standing rule of YAZ-797). A folder becomes adopted by its first config write, or — 🔒 — by the first note or folder created in it from a window rooted there: the `fs:create-file` and `fs:create-dir` handlers (`desktop/src/main/ipc/fs.ts`) call `adoptVault(root)`, and when that call is what adopted it, `sweepIndexed(root)` (`live.ts`) gives ids to the notes already indexed and to the folders there. In an un-adopted folder the sweep touches no file, a note that arrived without an id stays without one, and it is linked by name; a folder there has no `.folder.md` until something changes it, and reads as the defaults. **The id is DERIVED, not random** (`giveId`): `noteIdFrom` over the sha256 of the note's vault-relative path (`/` separators), a NUL and the file's content — hashed again for an all-letters draw, and for an id some indexed note already holds (YAZ-2378: the sweep hands `giveId` that check, so a taken id is never written; the command line has no index and passes none). Two devices that both meet the same note before syncing therefore make the SAME edit, which merges; a different id on each would be a conflict, and the built-in sync stops on one (`git/sync.ts`). **Compare-and-set**: `giveId(root, file, held, taken?)` re-reads the file, goes on only while its frontmatter `id` still IS what the index saw (none, or the shared one), sets that one key through `setFrontmatterProperty` — every other byte kept — and writes with `expectedMtime`, so a note that changed under the sweep is left for its next index event. Left ALONE, silently: a properties block that does not parse, a hand-written `id` of another shape, an oversize file. It never throws and is safe to run any number of times: its own write comes back as a watch `change`, rescans the note, and the second look needs nothing. A folder's settings file (`.folder.md`) sits in the same record map and is given an id like any note. The ONE file it creates is a missing `.folder.md` (D13): `giveId` reads it as empty, so the id is derived from its vault-relative path alone — the same file, byte for byte, on every device — and creates it exclusively (`createDurable`), so one that appears before the write stays as it is; a folder gone by then is passed over. A folder renamed or moved keeps its file and its id: nothing is derived again.
- **Links by id**: resolution tries the id first ("Link resolution"); the app writes `[[<id>]]` from every `[[` picker row and its Create row (Editor rule 24) and from the views' link editors ("Cell editing"); a rename never counts or rewrites one ("Links": Rename). Still written BY NAME: a link to a note that has no id, a hand-typed link, and a link to a FOLDER that has no id — the `[[` picker inserts a folder's id when it has one, else its name or path (YAZ-2290 D10, "Links": Links to folders).
- **What a link reads as** (D8 — the file stores the id, the app shows the title): in the editor, Editor rule 23; in the backlinks snippets, rule 25. In views the ONE answer is `linkText(link, resolve?)` (`client/src/views/expr/values.ts`): the hand-typed label, else the current title — the file name without its extension — of the note the id names (a `#heading` rides along), else the target as written. `render(v, resolve?)`, and `chip` / `cellContent` / `GroupHeader` in `client/src/views/view/GroupHeader.tsx`, take that optional resolver, and WITH it the title is what is shown and what is ordered by: table, list, cards and board values and chips, group headers and board column headers, the summary values, the properties panel's link rows, a link column's sort, the order and label of groups, and the views' search. WITHOUT a resolver `render` is exactly the stored `[[…]]`, and that is what every write-back and persisted key passes — a cell commit, the group-drag swap, a group "+" seed, and the collapse key `groupKeyOf` (`v:[[<id>]]`) — so renaming a note never rewrites a property and never reopens a collapsed group. A formula's `list.join()` renders without one too. The hover preview (`client/src/views/view/PreviewCard.tsx` `usePreview(enabled, wikilinks?)`) receives the window's link source, because an id link has no readable text of its own.
- **Copy ID** (`client/src/lib/copyNoteId.ts` `copyNoteId(id, onNotice?)` — the one body behind every surface): exactly the note's id on the clipboard, said through the passive notice either way — `Copied ID`, or `Can't copy ID: <reason>` when the clipboard refuses. It is offered ONLY where there is an id to copy: a note with none, a text / PDF / image file, a folder and blank space offer nothing. Four surfaces: (1) the sidebar row menu, in every lens — the `copyId` leaf of `menuSections.ts`, directly under Copy path and above Copy for Agent, no hint because there is no chord, and never in a 2+ selection (`MenuTargets.noteId`, read off the window's index snapshot when the menu opens); (2) the tab menu (`TabBar`'s `noteId(path)` prop, read when the menu opens) — Copy path · Copy ID · Copy for Agent; (3) the views' page menu on a Table row, a Board card, a card or a List row (`PageContextMenu`'s `noteId`, the row's own record) — Copy path · Copy ID · Reveal in Finder; (4) right-click on a rendered id link in the editor (Editor rule 23), where the id is the LINK's — the note it points at.
- **Reserved**: `id` is one of `RESERVED_KEYS` (`client/src/links/reservedKeys.ts`) — a **Reserved** row with no editor in the properties panel ("The properties panel") while its value is a note id (a hand-written `id: 42` is the user's own property, with an editor), and a column a view can hide but never delete ("Folder settings: `.folder.md`": Delete column). It is never a DEFAULT column: `propertyKeys` (`client/src/views/engine.ts`) leaves it out of a view with no saved order, and `allPropertyKeys` (`client/src/views/view/properties.ts`) still offers it, so a view can add it.
- **The command line** (`desktop/src/cli/cli.ts` — the `yaseendocs` door of "Comments", whose `--help` is the contract an agent reads): two verbs, so an agent can work in names while the files hold ids. `yaseendocs id <page.md>` prints the page's id. A page that has one is only read — not a byte and not its mtime changes, vault or no vault. A page with none is given one first, through the sweep's own `giveId`, so the command and the app give a note the SAME id — and only inside a vault, which is the nearest folder above the page that holds `.yaseendocs/`. Refused with exit 1, the reason on stderr and the bytes untouched: `the properties block does not parse (invalid)`; `the id property is not a page id (foreign)`; `<page> has no id and is in no vault (no .yaseendocs folder above it)`; and `<page> changed while it was being given an id — run this again`. `id <folder>/.folder.md` on a folder with no settings file yet creates it, as the sweep would, and prints its id; a folder that is not there is not found. `yaseendocs links <page.md> [--json]` lists every id ON a page once — the note ids among its links (the index's own `links` extraction) first, then the note ids in its frontmatter `also_in` list — each with what that id names NOW, found by walking and scanning the vault exactly as the index does (`walk` + `scanAll`), in path order, so of two pages still sharing an id the one named is the sweep's first choice too. One line per id, `<id>  <what>`: a note → its vault-relative path; a folder (its id is carried by its `.folder.md`) → the folder's path with a trailing `/`, followed by `  (also in)` when the page reaches it only through `also_in`; no page or folder → `(missing)`. A link written by name is not an id and is not listed; a page with none prints `no ids on <page>`. `--json` prints the same rows as `{ id, kind: 'note' | 'folder' | 'missing', path? }`, 2-space indented. A page in no vault is refused: `<page> is in no vault (no .yaseendocs folder above it)`.
- **Testing**: `desktop/src/main/noteId.test.ts` (the shape), `desktop/src/main/fs/create.test.ts` (born with it), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/idSweep.test.ts` (the sweep, alone and wired into the live index), `scan.test.ts` / `cache.test.ts` (the field and the version pin), `desktop/src/cli/cli.test.ts` (the two verbs); in the client `views/engine.test.ts` (resolution, sort and group labels), `links/completion.test.ts`, `links/renameLinks.test.ts`, `links/backlinks.test.ts`, `editor/wikilink/wikilinkPlugin.test.ts` / `wikilinkClick.test.ts` / `wikilinkPicker.test.ts` / `wikilinkMenu.test.ts`, `views/expr/values.test.ts`, `views/view/EditableCell.test.tsx`, `sidebar/menuSections.test.ts`, `tabs/TabBar.test.tsx`, `views/view/PageContextMenu.test.tsx` and `editor/FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx`. Hand steps: `docs/REGRESSION.md`, "Note ids".
- Files: `shared/noteId.ts`, `shared/types.ts` (`IndexRecord.id`, `CreateFileRequest.id`, `CreateFileResponse.id`), `desktop/src/main/fs/create.ts`, `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/idSweep.ts` + `scan.ts` (`walk`, `scanFile`) + `live.ts` (the two hooks) + `cache.ts` (`CACHE_VERSION`), `desktop/src/cli/cli.ts`, `client/src/views/engine.ts` (`makeResolver`, `ResolverOptions.ids`), `client/src/links/completion.ts` + `renameLinks.ts` + `backlinks.ts` + `reservedKeys.ts`, `client/src/editor/wikilink/wikilinkPlugin.ts` (`idLinkTitle`, `linkDisplayText`) + `wikilinkClick.ts` + `wikilinkPicker.ts` + `createFromLink.ts` + `wikilinkMenu.ts`, `client/src/editor/blockHandleMenu.ts` (`renderMenu`), `client/src/views/expr/values.ts` (`linkText`, `render`), `client/src/views/view/GroupHeader.tsx` + `EditableCell.tsx` + `PageContextMenu.tsx` + `PreviewCard.tsx`, `client/src/lib/copyNoteId.ts`, `client/src/sidebar/menuSections.ts`, `client/src/tabs/TabBar.tsx`.

## Folders are the pages (YAZ-2290 — decisions D1-D11 and E1-E6, decided 2026-10-04, LOCKED)

A real FOLDER is the page: the subject is a DIRECTORY, never a note. Its config is "Folder settings: `.folder.md`", the notes it shows from elsewhere are "Note shortcuts", a link to it is "Links": Links to folders; this is the surface.

**A folder is a tab** (🔒 D3 — OVERTURNING 🔒 D3 of YAZ-1578, "a folder cannot be a tab"): the tab's identity IS the directory path, held in `WindowEntry.tabs` beside file paths. Three doors, all on the folder's row in the Files tree (`sidebar/Tree.tsx`): a DOUBLE-CLICK, **Open** in its menu (`menuSections.ts` `open`, `MenuTargets.openPath` — a folder row outside a 2+ selection), and ENTER on the focused row. A single click still selects and folds, Space still folds, shift never opens; "Open N in new tabs" counts folders with files. Only the Files tree can say which path is a folder — one may be NAMED `Notes.md` — so `Editor` asks the window's newest tree (`lib/pageLabel.ts` `useTreeKind` — folder, file or neither; `isFolderPath` for labels) BEFORE the file-kind dispatch and mounts `FolderView` for it; until a tree for an in-root path is known it renders nothing rather than guess, and a tree that lists the path as neither is not believed until one asked for NOW answers (it may only be behind a rename). The tab strip, the right panel and the window title label a folder by its WHOLE name (`pageLabel`), and its tree row is the active row while its tab is. An in-app rename or delete of the folder carries or closes its own tab like a file's (`useWorkspace`). A folder deleted OUTSIDE the app while its tab is showing keeps the tab, on the missing-file message (`useTreeKind` answering neither); the sidebar's two stale-tab checks — the first tree after launch, and a tab activated after its path left the tree — close a folder tab exactly as they close a file's (`treeHasPath`). The vault root has no row to open and no page (D11).

**What a folder shows** (🔒 D4): `links/shortcuts.ts` `folderRows(records, folders, folder)` — every markdown note UNDER this folder, at any depth, plus the notes that are shortcuts into it or into a folder under it, each once, in the index's own order. A subfolder is no row, and no other file kind is. The same rule from the note's side is `foldersShowing(folder, properties, byId)` — the folder the note lives in and each folder above it, nearest first and short of the root, then each folder its `also_in` names with the folders above that one, each once; a note directly in the vault root is shown by the root alone — and the rows are BUILT from it (`rowsByFolder`), so the two can not disagree. No set-level filter is stored or offered (🔒 Q3 of YAZ-815 — the set IS what the folder holds); a view's own `views[i].filters` narrow within it (YAZ-1218). The engine RUNS over those rows and RESOLVES over the whole vault (`RunOptions.resolve`, 🔒 D2 of YAZ-818), so a link cell pointing outside the folder still resolves, and the link pickers get the whole snapshot too (`FolderHost.vaultRecords` / `vaultFolders`). Feed: the window's ONE `WikilinkResolveSource` (`records`, `folders`, `resolve`) — no fetch, watcher or IPC of its own. Before the first snapshot (`resolve === null`) nothing renders: only the index can say a folder has no settings file.

**The page** (🔒 D9), top to bottom in the note editor's own scroller (`.editor-host`): `PageTitle kind="dir"` — the folder's whole name, a commit reaching App's one rename door as `(oldPath, newPath, 'dir')` and renaming the directory ("The page title") — then the folder's OWN `FrontmatterPanel`, the views (`.folder-view` → `ViewsPane`), the folder's OWN `CommentsSection`, and `BacklinksSection` for the directory path (D10). Properties and comments are the components a note uses, mounted on the bytes of `<folder>/.folder.md` and re-read whenever that file's index record moves; a folder with no settings file reads as `''`, and its first property, comment or raw-YAML Save creates the file. There is no body: no Crepe mount, no autosave, no Reviews section.

**The adapter** (🔒 D3 of YAZ-818): `ViewsPane` stays ONE component with ONE mount, `FolderView`. The host builds the def IN MEMORY from the settings — `folderViewSet`: `parseViews(stringify({ formulas, properties, views, defaultView }))`, a real parse because `ParsedViews` carries the yaml `Document` every edit is written into; YAML that will not parse falls back to `DEFAULT_VIEWS` — and turns every `onChange` into exactly ONE `folder_settings` write. Which view is ACTIVE is session state and switching writes nothing; the tabs themselves edit (🔒 D0, YAZ-1471), and the saved START rides in the def as `defaultView` (YAZ-1104; 🔒 D4, YAZ-1471). Values ARE the host's (**Values** below): it maps the rows it hands over and owns the one writer. **The echo guard**: one `stampOf` over views / formulas / labels / `defaultView` decides whether the def is rebuilt from an index snapshot; own writes queue their stamp in `pending`, so the echo of an older own write never rebuilds the def under a menu mid-edit (YAZ-1234/1241), while a real external edit clears the queue — disk wins. Column declarations run AHEAD of the index the same way (`ahead`, YAZ-1549) and are what `FolderHost.settings.columns` exposes. A refused settings write shows `Could not update the folder: <message>` above the views, never a dialog.

**Collapsed groups** persist in app state as `baseGroups[<folder path>::<viewName>]` (4C), never in `.folder.md`; `ViewsPane.moveCollapsed` carries the entry on a view rename and prunes it on a delete (YAZ-1493). The keys inside are a persisted contract: `groupKeyOf` — `∅` for "No value", else `v:<render(key)>` with NO resolver, so an id link is keyed as stored (YAZ-2293 D8) — and for an inner group `nestedGroupKeyOf`, outer and inner joined by U+001F (YAZ-745).

**The outline is a document** (🔒 D5/D5a; 🔒 D2 of YAZ-900 stands): ONE free-form markdown bullet list, held whole as the string `views[i].outline`. A link in it is just a link — it decorates, completes and navigates like any wikilink and says NOTHING about what the folder holds. `views/outlineDoc.ts` owns the string's grammar (relative-indent depth, one canonical spelling, `escapeBlockStart`); `OutlineEditor` (YAZ-901) is a second, small Milkdown instance — `createCrepe()` locked to one bullet list by `editor/outline/bulletsOnly.ts` — seeded at mount, guarded against a lossy seed (YAZ-974: read-only, reported, never written back), fed later disk changes as a diff in which typing wins (YAZ-1356), saving on Crepe's debounce plus 500 ms and flushing on unmount and on the close/quit handshake. One per folder: `ViewsPane` reads and writes the FIRST outline view's document, and the "+" menu offers Outline only while there is none. While it shows, the toolbar offers no Properties, Filter, Preview or Search.

**Births** (🔒 D4, E1, E3): "New" and a group "+" in the folder view (`FolderView` `createInFolder`), "New note" in the Files tree (`sidebar/hooks/rowGestures.ts`) and a note created from an unresolved `[[link]]` (`editor/wikilink/createFromLink.ts`) are born alike — `views/scaffold.ts` `createNote(path, seed?)`, the ONE birth function: the `.template.md` of the folder it lands in, frontmatter and body, then what the caller's seed makes of its properties — a folder view's "New" sets its values in that folder's block ("Folder settings": Values) — and nothing else, in one atomic `createFile`. From the view and the tree the note lands IN the folder. From the view it is named `Untitled`, `Untitled 2`… over the basenames of the notes that LIVE there (a shortcut's name is not taken here); a typed name — the board's inline add — has `/` turned to a space.

Files: `client/src/views/FolderView.tsx` + `folderView.css` (+ `FolderView.test.tsx`, `FolderView.page.test.tsx`), `client/src/views/ViewsPane.tsx` (`FolderHost`), `client/src/views/view/OutlineView.tsx` + `OutlineEditor.tsx` (+ tests), `client/src/views/outlineDoc.ts`, `client/src/views/scaffold.ts`, `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (the dispatch), `client/src/lib/pageLabel.ts`, `client/src/sidebar/Tree.tsx` + `menuSections.ts`, `client/src/tabs/TabBar.tsx`, `client/src/right-panel/RightPanel.tsx`.

## Folder settings: `.folder.md` (YAZ-2290 — D1, D8, E1-E4; the key's rules are Q1-Q8 of YAZ-815, LOCKED)

**Storage** (🔒 D1): a folder's settings live in a hidden `<folder>/.folder.md` (`FOLDER_SETTINGS_FILE`, `shared/types.ts`), FRONTMATTER ONLY. It holds the view settings under the ONE key `folder_settings`, and beside it the folder's own properties (ordinary top-level keys), its `comments` and its `id`. The file travels with the folder, so a rename or move of the folder updates nothing in it.

**Born with the folder** (🔒 D13): every folder the tree shows holds a `.folder.md` with its `id` from the moment it exists — written by `createDir` for a folder made in the app (a fresh id), and by the id sweep, in an adopted vault, for a folder made anywhere else (an id derived from its path; "Note ids": The sweep). At birth it holds ONLY `id`: no `folder_settings`, no body, so it still reads as the defaults, and a first settings write is an ordinary edit of it.

**Else created on the FIRST change** (🔒 D1) — an un-adopted folder, or a folder the sweep has not reached yet: no file means the defaults, and opening a folder writes NOTHING. `views/writeProperty.ts` `readForWrite` reads a missing `.folder.md` (`isFolderSettingsPath`) as EMPTY — `{ content: '', mtime: 0 }`, nothing created — and the WRITE that follows is what creates it (`writeFile` with `expectedMtime: 0`; another writer creating it first is a `CONFLICT`), so a first save that changes nothing, or is refused, leaves no file on disk. Every `transformFile` writer and the properties panel's raw Save get it from there. It is born by a plain write, not through the `fs:create-file` door: it holds no `id` until the sweep gives it one ("Note ids": The sweep) — a folder's first shortcut writes the id itself ("Note shortcuts": Add) — and its birth does not adopt the vault. The command line's first `comment` on it creates it too (`transformOnDisk(…, create)`), holding no `id` until the app's sweep gives it one.

**The index and the watcher see it** (🔒 D8): `.folder.md` is the ONE dot-entry the index walk (`vaultIndex/scan.ts` `walk`) and the shared watcher (`fs/watchers.ts` `ignored`) let through, unless a folder above it is skipped. One record map holds notes and settings files; `getIndex` splits them by file name into `IndexResponse.folders` (sorted by path) and never `records`, so a settings file is no view row, no search result, no `[[` completion row, no backlink source, no review candidate and no external-rename hypothesis. `tree(root)` still skips it like every dot-entry, so the Files tree never lists it. Its record's `folder` (root-relative) names the folder it configures. The renderer holds the list as `WikilinkResolveSource.folders` (`useIndex` → `WikilinkIndexBridge`); the id sweep gives it an `id` like any note ("Note ids").

**The one key, the one module** (🔒 Q1): `client/src/views/folderSettings.ts` is the only reader and the only writer of `folder_settings` — `folderSettings(record | undefined)` reads, `writeFolderSettings` and `writeFolderColumn` write, and the rename engine comes in through `mapFolderSettingsLinks` ("Links": Rename). Shape: `columns` (name → `{ kind, target?, required?, options?, optionSort? }`, `kind` one of `PROPERTY_KINDS`' nine; an unknown kind means the column is absent) · optional `defaultView` (a view NAME; absent or stale → the first view) · optional `formulas` (name → expression, `ViewSet.formulas` verbatim, YAZ-745) · optional `properties` (the column LABELS, below) · `views` (the `ViewDef` objects verbatim — unknown view types and extra keys ride along).

**Tolerant parsing** (🔒 Q1): the read never throws and never blocks. Every bad shape becomes one human line in `problems` plus a safe default — a non-map `columns` declares none, a bad column costs only that column, a bad view entry is dropped — and `problems` is shown beside the views, never thrown, never written back.

**The defaults** (🔒 D5a, E2; Q7 as amended): a folder with no file, or a file without the key, has `DEFAULT_VIEWS` — **Table, then Board** — and `DEFAULT_COLUMNS`, one `status` Select (`1-Backlog` / `2-Todo` / `3-In-Progress` / `4-Done`, `client/src/views/folderSettings.ts`): every folder has a Status column by default, and nothing is written for it until a value is set. Once the key EXISTS it states the columns exactly: the first settings write states the default columns with it, a file that saved no columns has none, and Status is never added back. Cards, List and Outline are the "+" menu's to add. A `views` list that is unusable or empty reads as the defaults; a usable one reads back exactly as written, nothing injected (🔒 D3, YAZ-1471 — a read that adds a view back is a Delete the user cannot make stick).

**No empty keys in notes** (🔒 E1): a declared column is NEVER stamped into a note — not on open, not when a column is added or retyped, not at birth. A missing value is an empty cell: `RunOptions.declared` hands the folder's column names to `propertyKeys`, so a column shows before any note carries it, and a key reaches a note only when a value is set. A note's own properties panel lists the columns of the folder in force that it has no value for as empty rows ("The properties panel"). TOMBSTONE (YAZ-2290): YAZ-999's presence invariant, which stamped every declared key into every member, is gone.

**Typing is per folder** (🔒 Q8): `settings.columns[key]` — read by `views/editorType.ts` `columnTyping` — is ONE folder's own declaration, the typing ladder's top rung ("Cell editing"). Two folders may declare the same key differently and neither wins globally — no conflict resolver exists and none may be built. Retyping moves the declaration only; note values are never migrated or rewritten (C1 of YAZ-896/897, LOCKED).

**Labels** (🔒 D4, YAZ-1513): a column's KEY never changes; `properties` (`key → { displayName }`) says what the header says. Reading is `engine.ts` `propertyLabel` (the entry, looked up as written, bare and `note.`-prefixed, else `defaultLabel`); writing is the one function `view/columnLabel.ts` `setDisplayName`. A blank label is written as absent, and an emptied map deletes itself.

**Values** (🔒 D19): a folder's values for a note are saved in the NOTE under `in`, in a block named by the folder's id — `in: { <folder id>: { <column>: <value> } }` (`shared/folderValues.ts`) — and a folder's views read and write only that block, so two folders showing one note can hold different values under a column of the same name. The note's OWN fields — `id`, `aliases`, `tags`, `also_in`, comments, the review log, anything typed at the top level — are the same wherever it shows and are not `note.*` columns: a top-level field named like a column is the note's own, and no table shows it. ONE door each way, both in `FolderView`: the rows it hands `ViewsPane` carry `folderValues(properties, <folder id>)` as their `properties` — the engine, filters, sorts, groups, formulas, summaries and skins know nothing of folders or `in`; `vaultRecords`, which only resolves links, stays unmapped — and every value a view writes (a cell, a board or section drop) goes through `FolderHost.writeValues(path, writes)` → `writeFolderValues`, one guarded transform of that block (`setFolderValue`). `EditableCell` has no writer of its own. A missing value is an empty cell and nothing is written until one is set; an emptied block is removed, and `in` with its last block. An `in`, or a block, that is not a map reads as empty and is never written over (`FrontmatterWriteError`). A folder with no id shows empty values, and the first value written gives it one first — `folderId`, the path its first shortcut uses. "New" writes its seeded values into the OPENED folder's block of the new note, beside whatever the template holds there (`withFolderValues`) — also when a group-by-Folder "+" births it in a subfolder — while a `file.hasTag` seed is the note's own `tags`; a template is otherwise copied as written. `in` is one of `RESERVED_KEYS`. There is no fallback to top-level values and no migration.

**Delete column** (YAZ-1513): confirm-first (`ConfirmDeleteColumn`), ONE function behind both doorways (`views/deleteColumn.ts`), and its ORDER is the contract: (1) the declaration, every view reference (`order` through `withOrder`, `sort`, `groupBy`, `summaries`, `columnSize`, `cardStyle`, a cards `image`, every filter leaf naming it) and the label go in ONE settings write that is AWAITED — refused, nothing else moves; (2) only then the field is stripped, per file against fresh bytes, from THIS folder's block in every indexed note that holds it there (`notesHolding`) — nothing else in a note is touched: not its own field of that name, not another folder's. The confirm sheet states that count (`FolderHost.valueCount`), taken once, when it opens: `Delete "<label>"? This removes the column from this folder and the "<key>" value from N notes.` Strip failures are aggregated into the folder's banner; successes stay, no rollback. Never deletable, only hideable: `file.*`, `formula.*` and `RESERVED_KEYS` (`undeletableReason`).

**Templates** (🔒 E3): a folder's note template is the hidden `<folder>/.template.md` — existence is the whole declaration, read over plain `readFile`, invisible to tree, index and watcher like any dot-entry. Its frontmatter and body seed a new note under the caller's seed; an `id` in it is never copied. It replaced `<root>/.yaseendocs/templates/<name>.md`, which nothing reads now.

**The write path** (🔒 Q1): `writeFolderSettings(path, next)` writes the whole block as the one key through `writeProperty` (read → rewrite → `expectedMtime` → one retry), so the file's other keys survive; the block itself is rebuilt from the typed value, so comments and hand formatting INSIDE it do not. `columns` is omitted while empty, `problems` never reaches disk, `undefined` deletes the key. The UI reaches it through two doors on the host: `onChange` (every views edit) and the declarations' — `FolderHost.setColumns(columns, views?, labels?)`, which writes the LIVE def's views and labels when the caller names none (YAZ-1471 D4), and `setColumn` → `writeFolderColumn(path, key, next, base)`, one definition against fresh bytes, other columns and unknown metadata kept, a stale `base` refused. `deleteColumn` is not a third door: its settings half awaits the same one.

Files: `shared/types.ts` (`FOLDER_SETTINGS_FILE`, `folderSettingsPath`, `isFolderSettingsPath`, `IndexResponse.folders`), `client/src/views/folderSettings.ts` (+ `folderSettings.test.ts`, `writeFolderColumn.test.ts`), `client/src/views/writeProperty.ts` (`readForWrite`), `client/src/views/deleteColumn.ts` (+ test), `client/src/views/view/columnOrder.ts`, `columnLabel.ts`, `TableHeaderMenu.tsx`, `ConfirmDeleteColumn.tsx`, `client/src/views/scaffold.ts` (+ test), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts` + `live.ts` (+ `folderSettings.test.ts`), `desktop/src/main/fs/watchers.ts` (+ `watchers.test.ts`).

## Note shortcuts (YAZ-2290 — D2 as amended by YAZ-2293 D7, E4-E6, LOCKED)

**The model** (🔒 D2): a note LIVES in exactly one folder and may also APPEAR in others. The shortcut is stored on the NOTE — `also_in: ["<folder id>"]`, a folder's id being the `id` in its `.folder.md` — so it is the same file wherever it shows, and nothing is rewritten when a folder or a note is renamed or moved. `client/src/links/shortcuts.ts` is the one door to the key (`foldersShowing`, `rowsByFolder`, `folderRows`, `isShortcut`, `addShortcut`, `removeShortcut`, with `folderRecord` and `foldersById` for the folder's side), over the key's name and its tolerant readers in `shared/alsoIn.ts` (`ALSO_IN_KEY`, `alsoIn`, `alsoInEntries`), and `also_in` is one of `RESERVED_KEYS`. Like `id` it is never a DEFAULT column of a view with no saved order (`propertyKeys`, `client/src/views/engine.ts`); a view can still add it. Reading is tolerant: a scalar is ONE entry, anything that is not a note id is ignored, an id no folder has is ignored quietly, a folder with no `.folder.md` (or one with no id) has no shortcuts, living in a folder and naming it — or naming it twice — is one row, and of two folders still sharing an id the first in path order is the one named.

**Add**: "Add note shortcut" on a FOLDER row outside a 2+ selection (`MenuTargets.shortcutDir`, under the favorite toggle) opens `ShortcutPicker` — the search bar's own candidates, matcher and result list in the confirm sheet's shell: notes only, none the folder already shows; ↑/↓ clamp, ⏎ picks, Esc or a click away closes. The pick runs `addShortcut(dir, path)`: the folder's id is asked of the settings file's OWN bytes, never the index — a folder with no `.folder.md` gets one holding just its id, a file with no id is given one, and a file whose `id` is someone else's value is REFUSED with nothing written — then one `transformFile` appends the id to the note's `also_in`, beside every entry already there. The row and the count arrive with the index; only a refusal is said (the passive notice).

**Where it shows**: in the folder's views as a row like any other — a cell edit writes the ORIGINAL file — wearing `ShortcutIcon` beside its name (the skins learn which folder "here" is from the `ViewFolder` context). In the tree, under the folder among its own files in name order (`sidebar/folderCounts.ts` `folderCounts`' `shortcuts`), wearing the same mark: the row IS the note — it opens it, and the selected and active highlights follow its real path — but a click or right-click on it never joins the selection (what ⌘C / ⌘X / ⌘V act on, so the keyboard never cuts, copies or pastes at the real note from there), it never drags and is never the inline-rename seat. In the note's own properties panel `also_in` is a Reserved row read as folder NAMES.

**Remove** (🔒 E5): on a shortcut row "Remove shortcut" stands where Delete would (`MenuTargets.removeShortcut`) — not `danger`, no confirm, because nothing is destroyed. The row offers no Delete, Rename, Cut, Copy or Paste: the note is deleted and renamed where it lives. `removeShortcut` takes the folder's id out of `also_in`, and the key goes with its last entry.

**Counts** (🔒 E6): every folder row shows the number of notes it shows — every note under it at any depth plus its shortcuts, each once (`folderCounts`, off `rowsByFolder`, the lookup the view uses); a folder showing none shows nothing. Rebuilt once per index snapshot, and a snapshot that moved no count re-renders no tree level.

**Elsewhere**: a column delete strips the folder's value from a shortcut note like any other ("Folder settings": Delete column); a note's properties panel offers the folders it is a shortcut in under "Properties from" ("The properties panel"); a link column narrowed to a folder offers its shortcuts with the notes that live there ("Links": Links to folders); `yaseendocs links` lists the ids in `also_in` ("Note ids": The command line).

Files: `client/src/links/shortcuts.ts` (+ test), `client/src/links/reservedKeys.ts` (+ test), `client/src/sidebar/ShortcutPicker.tsx` (+ test), `client/src/sidebar/folderCounts.ts` (+ test), `client/src/sidebar/Tree.tsx`, `menuSections.ts` (+ test), `Sidebar.tsx` (+ `Sidebar.test.tsx`), `client/src/views/view/GroupHeader.tsx` (`ViewFolder`), `client/src/views/view/icons.tsx` (`ShortcutIcon`).

## The page title + rename confirm (YAZ-888 — LOCKED, ⚡ amending decision E of GRO-2096)

What every page shows above its body, and the one beat a rename now earns. The model is "Links": Rename (the pipeline, unchanged); this is the surface that names a page and the rule that guards renaming it.

**The title IS the file name** (🔒): `stripExt(basename(path))` — for a FOLDER (`PageTitle kind="dir"`, YAZ-2290 D9) the directory's WHOLE name, with no extension logic, and a commit renames the directory — never a frontmatter `title` — the whole link system resolves pages by NAME, and aliases already cover alternate display names (E2, GRO-2214). It is BLOCK ZERO of the note's own scroller (`.page-title`, above the properties panel and `.editor-mount`), so it scrolls with the note like every other block there, inside `.editor-instance`'s content column at the editor's own h1 scale. React-side chrome and NEVER a ProseMirror node: the note's markdown round-trips past it byte-identically, and an in-document `# Heading` stays a block of the note like any other — syncing the two is deliberately out of scope, as is a `title` property.

**ONE DOOR** (🔒): every rename gesture in the app — the sidebar's inline rename, its drag-move, and now the title — reaches `App` as `(oldPath, newPath, kind)`, and the rule is asked there and nowhere else (`requestRename` in front of `renameFile`, which stays the executor). THE RULE: a changed NAME asks first (`isNameChange` = the basenames differ), a MOVE runs silently exactly as it always has — a confirm on every drag would be hostile, and bare links keep resolving across a move anyway. Files and FOLDERS alike: a folder's name is a name. No surface reimplements this, which is why the sidebar needed no change to inherit it.

**The copy** (🔒 LOCKED verbatim, `renameConfirmMessage`, pure and separately tested like `deleteConfirmMessage`):

> Rename 'Old Note' to 'New Note'? Links in 3 notes will be updated.

singular at 1, and for a page nobody links to:

> Rename 'Old Note' to 'New Note'? No other notes link to it.

Names are PAGE names (basename minus extension) — the title's own spelling, not the file's. N is `countLinkReferences` over the window's OWN index snapshot: synchronous, no fetch, so the sheet opens in the same frame as the gesture, and it counts the very set the rewrite walks (settings-only references included, YAZ-864). Kind comes from the GESTURE — the tree row's type, or the surface the title sits on — never from the index or the extension: a folder may be named `Archive.json`, and a JSON file has no record. A folder's count also takes the links to the folder itself (YAZ-2290 D10). The rewrite re-reads a FRESH snapshot inside `renameFile`, so the copy promises what WILL be updated rather than an exact receipt. NO Obsidian-style 3-way: links ALWAYS update on confirm.

**The sheet** (`ConfirmRename`) is on `ConfirmSheet` like its siblings: our own sheet and never a native dialog, initial focus on CANCEL, Esc cancels, Enter confirms, click-away cancels, `dialog` + `aria-modal` labelled by its own text, Cancel / **Rename**, NOT `--danger` (nothing is destroyed), and no "Don't ask me again" (the gesture that silently rewrites N notes is exactly the one that earns a beat). It is rendered by App, not the sidebar, because the door is App's. ONE deliberate departure, load-bearing: the keys are bound to the OVERLAY, not to `window`. This is the first sheet a KEYSTROKE can open, and React flushes its effects inside that very keydown's dispatch — a window listener hears the Enter that opened the sheet and confirms before the sheet is read. The other half of that same hazard lives in the inputs: an Enter that commits MUST `preventDefault`, or the keystroke's default activation lands on the freshly focused Cancel button and cancels what it just asked for (both `RenameInline` and the title input do; the e2e caught this).

**Editing** copies `RenameInline`'s patterns, because a title edit IS a rename: click swaps the heading for an input prefilled with the current name, and LEAVING the field commits (YAZ-1553: click-away, Enter, ArrowDown and Cmd-Tab all reach the one `onBlur` door → `renamedPath(path, name, kind)` → App's door; a `settled` ref makes Chromium's last blur on removal inert), Esc is the only discard, ArrowDown then hands focus to the editor below (`focusEditor`, the same call the mount ends with). The sidebar's `RenameInline` follows the same three verbs (open/leave/discard).
