An empty/whitespace name never commits and neither does the unchanged one; a name `validateEntryName` rejects lands in the passive notice, exactly where `ALREADY_EXISTS` and every other failure already land — the title never grows an error slot of its own.

**Known + accepted (v1)**: committing a rename REMOUNTS the editor (`CrepeHost key={file.path}`); the dirty buffer survives through `takeRenameBuffer` (Rename, step 4), and the scroll position and caret reset. Do not "fix" this by keeping the mount alive across the path change.

Files: `client/src/editor/PageTitle.tsx` (+ `PageTitle.test.tsx`), `client/src/sidebar/ConfirmRename.tsx` (`renameConfirmMessage`, `isNameChange`, the sheet — its `kind` names a file or a folder through `lib/pageLabel.ts` `pageLabel`) + `ConfirmRename.test.tsx`, `client/src/App.tsx` (`requestRename` / `confirmRename` in front of `renameFile`, the sheet's one render site) + `App.test.tsx`, `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (block zero, ArrowDown), `client/src/views/FolderView.tsx` (a folder's title, `kind="dir"`), `client/src/sidebar/RenameInline.tsx` (the Enter `preventDefault`), `client/src/app.css` (`.page-title*`), `desktop/e2e/title.spec.ts`.

## The properties panel (YAZ-883, typed rows YAZ-884 — LOCKED)

What every page shows BETWEEN its title and its body: its own frontmatter — a note's own, and on a folder's page the folder's (`.folder.md`, **A folder's own panel** below). The model is the frontmatter primitives (`shared/frontmatter.ts`, Editor rule 1) and the write dance is `writeProperty`'s ("Cell editing"); this is the surface.

**TWO MODES, one surface** (🔒 ⚡ YAZ-884). TYPED ROWS are the DEFAULT expanded view: one row per top-level frontmatter key, each with the editor its type asks for. RAW YAML (⚡ YAZ-883) is the FALLBACK underneath them, reached by the panel's one mode control — "Edit as YAML" / "Edit as rows" — and it stays honest by staying literal. Every rule below headed VERBATIM / VALIDATE-BLOCKS-SAVE / *Editing* is the raw mode's; the typed rows' own rules follow them.

**Placement**: BLOCK ONE of the note's own scroller (`.frontmatter-panel`, between `.page-title` and `.editor-mount`), so it scrolls WITH the note like every other block there, in `.editor-instance`'s content column. React-side chrome and never a ProseMirror node, exactly like the title above it.

**VERBATIM** (🔒): a save writes the user's own bytes back through `replaceFrontmatter` — NEVER a parse→reformat. Comments, key order, quoting styles, blank lines and indentation all survive a round trip that only meant to change one value; the FRAME is the file's (opening fence, `---` vs `...` terminator, the line endings around them) and the body is untouched. One trailing newline is supplied when the text lacks it and never a second when it has one, so an unchanged interior round-trips to the identical string and the write is skipped — identity never touches disk, the same rule as the editor's autosave and `writeProperty`.

**VALIDATE-BLOCKS-SAVE** (🔒): what the panel DOES enforce is that the block still parses. `parseFrontmatter` runs over the candidate CONTENT (fences and all, never a hand-wrapped copy) and an `error` blocks the write outright, because a block that will not parse would corrupt every index that reads it (`shared/frontmatter.ts`'s header) and the user would find out through a page that silently lost its properties. Valid-yet-weird YAML saves untouched — the rule is "it parses", not "we like it".

**The write**: read the file FRESH (`api.readFile`), `replaceFrontmatter`, write with `expectedMtime`, and on `CONFLICT` re-read once and retry — the user's YAML wins over a concurrent edit, whose BODY is preserved because only the block is rewritten; a second conflict throws. The page's own open editor absorbs the resulting frontmatter-only `change` silently (`absorbFrontmatterOnly`, GRO-2186), so the panel talks to the editor not at all.

**Its own error surface** (🔒): bad YAML, a `FrontmatterWriteError`, a failed key write, a failed declaration and a failed bridge call all land on ONE inline line inside the body (`Not valid YAML: …` / `Could not save the properties: …` / `Could not delete "x": …` / `Could not set the type of "x": …`), never a dialog and never App's passive notice — that notice is for gestures with no place of their own to speak, and this one has one. A rejected save reverts nothing: the text stays there to fix. The one exception is a typed row's own optimistic failure, which `EditableCell` already shows in place as its "Save failed" chip (5B) — the panel does not double-report it.

**The header**: the backlinks / view-group collapsible idiom — one `<button aria-expanded>` with the chevron rotated −90° while collapsed — showing the chevron plus the `PropertiesIcon` glyph and the BARE top-level key count when the block parses to at least one (`4`; 0 or unparseable → no count, never a guessed one). The words live in the button's `title` and `aria-label` rather than on the chip (YAZ-1758): **Add properties** for a page with no frontmatter — the act rather than the noun, ONE control and not two — and **Properties** / **Properties (4)** otherwise. COLLAPSED by default and deliberately SUBORDINATE to the title above it (small, muted): most pages have nothing to say here. The no-frontmatter page opens on an empty row list carrying the same two acts every page's panel carries.

**Editing (raw mode)**: a monospace `<textarea>` holding the raw interior (`frontmatterInterior`, so the fences are the app's business and never the user's). Text differing from disk is DIRTY, which is the only thing that puts **Save** / **Cancel** on screen; Esc reverts to the disk text (the textarea owns the key while focused, so it never reaches the window's other listeners). Open state AND mode are session-only component state, never persisted — neither an open properties panel nor the mode it was left in is part of a note's identity (BacklinksSection's rule).

**The rows (YAZ-1417):** one row per top-level key in the block's own order, with a type icon, property-name button and shared `EditableCell` value. Clicking the name opens configuration; clicking the value opens its typed editor. Empty values have a quiet placeholder. The footer retains Add property and raw YAML access. Above the rows sits the views' own `ColumnSearch` (YAZ-1473) whenever the expanded typed view has at least one row: it filters rows by KEY — trimmed, case-insensitive substring, block order kept, Reserved/YAML-chipped rows included — while the header count stays the block's total and the footer stays put; no match shows "No properties found."; the query is session-only component state that resets when the panel collapses, when Add property starts and when the mode toggles, so a new row is never born hidden; typing never writes; raw mode, unparseable blocks and empty pages show no box.

**Folder context** (YAZ-1417, re-pointed by YAZ-2290): the panel subscribes to the window's link feed (`wikilinks.folders`) and lists two groups in ONE list (🔒 D19): the note's OWN fields — the top level of its frontmatter, without `in` — then a heading row, **Properties from <folder name>** (`li.frontmatter-property-context`), and under it the values of ONE folder at a time: that folder's block of `in` ("Folder settings": Values). The choices are the folders that show the note (`foldersShowing`, "Folders are the pages"): the folder it lives in, each folder above it nearest first, then each folder it is a shortcut in with the folders above that one; a top-level note's living folder is the vault root, by the vault folder's name, and the root is no choice otherwise. One choice is plain text; more are a `<select>` the words label, each choice by its folder's name, or by its root-relative path when another choice has that name. Chosen first: the first in that order whose `.folder.md` SAVED settings (`hasFolderSettings`), else the folder the note lives in, with its default columns. The pick is component state — never written, chosen again by the rule on a remount, and the panel is mounted per note — and picking writes nothing. A folder row is typed by that folder's declaration, then legacy vault declarations, then the value, then text, through the one `columnTyping`/`cellEditor` ladder; one of the note's own fields starts at the second rung — a folder never types it. The chosen folder's columns the note holds NO value for are listed as EMPTY rows (🔒 E1), a value in its block for a column it no longer has is a plain row there (editable, removable), and a field reaches the note only when one is filled in — a column is never stamped into a note. Every edit goes where its row lives: a folder row's edit, removal and — while a folder is chosen — Add property write that folder's block (`writeFolderValues`; a folder with no id is given one first, `folderId`), one of the note's own rows the top level. The header's count is the fields the note holds: its own plus its values for the chosen folder. Human-readable existing keys such as `Status` can carry local definitions. "Edit property" — a folder row's — saves the definition to the CHOSEN folder (`writeFolderColumn` on that folder's settings file) and its popup says `In <folder name>`; there is no shared Status vocabulary. A table cell is not this panel: it is typed by its own folder's columns ("Folder settings": Typing is per folder). With no feed, or for a note outside the vault, there is no folder: the note's own rows alone, and Add property adds at the top level. A folder's own panel is unchanged — its properties are the top level of its `.folder.md`.

**A folder's own panel** (🔒 D9, YAZ-2290): on a folder's page the SAME component is mounted on `<folder>/.folder.md` itself. Its properties are facts about the FOLDER: the columns it declares for its notes neither type nor list here, `folder_settings` is no row and is left out of the count (it is edited through the views), and the other reserved keys show their chip as on a note. Its first write — a typed row, an add or a raw Save — creates the file (`readForWrite`); a raw block that will not parse creates nothing.

**A row with no editor offers nothing but its chip** (🔒): the app's RESERVED keys — `comments` (YAZ-1472), `id` (YAZ-2293), `reviews` (YAZ-2322), `also_in` (YAZ-2290 D2) and `in` (D19), spelled from `COMMENTS_KEY` / `NOTE_ID_KEY` / `REVIEWS_KEY` / `ALSO_IN_KEY` / `FOLDER_VALUES_KEY` (each exported by the one module that owns the string) and never a local literal, gathered since YAZ-1513 into `links/reservedKeys.ts` `RESERVED_KEYS` (the one list this panel and the views' column delete both read — "Folder settings: `.folder.md`": Delete column) — render read-only with a **Reserved** chip, because each already has its own door (the Comments block, the Reviews section, a folder row's "Add note shortcut") and a second one would be a second gesture to keep in step — `id` alone has no door at all: it is the note's permanent identity, and editing it would orphan every link to the note ("Note ids"). `also_in` reads as the folders' NAMES, an entry no folder has as written. `in` alone is no row at all: its blocks ARE the folder rows, and raw mode shows it as written. Add property refuses a reserved name. `folder_settings` is NOT one of them (YAZ-2318): the key is the app's only in a folder's own `.folder.md`, whose panel hides its row, leaves it out of the count and refuses the name in Add property (`FOLDER_SETTINGS_KEY`, the door being the folder's views); on an ordinary note it is an ordinary property. Values no editor can hold without LYING about them — nested maps, multi-line scalars, and lists carrying either — render read-only with a **YAML** chip pointing at raw mode. Neither kind offers the type control or the delete; a lossy editor is never the answer, and the fallback underneath is.

**Per-key writes are surgical** (🔒): a row's edit, an add and a delete all go through `writeProperty(path, key, value)` — the one-key writer, reused whole and never duplicated — so the rest of the block stays byte-identical, comments included; `undefined` deletes the key. A folder row goes through `writeFolderValues(path, <folder id>, [{ key, value }])` instead, the same guarded transform over that folder's block, which rewrites `in` and no other key. A DELETE takes the deleted key's own leading comment with it: a comment sitting directly above a property describes that property, and `setFrontmatterProperty` treats it as part of the pair. The panel then applies the SAME one-key transform to its own copy of the file, so the raw fallback can never show a block a typed edit already left behind and a later raw Save cannot silently revert it. **Add** takes a name and a value: the name is checked against `PROPERTY_NAME` and reported in the registry's OWN words (`property names are snake_case (…)`), a duplicate is refused outright, and the first value is shaped by the kind the key will be read back at (a declared `number` gets a number, a declared `list` a one-item list) — the registry is the authority, not the typing.

**Editing a definition (YAZ-1417):** both note and view property settings use the shared definition editor and explicit Save/Cancel. `writeFolderColumn(path, key, next, base)` reads fresh bytes and modifies only that column inside the settings map, preserving other columns, views and unknown metadata. Absent settings start from the default columns (🔒 E2, YAZ-2290 — the first write states them) and inject no views. The captured base is compared against current known definition fields; stale same-column changes are rejected with a reopen message. The check repeats after an mtime conflict. A definition edit never bulk-renames or rewrites note values.

**Select options and ordering (YAZ-1417):** `select` stores a scalar and `multi-select` a list. `PropertyDecl.options` stores exact labels in manual order; new options start empty. Optional `optionSort` is `manual`, `ascending` or `descending`, absent meaning Manual. One shared helper computes natural, case-insensitive display order without mutating the manual array. Settings, value/filter pickers and default Board groups consume that order. Alphabetical modes hide drag grips; Manual supports keyboard and full-row dragging with a stable insertion boundary. Unknown stored values remain visible. Renaming/removing an option changes metadata only, as the UI explains.

**Value persistence (YAZ-1417):** Select uses an anchored searchable popup; Multi-select saves each choice and stays open. Per-cell writes serialize so slower earlier requests cannot overwrite the final selection. Optimistic state survives intermediate index updates while writes are pending; failed writes revert the picker to the supplied durable value and show the error. Property and value popovers are portaled outside document zoom; anchored popovers refit as content size changes.

**The dirty draft never loses** (🔒): switching modes asks NOTHING and discards nothing, because the toggle back to typed rows is DISABLED while an unsaved raw draft stands (its tooltip says why — "Save or cancel your YAML edits first"). The simplest honest rule: no dialog, no silent discard. Typed writes land immediately, so typed → raw is always free. A block that will not PARSE has no rows to show, so raw is the whole surface and the toggle is not rendered at all.

**Live truth** (⚡ amended by YAZ-1356): the panel's disk text is derived from the `file.content` prop — since YAZ-1356 CrepeHost's own `disk` state, the whole-file bytes last seen on disk, fed by the watcher path in BOTH the frontmatter-only absorb and the body reload, because `useFile`'s content is frozen at mount and a fresh `file` would remount Crepe — re-derived whenever that prop moves unless a dirty draft is standing (typed text is never thrown away by a load), and replaced by what it WROTE after its own save — compared against the prop it last saw, so its own write never reads as an external change and bounces back. KNOWN + ACCEPTED (v1): `useFile` loads a path ONCE and CrepeHost handles external changes without touching that object, so a frontmatter change made ELSEWHERE reaches the panel on the next open of the page, not the same instant. The panel is already written to follow the prop the moment it becomes live; do not "fix" this by giving the panel a watcher or a read of its own.

Files: `client/src/editor/FrontmatterPanel.tsx` (+ `FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx`), `shared/frontmatter.ts` (`replaceFrontmatter`, `frontmatterInterior`, `setFrontmatterProperty`) with `client/src/views/frontmatterWrite.test.ts`, `client/src/views/writeProperty.ts` (the one-key write), `client/src/views/editorType.ts` (the one ladder), `client/src/views/view/EditableCell.tsx` (the shared typed editors + the `onCommit` seam the panel writes through), `client/src/views/folderSettings.ts` (`FOLDER_SETTINGS_KEY`, the row a folder's own panel hides), `client/src/links/reservedKeys.ts` (`RESERVED_KEYS`) and `shared/comments.ts` (`COMMENTS_KEY`, exported for the RESERVED list — YAZ-1472), `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (block one, and the `root` / `properties` / `wikilinks` it threads in), `client/src/app.css` (`.frontmatter-panel*`), `desktop/e2e/properties.spec.ts`.

## Comments (YAZ-1472 — decisions D1-D17 of its scoping, LOCKED)

What every page carries UNDER its body: its own comment stream, Linear's shape inside the note's own scroller. The model is `shared/comments.ts` (whose header doc is the spec), the write dance is `transformFile`'s ("Cell editing"), the surface is `client/src/comments/`; this section is the rules.

**Storage IS the note** (🔒 D1/D2): ONE reserved frontmatter key, `comments`, holding a YAML list of maps — so a comment travels with the note through every sync, rename, copy and Obsidian, and a vault carries no second file to keep in step. A sidecar and a body section were both REJECTED: the sidecar would be the one thing in a vault the note itself does not carry, and a body section would be text the editor could reflow, fold and re-serialise. The list is FLAT: a reply carries `reply_to: <id>` naming its TOP-LEVEL parent, a thread is exactly ONE level deep, and a reply to a reply is filed under the root (`addComment` re-roots it, and a hand-nested one is read the same way) — deeper nesting is what makes comment trees unreadable, and Linear stops at one level too. The spelling lives in ONE place, `COMMENTS_KEY` in `shared/comments.ts`: the block, the index scan and the properties panel all import it, and there is no local literal anywhere.

**The schema** (🔒 D11 — key order as written on disk): `id` (8 hex, `crypto.randomUUID().slice(0, 8)` — enough for one note; a hand-written id that is all digits must be QUOTED, or YAML reads a number and the entry is skipped — the app's writer quotes it), `n` (🔒 D15 — the comment's NUMBER, worn like an id: a top-level comment is one past the highest top-level `n` on the page, shown `#3`; a reply is one past the highest among its parent's replies, shown `#3.1`; handed out once on create and NEVER reassigned — a delete leaves a gap, the way an issue tracker does, so "see comment #3" written anywhere keeps pointing at the same comment; absent on a hand-written entry, which shows a dot), `at` (ISO UTC at SECONDS precision, `2026-09-11T18:22:31Z`, so it sorts as a plain string), `reply_to?` (the top-level parent's id, never a reply's), `by?` (WHO wrote it, declared by the WRITER and never inferred — 🔒 D3: the app writes NO `by` for the person at the keyboard, because a file carries no authorship and a guessed one would be a lie; an agent editing the file writes `by: agent` — and the `yaseendocs` command writes it for its caller, `agent` unless `--by` says otherwise, YAZ-1617 D5), `title?` (a one-line heading; blank means no key at all — a title is a key only when it says something), `edited?` (stamped by `editComment`, same format as `at`), `body` LAST (plain text, trailing whitespace trimmed on write and nothing else; a multi-line body serialises as a block scalar). `addComment` writes `id, n, at, reply_to, by, title, body` — `by` only when the writer declares one (the command does; the UI never); `editComment` strips and re-appends `title`, `edited`, `body` so they land last whether it is the first stamp or a re-stamp, and every other key — `by` included — rides through in its place. **Carry what you do not understand** (🔒): unknown keys on an entry and unknown entries in the list are spread through EVERY write byte-for-byte, never rebuilt from the typed subset — the list is the user's (and an agent's) data, and a writer that only knows its own fields must not eat theirs. Deleting the LAST comment deletes the KEY (🔒 D7 — never a `comments: []` left behind: a key with nothing to say is no key), and deleting a comment takes every reply to it in the same write. On screen the order is CHRONOLOGICAL by `at`, oldest first, stable for equal stamps — or newest-first by the ROOT's `at` when `SettingsState.commentsOrder` is `newest` (YAZ-1515: a global reading preference, one state-driven **Oldest first** / **Newest first** toggle left of fold-all, hidden below two threads; replies inside a thread always read oldest-first; the composer stays at the BOTTOM of the block under both orders — Yasin's call on the demo; under newest-first a new comment therefore lands at the top of the list, away from the box); the FILE is never reordered — a write appends or edits in place, so a hand-arranged list keeps its arrangement and a diff shows one entry.

**Placement** (🔒 D4): a block of the Markdown host's scroller — `.page-title`, `.frontmatter-panel`, `.editor-mount`, then `.comments`, then "Linked mentions" (Editor rule 25), then "Reviews" (YAZ-2322) — so it scrolls WITH the note, inside `.editor-instance`'s content column (backlinks.css's rule, byte for byte), never a panel and never a pane. The blocks stack flush: none of them carries a bottom padding, the scroller (`.editor-host`) owns the page's one 64px tail (YAZ-1680 — before that each block carried 64px, so every pair sat 64px apart). Unlike the backlinks it is ALWAYS rendered: the composer is the door to the first comment, and a block that appeared only once something was in it would have no way in. It opens EXPANDED on a page that has comments and COLLAPSED on one that has none (🔒 E, YAZ-1515 — decided once at mount, `readComments(file.content).length > 0`; a `foreign` / `invalid` block has nothing to read and starts collapsed too), so on an empty page the header is the door to the composer; nothing follows the count afterwards. It is a VIEW over the same disk truth the properties panel reads — `file.content` is CrepeHost's `disk` state (YAZ-1356), re-derived whenever that prop moves — with no watcher of its own, no read of its own and no IPC (the panel's "Live truth" rule). The header's collapse and every fold are SESSION chrome, never persisted — an open comment stream is not part of a note's identity (BacklinksSection's rule).

**A folder's comments** (🔒 D9, YAZ-2290): a folder's page carries the same stream under its views — the same component, on the bytes of `<folder>/.folder.md`, stored under that file's own `comments` key. A folder with no settings file shows the empty stream, and its first comment creates the file (`readForWrite`); from the command line, the first `comment` on a `.folder.md` path creates it too (`transformOnDisk(…, create)`), and `comments` on one not written yet prints `no comments on <path>` (`[]` with `--json`) and writes nothing — a folder that does not exist is still not found.

**Writes** (🔒 D8): every mutation — add, reply, edit, delete — is exactly ONE `transformFile(path, fresh => …)` ("Cell editing"): the change is computed on the FRESH bytes, lands with `expectedMtime`, and on `CONFLICT` re-reads and recomputes once (a second conflict throws) — so another window, an agent appending and a cell edit from a folder's view can all interleave with a comment and nobody's entry is lost. `transformFile` returns `{ mtime, content }` since YAZ-1472 (the transformed bytes, or the fresh read when the transform was a no-op) and the block ADOPTS exactly what landed — never an optimistic copy of its own — holding it against the `file.content` prop it last saw, so its own write never reads as an external change. The watcher echo is frontmatter-only, so it lands through `absorbFrontmatterOnly` → `setDisk` (GRO-2186): an open, DIRTY note never sees a conflict bar from a comment, its unsaved body edits and caret survive, and the block recognises the echo as the bytes it already holds. A failed write is ONE line at the bottom of the block (`Could not save the comment: <message>`) — never a dialog, never App's passive notice — and the draft stays in the box to retry.

**Shapes** (🔒 D10, `commentsShape`): what the key holds is one of FOUR — `absent` (no key, or a bare `comments:`), `list` (a YAML sequence, empty included), `foreign` (a value of the user's OWN under the name — an Obsidian vault may already use `comments` for a scalar or a map), `invalid` (the whole block does not parse). Only `absent` and `list` are OURS to write: `foreign` and `invalid` make EVERY mutation throw `CommentsShapeError` BEFORE any write; the block shows the header and one muted line saying which (`This note's comments property isn't a comment list, so nothing can be added here.` / `The properties block doesn't parse. Fix it in Properties to comment here.`) and no composer — nothing is ever overwritten, because a comment feature that could eat a user's own property would be worse than none. Within a list, MALFORMED entries (not a map, or missing a string `id`, `at` or `body`) are kept on write and skipped on screen; an EMPTY-string body is a comment and still renders; an ORPHAN reply (its parent gone) shows at top level in time order; a hand-made `reply_to` CYCLE never loops — its earliest member stands in as the root; an `at` that will not parse shows as written rather than as "just now".

**Bodies are Markdown, rendered read-only, sanitised** (🔒 D9, `comments/markdown.ts`): GitHub-flavoured — tables, task lists, strikethrough, fenced code, and single newlines are line breaks, the way a typed comment reads. `marked` (gfm, breaks) → DOMPurify (the `html` profile, with `style` / `form` / `input` / `button` / `select` / `textarea` forbidden on top — NO form control survives, a task box is drawn as a glyph, ☑ / ☐) → `innerHTML`, so a body — yours or an agent's — can never run script or reach outside its box. A link in a body opens through `shell.openLink` like the editor's own (one delegated click handler on the block) — never by navigating the app window. Both libraries were ALREADY in the tree through the drawing engine and are now declared DIRECT dependencies of `client/` (a transitive dependency is not a dependency). This is the ONLY place the app turns Markdown into HTML rather than into a ProseMirror document — a second renderer would be a second set of rules to keep in step. The composer is a plain `<textarea>`: what you type is what the file holds.

**The UI** (🔒 D12 — the parts that are contracts): the header is the properties panel's control (chevron, muted label, plain count) at the backlinks header's size, with NO hairline above it (YAZ-1675 — the body flows straight into the header; Linked mentions lost its line the same way in YAZ-1680 — no block under a note draws one) — chevron + **Comments (N)**, N counting every comment and reply, absent at 0, collapsing the rest — with the tools grouped at its right (`comments__tools`, one `margin-left: auto`): FIRST the order toggle (YAZ-1515 — `Oldest first` / `Newest first`, the CURRENT order as its label, the action as its tooltip, shown only with two or more threads), then ONE fold-all button, state-driven the way the views' groups toggle is: **Collapse all** while anything is open, **Expand all** once everything is folded (comments AND reply groups, together). ONE card per thread (on `--bg` — white in the light theme, so dark mode follows): the parent on top, an **N replies** fold, the replies one level in behind a guide line (the outline's, hung under the parent's dot), and a **Reply…** row at the bottom of a threaded card that is ONE line until focused (Linear's row); a comment with no replies yet offers **Reply** on hover instead. The header text NEVER moves (🔒 D16) and, since ⚡ YAZ-1516, NEVER clips — a long title or first line WRAPS and the number, stamp, writer and actions stay on its first line: a titled comment keeps its TITLE in the header row and folds its body under it (chevron, `aria-expanded`); a title-less ONE-LINER is its own header row — it wraps, has no chevron, nothing below, nothing to fold, and keeps the fold's seat so the numbers line up; a title-less multi-line comment keeps its FIRST line (Markdown marker stripped, `commentSplit`, its inline Markdown rendered by `commentInlineHtml` — same renderer, same sanitiser) in the header permanently and folds only the rest. Fold-all counts only what can fold, and is not shown when nothing can. The stamp is relative (`lib/relativeTime`, a minute's resolution) with the absolute time in its tooltip; `(edited)` follows it, and the WRITER shows as `(agent)` after the time in that same quiet voice, because the mark already says it: the avatar's seat holds the comment's NUMBER (`#3`, a reply `#3.1`, a dot when there is none), indigo-soft for you and violet on the `--agent` / `--agent-soft` tokens for a declared writer (a voice that is not yours, deliberately never the accent) — no icons. ⌘Enter submits and Esc cancels — or clears a draft that has no Cancel — with the textarea owning Esc while focused, the properties panel's way. Delete ASKS first (🔒 D17): the app's own sheet, a mirror of the sidebar's `ConfirmDelete` — focus on Cancel, Esc cancels, Enter confirms, click-away cancels, red Delete — and NO "Don't ask me again", because a comment has no Trash behind it and the sheet is the only undo; the copy names the number and, on a parent, the replies that go with it. Copy, LOCKED: **Comments**, `Leave a comment…`, `Reply…`, `Title (optional)`, **Comment** / **Reply** / **Save** / **Cancel**, `⌘ Enter`.

**Never a property** (🔒 D5/D6): `scanFile` DELETES `properties[COMMENTS_KEY]` right after `parseFrontmatter` — comments are CONTENT, not a property — so the key is never in the index, never in its cache, never a view column, never a link source (a `[[link]]` inside a comment is text, not a mention, and never a backlink). `CACHE_VERSION` 2 → **3** (the GRO-2230 rule: the extraction semantics moved, so every old snapshot degrades to one rescan, and the fingerprint pin in `cache.test.ts` moved with it). In the properties panel `comments` is one of the RESERVED keys ("The properties panel") — chipped, read-only, no type control, no delete — spelled from `COMMENTS_KEY`, because it has its own door (the block below the note) and a second one would be a second gesture to keep in step; raw mode still shows the bytes, as it does for every key.

**Testing** (🔒 D13): unit tests — `client/src/comments/*.test.ts(x)` (the model's contract lives at `client/src/comments/comments.test.ts` so vitest's client project collects it), `scan.test.ts` / `cache.test.ts` (the drop and the version pin) and `FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx` (the chip) — plus typecheck and a pointed real-app pass on an isolated profile. Since YAZ-2171 one pointed Playwright spec, `desktop/e2e/comments.spec.ts`, walks add → reply → fold → delete against the note's frontmatter; the unit suites stay the default evidence.

**The door** (🔒 YAZ-1617 D1–D5): an agent leaves a comment through `yaseendocs` — the app's own Electron run as plain Node (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, VS Code's `code` pattern): a shell shim at `Contents/Resources/bin/yaseendocs` runs `app.asar/out/main/cli.js`, a second main entry that never imports `electron`. Never the app window, never the single-instance lock, no Node install, no checkout. It writes through the SAME `addComment` / `editComment` / `deleteComment`, guarded the SAME way as D8 on node fs (`transformOnDisk`: fresh read, `expectedMtime`, one retry on CONFLICT, a second conflict refuses). Six verbs — four for comments, `comment`, `comments [--json]`, `edit`, `delete`, and two for a page's id and the ids it points at, `id` and `links [--json]` (YAZ-2293 — "Note ids": The command line) — a comment is NAMED the way the app shows it, `#3` / `#3.1` (or bare `3`), or by its id, and a name that matches nothing is REFUSED with nothing written (🔒 YAZ-1627: the model forgives an unknown `reply_to` in a hand-written file; the command never creates one) — and `--help` IS the contract: the only documentation an agent reads, so its wording is UI copy. `comment` writes `by: agent` unless `--by` says otherwise (choosing the tool IS the declaration; D3 stands — the UI still writes no `by`); `edit` and `delete` REFUSE a comment with no `by` — a person's comment is the app's alone (D17 asks first there; a script cannot ask). Exit codes 0 done · 1 refused or failed · 2 usage. **The handshake** (🔒 D2): right-click a PAGE — a Markdown file or, since YAZ-2290 D9, a FOLDER; its sidebar row in either lens, or its tab — › **Copy for Agent** puts three lines on the clipboard (`shared/agentInstructions.ts` `agentPrompt`; for a folder the path handed out is `<folder>/.folder.md`, `lib/copyForAgent.ts` `agentPage`): the page's path, that the app has a command line for its pages, and the command's full path with `--help`. It names NO verb, ever: `--help` owns capabilities, so a verb added later changes nothing here. Main composes it (`shell:agent-prompt`, reveal's read-only posture: `NOT_FOUND` for a vanished page, `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION` for a non-Markdown file; a folder's settings file exists only after its first change, so for a `.folder.md` path it is the FOLDER that must exist) because only main knows where the command lives — the quoted shim path when packaged, `node "<out/main>/cli.js"` in dev; the renderer writes the clipboard exactly as Copy path does (`client/src/lib/copyForAgent.ts`, one helper for both surfaces) and the notice says **Copied for agent**. Rejected and NOT to be added back: a PATH installer (🔒 D3 — the path travels with the text; on Apple Silicon `/usr/local/bin` means admin prompts for no gain), per-vault `AGENTS.md` (loads only when the agent STARTS inside the vault), writing into global agent files (imprinting config unasked), vault detection (the handshake states the fact; the command works on the file), the `yaseendocs://comment` deep link (no receipt; a link anywhere could write).

Files: `shared/comments.ts` (the model, `COMMENTS_KEY`), `shared/agentInstructions.ts` (the handshake), `desktop/src/cli/cli.ts` + `index.ts` + `cli.test.ts` (the command), `desktop/build/bin/yaseendocs` (the shim, mode 755), `desktop/src/main/ipc/agent.ts` (`shell:agent-prompt`), `client/src/lib/copyForAgent.ts`, `client/src/comments/CommentsSection.tsx` + `comments.css` (the block), `client/src/comments/markdown.ts` (`commentHtml` / `commentSummary`), `client/src/comments/comments.test.ts`, `client/src/views/writeProperty.ts` (`transformFile`'s `{ mtime, content }` return), `client/src/hooks/useAutosave.ts` (`absorbFrontmatterOnly`, unchanged — relied on), `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (the mount, between the Crepe host and "Linked mentions") and `client/src/views/FolderView.tsx` (a folder's stream, under its views), `client/src/editor/FrontmatterPanel.tsx` (the RESERVED entry), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts` (the drop) + `cache.ts` (`CACHE_VERSION` 3), `client/src/app.css` (`--agent` / `--agent-soft`), `client/src/lib/relativeTime.ts`, `client/package.json` (`marked`, `dompurify`).

## Review (YAZ-2322 — decisions D1-D9 and C of its scoping, LOCKED)

Upkeep review: a note that has gone unchanged and unchecked long enough comes up in an Inbox, to be confirmed as still relevant or fixed. It is NOT a memory algorithm — there is no front and back, no grading of recall (🔒 D3, D5). The model is `shared/reviews.ts` and `shared/schedule.ts` (whose header docs are the spec), the app's state is `client/src/review/`; the decision record and its scenario tables are the comments on YAZ-2322. This section is the rules.

**Storage IS the note** (🔒 D1): ONE reserved frontmatter key, `reviews`, holding a flat list with one line per review — `- {at: 2026-10-04T14:02:11Z, rating: keep, text: 9f3a1c2e}`. `at` is ISO UTC at seconds precision, `rating` is `keep` (the one answer v1 writes, 🔒 D4), `text` is the fingerprint of the body the review was written into. It travels with the file, and sync conflicts only when the same note is reviewed on two devices between syncs. A vault-wide log and a sidecar were REJECTED. Like `comments`, every write carries what it does not understand (unknown keys on an entry, unknown entries), a `reviews` value that is not a list is the user's own and is never overwritten, and an emptied list deletes the key.

**The file stores what happened; the date is computed** (🔒 D2): nothing writes a due date anywhere. `dueAt` in `shared/schedule.ts` is the ONLY place one is computed, from the record's `mtime`, `reviews` and `text` and the vault's settings — so an edit made by anything, on any device, moves the date with no extra write, and a changed setting moves every note at once. The cost, accepted: the due date is not a property or a view column, and an agent reads it through `yaseendocs due`.

**The rule** (🔒, v1): a note never reviewed, or whose body changed since its last review, is due `baseDays` after the file last changed. Otherwise it is due after its last review by `baseDays × growth^n`, capped at `maxDays`, where n is the reviews in a row that saw the body as it is now. With the defaults: 30 days untouched, then 60, 120, 240, 365. "Due now" is anything due before local midnight tonight. Smarter signals are a Future issue (YAZ-2372); they change `dueAt` and no file.

**"Changed" is the body, by fingerprint** (🔒 C): `textFingerprint` is FNV-1a over the body with line endings normalised and outer whitespace trimmed, eight hex characters, the same in the renderer and the main process. A reviewed note whose body still matches its last review is unmoved by comments, properties or the log itself. A note with NO matching review has only the file's modified time for a clock, so any write to it restarts the base wait — the one place the modified time decides.

**Who is in review** (🔒 D6): with upkeep on (🔒 D7, below), every Markdown note, until turned off. `review: false` takes one out, `review: true` puts one in whatever the vault default; any other value is ignored. `setInReview` writes the flag only when it differs from the vault default and never touches the log. Never in review: a folder's `.folder.md` (it is not among the index's `records`), a file too large to read (no `text`), a note whose frontmatter does not parse (a review could not be written into it).

**The index carries it**: `IndexRecord.reviews?` (the log as `at` / `rating` / `text`, time order; absent when never reviewed) and `IndexRecord.text?` (the body fingerprint; absent when the file was not read). `scanFile` lifts `reviews` off `properties` exactly as it drops `comments`, so the key is never a property or a column. `reviews` is in `RESERVED_KEYS`; `review` is an ordinary true/false property, the user's to edit.

**Settings are the vault's** (🔒 D7): `<root>/.yaseendocs/review.json` — `{ algorithm: "upkeep", enabled: false, baseDays: 30, growth: 2, maxDays: 365, reviewByDefault: true }` — through the generic `vaultConfig` door, sanitised field by field (`sanitizeReviewSettings`), so every device computes the same dates. No file means the defaults; nothing is created until a setting is changed. A file that is not valid JSON (a hand edit, a file half synced) changes nothing: the window keeps the settings it has, an open review stays open, and a save is refused with a notice, never written over it. `algorithm` has one value: it is in the file so a second algorithm is a new value, not a new shape. App owns the one `useReviewSettings`; the Settings dialog's Review section edits it.

**Upkeep is off until a vault turns it on** (🔒 D7): `enabled` is false with no file, with no `enabled` in it, and with one that is not true or false. ONE gate: `isInReview` answers false for every note, so the queue is empty, nothing is due, and `yaseendocs due` says so. Off, the app shows none of it — no Inbox row, no "Review this folder", no "Turn review on / off" (`useReview`'s `inReview` is null for every path), no Reviews section (App hands the editor no settings), and the Settings dialog's Review section is the one switch, "Enable upkeep review" (its other rows are `available` only while it is on). An open review closes when upkeep turns off. Nothing is written either way: a note's `reviews` and `review` stay as they are, `reviews` stays reserved, and the index carries the log and the fingerprint whatever the setting, so turning upkeep back on finds every note where it was.

**The Inbox and a session** (🔒 D8, D9): `useReview` (App owns one) gives the due count — the Inbox row above the lens tabs, there while upkeep is on — and one session per window. A session takes its queue ONCE at start, most overdue first, for the vault or for one folder and its subfolders ("Review this folder"). It REPLACES the main pane and is not a tab: tabs, their history and the right panel are untouched underneath. The page shown is the real editor. A note is checked against the live index only when its turn comes (gone, turned off or no longer due, it is passed over) and never while it is showing, because editing the page it shows is what makes it no longer due. "Still relevant" (⌘⇧⏎) appends one review through `transformFile`; Skip (⌘⇧S) writes nothing and sends the note to the end; Undo removes the review just written. Leaving: Esc, the bar's ✕, the Inbox row again, or ⌘W close the review and nothing else (⌘W never closes the tab hidden under it); going to a tab — a sidebar click, ⌃Tab — ends it and shows that tab; a link clicked in the note opens in a background tab and the review stays. One editor per note: a note already open in a tab shows through that tab's own layer, and one open in the side panel is reviewed there.

**Seeing it** (with upkeep on): the Reviews section follows "Linked mentions" on a note's page — the next check, then one row per review with a delete, and "Reset review history". A note's row and tab menus turn its review off or on.

**For agents**: `yaseendocs due <page.md>` prints the day a page is next due; given a folder it lists the pages due now under it; `--json` for the raw shape. It runs the app's own scanner and `shared/schedule.ts`, so it answers what the app shows.

Files: `shared/reviews.ts`, `shared/schedule.ts`, `client/src/review/` (`useReview`, `useReviewSettings`, the bars, the Reviews section), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts`, `desktop/src/cli/cli.ts`. Tests: `client/src/review/*.test.*`, `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.test.ts`, `desktop/src/cli/cli.test.ts`.

## The sidebar lenses (YAZ-847, YAZ-1766)

Two readings of one vault, switched by the tabs at the top of the sidebar: **Files**, the folder tree on disk — the only hierarchy there is — and **♥ Favorites**. Both are the same `Tree` component, the same `.tree__row` family and the same context menu.

**The chrome stack** (🔒 D4/D5, YAZ-847): above the lens row sits the Inbox row while the vault has upkeep on ("Review"). ROW 1 is the lens tablist — **Files ⇄ ♥ Favorites** (a `HeartIcon` glyph since YAZ-1766 D1, its word only in `title` + `aria-label`), rendered in `SIDEBAR_LENSES` order — carrying at its right end Focus Mode's eye and the expand/collapse-all button. ROW 2 is the persistent search bar of YAZ-801 (🔒 YAZ-797): ALWAYS visible, on every lens, loading / error / empty vault included. The tabs carry `role="tab"` + `aria-selected` and nothing else — no `aria-controls`, no `tabpanel` — because the body below is SHARED with the flat search results and belongs to neither lens while a query is typed.

**The lens is App's, and it is WINDOW IDENTITY** (🔒 D4, amended by YAZ-1628): one value, `WindowEntry.sidebarLens`, beside per-window `sidebarCollapsed`, so two windows on one vault browse on different lenses. It lives in App rather than in the Sidebar because the Sidebar is mounted `key={root}` and only while it is open. `changeLens` writes through `window.setIdentity` (anything but the two lenses → `BAD_REQUEST`) and then mirrors locally; another window's switch never reaches this one. A new, first or deep-link window starts on `DEFAULT_SIDEBAR_LENS` (Files, YAZ-1846), a duplicate inherits and then diverges, and a root CHANGE lands on Files (🔒 D2, YAZ-1846). On load, a window entry with no valid lens of its own takes the file's retired global `sidebarLens` when that is valid, else the default.

**Search across the lenses** (🔒 D5): a typed query replaces the ACTIVE tab's body, the tabs row stays visible and clickable, and switching lenses mid-search keeps the query. Everything else about the bar is the search contract under the `sidebar/` entry in "Repo layout".

**Plain DOM, no virtualization** (🔒 D6): every row of an expanded directory is mounted. `TableView`'s windowing is deliberately not taken here — a tree's mounted set is bounded by what the USER opened.

**Focus Mode** (YAZ-1605, every lens): right-click a folder row — or a shift-selection holding it — and "Focus on folder" / "Focus on N folders" narrows that lens to those rows. The focus is a per-lens path LIST in the WINDOW's identity (`WindowEntry.focusDirs` / `focusFavorites`, per window since YAZ-1628, the second since YAZ-1766 D5 — `sidebarCollapsed`'s rule, so two windows on one vault focus independently; empty = off, uncapped; ⌘⇧N inherits both lists by value, a root change clears them, `renamePath` / `removePath` repair them through the windows map like `tabs`), restored on mount through `window.identity` and written back idempotently through `window.setIdentity` — never the per-vault bucket, so another window's state broadcast cannot move it. FILES: `focusRoots(tree, focus)` in `treeState.ts` draws the focused dirs as the TOP rows in tree order, OUTERMOST only (a focused dir inside a focused dir draws once, under its parent); `Tree.tsx` just gets those nodes. FAVORITES: `favoriteRoots(tree, focusFavorites)` draws the focused favorited dirs as the tab's rows in the focus list's order, and reorder is inert meanwhile. SHARED, in `Sidebar.tsx`: `MenuTargets.focusPaths` is its OWN field (GRO-2296), computed by `focusable()` — the DIRS out of the right-clicked row or, inside a 2+ selection that holds it, the ordered selection (files in a selection are simply not focusable); `focusOn(paths, lens)` REPLACES the list of the lens the menu pinned (multi never stacks) and opens each row — a search row's menu pins FILES, so Focus from search lands in the Files lens (YAZ-2050). The eye (`.sidebar__focus-off`, accent, aria-label "Exit focus mode") renders ONLY while the ACTIVE lens is focused, one slot left of the chevrons, gone with them while a query is typed, and one click empties that lens' list; the expand/collapse-all button measures and acts on the dirs ON SCREEN (`bodyDirs`: the focused Files subtrees, or the favorites tree's dirs), so folds outside a focus are exactly as they were when it ends; a focused dir that leaves the tree (deleted, moved out) is PRUNED from the list in an effect — the last one leaving ends the focus, never an empty tree under a lit eye; a Files reveal whose target is outside every focused dir exits the focus first. Search stays global; blank-space creates still land in the vault root. Not in v1: a shortcut, a breadcrumb, focus inside search results, stacked focus. Files: `client/src/lib/treeState.ts` (`focusRoots`, `findDirNode`), `client/src/sidebar/Sidebar.tsx` + `hooks/useVaultTree.ts`, `client/src/app.css`; tests in `treeState.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx` (`focus mode (YAZ-1605)`), `store.test.ts`, `windows.test.ts`, `ipc/window.test.ts`, `storage.test.ts`.

**The Favorites tab** (YAZ-1766, 🔒 D1–D10 on the issue; D11–D15 in 6A–6C): the second lens shows the vault's pinned files and folders. THE LIST is `<root>/.yaseendocs/favorites.json` (D11 — it moved out of `FolderState` in 6A, D15: a clean break, no migration) — VAULT-RELATIVE POSIX paths on disk, absolute over the bridge (`favorites.get/set/onChanged`), so it TRAVELS WITH THE VAULT and GitHub sync carries it between machines; shared by every window on the vault (a second window's — or another machine's — write arrives through `favorites:changed` and a re-read), in the user's order: add appends, drag-to-reorder rewrites. The Sidebar has ONE writer, `saveFavorites`: optimistic, then `favorites.set`; a refusal (a malformed file → `INVALID_CONFIG`, D12) reverts the list and toasts an error. THE MENU ITEM (D3) is one state-aware rule, `toggleFavorite`, LEADING the "Open in ▸" group (… Rename · ─ · Add to favorites · Add note shortcut (a folder row, YAZ-2290) · Open in ▸ · ─ · Delete) on every file or dir row in every lens, never blank space; its OWN targets `MenuTargets.favoritePaths` (the row, or the ordered 2+ selection holding it) and `favoriteIsOn` (true only when EVERY target is pinned — a mixed selection reads "Add" and pins the missing ones); the label counts a plural ("Add 3 to favorites"), and the toast (`'favorite'` kind, the same heart) says "Added / Removed N to/from favorites". THE ROWS: `favoriteRoots(tree, list)` in `treeState.ts` — one node per stored path via `findNode` (file OR dir), STORED order, nesting and redundancy KEPT (a file pinned inside a pinned folder draws at the root AND inside it; `focusRoots` would de-duplicate, so it is not used); a path the tree no longer holds yields no row and NOTHING ELSE — there is no renderer prune (D14): a note that has not synced to this machine yet simply does not show until it arrives, in-app rename/delete repair the file in main (D13), and main heals dead entries on the user's next write. A pinned folder unfolds through the SAME `expanded` set as Files (D7) — open here, open there. DRAG (D4): root rows carry `Tree.reorder` (depth 0 only): a 2px accent line on the hovered row's edge (`.tree__row--drop-before/after`), drop rewrites the list; nested rows do not drag at all and the tab's `move` is inert — nothing on ♥ moves on disk. THE SUBSET RULE (D10, 3B1): the tab shows a subset of the vault, so a gesture that needs a row it lacks goes to Files — "Show in sidebar" hops the lens and reveals there (`App.showInSidebar`), and "New note" / "New folder" from a row whose target dir is not on the tab hops before the inline input mounts (`startCreate`). Search behaves as on every tab. Not in v1: reordering nested rows, a shortcut. Files: `desktop/src/main/favorites.ts` (the file, the repair) + `desktop/src/main/ipc/favorites.ts` (the bridge half, the per-root subscription and broadcast), `desktop/src/main/ipc/fs.ts` (the repair calls), `client/src/api.ts` (`api.favorites`), `client/src/lib/treeState.ts` (`findNode`, `favoriteRoots`), `client/src/sidebar/{Sidebar,Tree,menuSections}.ts(x)`, `client/src/views/view/icons.tsx` (`HeartIcon`), `client/src/components/NoticeIcon.tsx`, `client/src/lib/notice.ts`, `shared/types.ts` (`MAX_FAVORITES`, `FavoritesConfig`, `WindowEntry.focusFavorites`), `desktop/src/main/{store,windows}.ts` and `desktop/src/main/ipc/window.ts` (`focusFavorites`); tests `favorites.test.ts`, `ipc/favorites.test.ts`, `ipc/fs.test.ts`, `treeState.test.ts`, `menuSections.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx` (`favorites (YAZ-1766)`), `store.test.ts`, `ipc/window.test.ts`, `windows.test.ts`.

## Packaging (F1 — GRO-2175)

`npm run desktop:build` = electron-vite build + `electron-builder --mac` with the config in `desktop/package.json` `"build"`: appId `com.yasinarshad.yaseendocs`, productName `Yaseen Docs`, targets `dmg` + `dir` for arm64 ONLY, `identity: null` (ad-hoc signature — no Developer-ID, no notarization, locked), category productivity, icon `desktop/build/icon.icns` (placeholder). Output: `desktop/dist-app/mac-arm64/Yaseen Docs.app` + `desktop/dist-app/Yaseen Docs-0.1.0-arm64.dmg` (`${productName}-${version}-arm64.dmg` — the version is the ROOT package.json's, injected via `-c.extraMetadata.version`). `tools/packDesktop.mjs --mac` then recompresses electron-builder's zlib dmg to LZMA (ULMO, macOS 10.15+; −32 MB) in place, mounting the new image and verifying its app's seal before it replaces the old one, and deletes the stale `.blockmap` (nothing reads it — no auto-updater) (`tools/lib/dmg.mjs`, YAZ-2181). Its test, `tools/dmg.test.mjs`, drives the real `hdiutil` and `codesign` only when `YASEEN_DOCS_DMG_TEST=1`: `npm test` skips it, since every `desktop:build` already runs that path and checks the new image's seal. Before the ad-hoc signature, the `afterPack` hook (`desktop/build/adhocSign.cjs`) deletes Chromium's non-English `locale.pak` folders from the framework (Windows: all but `locales/en-US.pak` + `en-GB.pak`), −47.6 MB installed; the app-level `Contents/Resources/*.lproj` markers stay, so Open/Save panels keep the OS language, while on a non-English macOS sorting and dates follow en-US (🔒 YAZ-2131 D1, YAZ-2182). The bundle's Info.plist claims: `protocols` registers the `yaseendocs` scheme with LaunchServices (so OS-level link opens route to the app), and `fileAssociations` lists `.md`/`.markdown` with role Editor, rank **Alternate** — Yaseen Docs appears in Finder's Open With but never steals the default handler. Packaged files are `out/**` only, and `app.asar` carries NO `node_modules`: every dependency is bundled into `out/main` — electron-vite 5 externalizes `desktop/package.json`'s `dependencies` by default, so chokidar is a devDependency and main/preload set `externalizeDeps: false`; `tools/mainBundle.test.mjs` fails if the bundle requires anything but Node built-ins and electron (YAZ-2185). The renderer is esbuild-minified with `sourcemap: 'hidden'` maps (no `sourceMappingURL`), and `renderSourcemapsAside()` moves every `.map` to the gitignored `desktop/.maps/<version>/` as the bundle is written, so none ships and a minified stack can still be symbolicated locally; main, preload and the cli stay unminified (YAZ-2183). No auto-update, no Intel/universal, no Windows/Linux (locked — Future issues).


**Size and integrity gate (YAZ-2131 1B).** `npm run perf:budget` measures the build against `tools/perf/budget.json` and exits 1 when a row crosses its ceiling or an integrity check fails. Rows: packaged `appBytes`, `frameworksBytes`, `asarBytes`, `dmgBytes`, `chromiumLocaleCount`/`chromiumLocaleBytes`, `lprojCount` (exactly 55: the app-level markers that keep Open/Save panels in the OS language), `asarNodeModulesFiles`; build `rendererEagerJsBytes`/`rendererEagerCssBytes` (the entry's static-import closure + its stylesheet), `rendererTotalBytes`, `rendererMapBytes`, `mainBundleBytes`. Integrity: `out/main/{index,cli}.js`, `out/preload/index.js`, `out/renderer/index.html` exist (and are in the asar); every `./x.js|css` a renderer chunk names is shipped (a lazy Mermaid or CodeMirror chunk that went missing would only fail on first use); KaTeX and the drawing fonts are present; Info.plist carries `yaseendocs` and `.md` as Editor/Alternate; the bundle is ad-hoc sealed and `codesign --verify --deep --strict` passes; `Resources/bin/yaseendocs` is executable. `drawingAssetBytes` (`out/renderer/excalidraw-assets`) is reported on its own row and never gated: the drawing component is out of scope. **The ratchet:** a change that shrinks a row lowers its ceiling in `budget.json` in the same PR; raising a ceiling needs Yasin's OK in the PR description. `tools/perf/baseline.json` is the frozen v0.9.27 @ 63aeeea measurement every before/after quotes, and is never edited.
## Editor rules (client)

1. **Frontmatter**: on load, `splitFrontmatter(content)` → `{ frontmatter, body }`; only `body` goes into Crepe. On save, write `frontmatter + getMarkdownForSave(crepe)`. Frontmatter is re-prepended byte-identically (Crepe would otherwise turn `---` YAML into `***` + paragraph + setext heading underline).
2. **Crepe construction**: always via `createCrepe()` — features come ONLY from `src/editor/featureConfig.ts` (`ENABLED_FEATURES` / `DISABLED_FEATURES`, every `CrepeFeature` classified; ImageBlock, TopBar, AI off; `featureConfig.test.ts` asserts the running editor loads exactly the allowlist); the `Crepe` class itself is `src/editor/crepe.ts` (YAZ-2184) — Crepe rebuilt from its own `CrepeBuilder` and per-feature entry points with ONLY the enabled features bundled, because the package root imports every feature (KaTeX with Latex) and one VALUE import from it puts them all back in the main chunk; production code imports only types from `@milkdown/crepe`, `crepe.test.ts` guards that and holds the class to the package's own `Crepe` (same loaded features, schema, plugins, rendered DOM), and `main.tsx` imports Crepe's common theme file by file without `latex`/`top-bar`/`ai`/`diff` CSS, list_item content `block+` (extended from GFM task item schema), `markdownUpdated` listener wired. The allowlist says which features load; **`featureConfigs` configures one of them**. Its ONE entry is `[CrepeFeature.BlockEdit]`: `listGroup.orderedList = null` removes Crepe's stock Ordered List row (YAZ-1329), while optional `buildMenu` appends Drawing (YAZ-877); neither changes the feature SET. **The Drawing slash item** (`src/editor/drawingMenu.ts`, second build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852): Crepe builds its stock groups and then hands the `GroupBuilder` to `buildMenu`, so Drawing is APPENDED to the existing `advanced` group (Image / Code / Table / Math) of Crepe's OWN menu — never a parallel slash plugin, so filtering, keyboard navigation and hiding all come for free. HOST-SUPPLIED CREATOR: the row exists only when `createCrepe({ drawing })` provides `create()` (Editor wires `() => createDrawing(root)` plus the window's passive `onNotice`) — absent → NO Drawing row is added, while Ordered List remains absent in both cases. `onRun` matches the stock items' mechanism as far as it can: `clearTextInCurrentBlockCommand` first (the same opening move every stock item makes — it clears the block the `/query` was typed into), then, where a stock item would call a schema command, a `tr.insertText` of the embed — because a drawing embed is PLAIN MARKDOWN TEXT (rule 23's `![[…]]`, no schema change, no serializer change, round-trip byte-identical outside the one inserted line). Crepe's `onRun` is synchronous and the write is not, so the view is re-read after the await and the text goes at the LIVE selection; a failed create inserts nothing and goes to the passive notice, never a dialog. The file itself: `createDrawing()` (`src/drawings/`) writes a valid EMPTY Excalidraw scene (`{ type: 'excalidraw', version: 2, source: 'yaseen-docs', elements: [], appState: {}, files: {} }`, pretty-printed + trailing newline) to `assets/drawings/Drawing YYYY-MM-DD HH.mm.ss.excalidraw` through `writeAsset` with `create: true` — the drawings home is made by `writeAsset`'s own `mkdir -p`, never scaffolded, and NEVER OVERWRITES: a same-second collision comes back `ALREADY_EXISTS` and the bounded loop retries ` 2`, ` 3`, … (any other failure propagates to the notice). It returns the BASENAME, which is all the embed needs — `readAsset`'s shortest-path rule finds it anywhere under the root. End-to-end in `desktop/e2e/drawing.spec.ts` step 1 (the spec the preview YAZ-878 extended in steps 2-4, rule 27, and the modal YAZ-879 extended in step 5, rule 28).
3. **Save post-processing**: `postProcessMarkdown()` un-escapes `\[\[` → `[[` (wikilinks/embeds) and restores the visible same-line numeric bullet spelling armored by rule 8.
4. **Replacing content in a live instance** (external change / Reload): `setMarkdown(crepe, md)` → `replaceAll(md, true)` from `@milkdown/kit/utils`, keeping focus and caret. TAB SWITCHES never remount (GRO-2234): every VISITED tab keeps its editor mounted in its own layer, inactive layers hidden via `visibility: hidden` plus `content-visibility: hidden` (YAZ-2195: a hidden layer skips style, layout and paint, so switches, resizes and theme changes stop scaling with the number of mounted tabs; the right panel's editor layers do the same) — never `display: none`, which would reset the scroller's scrollTop — so scroll position, caret, undo history and the unsaved buffer all survive a switch. Opening a DIFFERENT file in the same tab still remounts the Crepe host (`key={path}`): the old file's editor unmounts (→ autosave flush) and the previous file stays on screen until the next one has loaded.
5. **Outline folding** (GRO-2011): `createOutlineFolding()` (`src/editor/outline/`) is registered in `createCrepe()`. Parent `list_item`s (those owning a nested `bullet_list`/`ordered_list`) — and since YAZ-1709 any `list_item` holding an `image` in its own blocks, leaf or not — get a widget `<button class="outline-toggle" aria-expanded>` at the start of their content (one stroked SVG chevron sized by `--fold-chevron-size`, default 14px, rotated −90° via CSS when collapsed — GRO-2093; the 18px box is unchanged so strip/gate geometry holds); collapsing adds `data-outline-folded="true"` to EVERY nested list of the item (CSS `display:none`) — mixed markers at one indent (`* a` then `- b`) are unified on load (rule 8, GRO-2112); should sibling lists still arise (e.g. bullet + ordered), all of them fold (GRO-2031). An image bullet's fold additionally stamps every image in the item's OWN blocks (nested lists excluded) with `data-outline-folded-image="true"` — a node decoration on the `image` node, so the image node view (rule 30) knows nothing about folding — and `imageView.css` turns that `.image-view` into a one-line chip (a fixed text-height thumbnail, `object-fit: cover`, NO label — a pasted image's alt is its file name, and images are named by typing beside them on the bullet; no handles); the plugin's `handleClickOn` on the chip's THUMBNAIL (not the wrapper) unfolds its bullet — the chevron's toggle, from the chip. Every image of a foldable item, folded or not, also carries `data-outline-foldable-image="true"`, which is what shows the node view's corner `button.image-view__fold` on an expanded image; the plugin's `handleDOMEvents.mousedown` folds the bullet from that button (taken on mousedown so PM never turns the press into a node selection). Fold-all and the guide-line click read the same `entries`, so image bullets take part like any parent. Toggles are metadata-only transactions (`tr.docChanged === false`) — the listener never fires `markdownUpdated`, `getMarkdownForSave()` is unchanged, and the file's mtime is untouched. Fold keys = FNV-1a hash of the item's first-block text (an image counts as its alt text, width stripped, src as the fallback — `itemLabelText`, YAZ-1709) + occurrence index (`outlineFoldKeys.ts`), persisted via `storage.getFolds/setFolds`. Fold transactions carry one of: a position (toggle), `fold-all` / `unfold-all`, `{ set, collapsed }` (guide-line click: direct parents on collapse, their full parent-item subtree on expansion), `undo-fold`; the multi-item ones remember the previous collapsed set so ⌘Z reverts them as one step.
6. **Accepted lossy normalisation** (standard remark-stringify behaviour; content preserved, formatting normalised): `-`/`+` bullets → `*` (alternating `-` for adjacent sibling lists), tabs → 2-space indent, structural `1)` ↔ `1.` ordered markers swap/renumber (but rule 8's same-line numeric bullet text keeps its visible delimiter), setext → ATX headings, two-space hard breaks → `\`, trailing whitespace stripped, `___` → `***`, indented code → fenced, tables re-padded, lazy blockquote continuation gets `> `, `_`/`*`/`[`/`=`/`&` escaped in text where ambiguous, bare URLs/emails → `<autolink>`, file always ends with a single `\n`. First save of an untouched file WILL rewrite the file in this normalised form.
7. **Outliner keymap** (GRO-2012, `src/editor/outline/listCommands.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` with keymap priority 100): Tab = indent (no-op on a first sibling — never inserts spaces), Shift-Tab = outdent (level 1 → paragraph; following siblings nest under the lifted item), Enter at the end of a parent item = new FIRST child (after the subtree when the parent is folded), Enter on an empty item = outdent / leave the list, Backspace at the start of an item = join into the previous paragraph (empty parent: children take its place; non-empty parent: no-op). Multi-item selection + Tab/Shift-Tab moves all selected items.
8. **List-item round trip** (`src/editor/listItemRoundTrip.ts`): an empty bullet/ordered item is written as a bare marker (`*` / `1.`), never `* <br />` — `<br />` opens a CommonMark HTML block that swallows the item's nested children on reload. Parsing gives an item whose nested list starts on a later line (`* ` + children, Obsidian's empty parent) an empty leading paragraph. **Same-line numeric bullet text** (YAZ-1329): a numeric delimiter immediately after a bullet marker (`* 6) text` / `* 6. text`, any bullet spelling or indent) is temporarily escaped before CommonMark parses it, so it remains the bullet's paragraph text rather than becoming a nested ordered list; save removes that boundary escape and preserves the visible digits/delimiter. Real ordered Markdown on its own line remains structural. Fenced code, decimals, prose and non-immediate numeric text are untouched. Before parsing, `normalizeEmptyItems()` also rewrites legacy `* <br />` lines to bare markers and an empty task `* [ ] ` / `* [x]` to `* [ ] <br />` (remark alone reads `[ ]` as text and would write `* \[ ]`); on save `stripEmptyTaskBreaks()` writes it back as `* [ ]` — `<br />` never reaches the disk. **Mixed markers** (GRO-2112): the same pass runs `unifySiblingMarkers()` — `-`, `*` and `+` are one bullet: bullets at one indent parse as ONE list (the first sibling's marker wins on load; a blank line starts a new list, as in Obsidian). CommonMark would otherwise start a new list on every marker change. Line rules live in the function's docblock. Disk is untouched by loading; the first save writes `*` throughout (rule 6) as before. **Empty nested item** (YAZ-1357): the same pass runs `separateEmptyNestedItems()` LAST — a text item followed on the very next line by a DEEPER bare marker gets one blank line between them, because CommonMark forbids an empty item from interrupting a paragraph (without it `* a` + `  *` reads back as the text `a *`, and the outline seed's `-` spelling as a setext heading the bullets-only schema drops whole, which is what tripped the YAZ-974 guard). The blank line is what Milkdown writes back, so the file converges on it; fenced code is untouched.

9. **Depth bullet glyphs** (GRO-2013, `src/editor/outline/bullets.css`): CSS only — Crepe's bullet svg is hidden and `.label.bullet::after` draws ● / ○ / ■ by nesting depth of `.milkdown-list-item-block` (repeating every three levels; ordered levels count), `--list-indent: 2.15em`, marker colour 78% of the text colour, all from Crepe theme tokens. Heading-first items (`* # Part 1`) offset the label row by the heading's margin and size it to the heading's line box (`--list-label-offset/height`, shared with the fold chevron). **Done tasks read as done** (YAZ-1514, 🔒 D1–D4): the same file strikes and dims (`--list-done-color`, 55% of the text colour) the FIRST line (a textblock — a first-child code block or table is left alone) of any `li.list-item` whose label carries Milkdown's own `checked` class — so `Mod-Enter`, the ☑ click and a `[x]` written by Obsidian all look identical; nested children keep their own state, a second paragraph in the item stays normal, and `~~` is NEVER written to the file. Markdown untouched.
10. **Typography** (GRO-2009, `src/app.css`): Obsidian defaults — system font stack, 16px / 1.5 body, heading scale 1.802 / 1.602 / 1.424 / 1.266 / 1.125 / 1em at weight 600 (h1 700), line-height 1.3 — set through Crepe's `--crepe-font-*` / `--crepe-base-font-size` custom properties on `.editor-instance .milkdown`.

11. **Underline** (GRO-2028, `src/editor/marks/underline.ts`): ProseMirror mark `underline` (`<u>` in the DOM). On disk it is obsidian-underline's inline HTML `<u>text</u>`: a `$remark` plugin wraps each mdast `html("<u>")` … `html("</u>")` pair (nearest match, any depth, inside any inline parent such as `**…**`) into an `underline` mdast node after parsing, and registers the remark-stringify handler that writes it back as `<u>` + children + `</u>` — so `a <u>b</u> c` round-trips byte-identically. Unmatched tags and every other inline HTML keep going through Milkdown's `html` atom node unchanged. Not a Crepe feature (allowlist untouched, no toolbar button).

12. **Zoom into a bullet** (GRO-2029, `src/editor/outline/zoom.ts` + `zoom.css`): view state ONLY, like folding — the plugin holds the zoomed `list_item` position (mapped through every transaction; cleared if the item is deleted), and decorations hide every block off the path root → zoomed item (`outline-zoom-hidden`), strip the ancestors' glyph/chevron/first block (`outline-zoom-ancestor`, a folded ancestor renders expanded without touching fold state) and render a breadcrumb widget (`File name › Ancestor › … › Item`, labels = first-block text truncated at 40 chars; crumbs zoom to that ancestor, the file crumb zooms out fully). Triggers: click the bullet glyph (`.label-wrapper`; task checkboxes keep toggling), `Mod-.`, `Mod-Shift-.` (see Keyboard). Zooming dispatches a metadata-only transaction: never `markdownUpdated`, disk untouched, fold state unaffected. Not persisted — switching files remounts the editor and clears it, and an external file change (watcher → `replaceAll`) replaces the doc and clears it too. `createCrepe({ zoom: { fileName } })`; allowlist untouched. **History** (GRO-2091): every zoom change (glyph, crumb, `Mod-.`, `Mod-Shift-.`, zoom-out included) pushes one in-memory entry `history.state = { mdappZoom: { file, key } }` with the URL untouched (2-arg `pushState`; `key` = fold-key scheme over ALL list items, `null` = zoomed out), and `popstate` restores that level without pushing — so Back / Forward walk zoom levels. Entries of another file are ignored (left behind by a file switch → a Back press can be silent), an unresolvable key zooms out, the original page entry (no zoom state) = zoomed out.

13. **List guide lines** (GRO-2030, `src/editor/outline/guideLines.ts` + `.css`): every nested list (`li.list-item > .children > .content-dom > ul/ol`) gets a CSS `::before` strip — 10px wide, absolutely positioned (`-1 * (--list-indent / 2 + 10px)`) so its centred 1px line runs under the parent's glyph centre; zero layout shift, folded lists take their line with them, colours from `currentColor` (14%, hover 35%). Pseudo boxes hit-test as the list element, so a pointer left of the list's border box = the strip: the plugin's `handleDOMEvents` turns mousedown into `toggleOutlineFoldChildren(listPos)` (GRO-2107/YAZ-1317: any direct parent expanded collapses the direct parents; when all are collapsed, the click unfolds every parent item in their full subtree; the line's owner and leaves remain untouched; an all-leaf line is a swallowed no-op; markdown/mtime untouched, caret unmoved) and mousemove into the `outline-guide-hover` highlight (element `:hover` would light up while editing text inside).

### Clipboard input and paste modes (YAZ-1394)

Normal Paste keeps Milkdown’s supported rich HTML preference, document typography, existing fake-bullet outline conversion, and Markdown interpretation when only plain text is available. `editor/clipboardPaste.ts`, registered after `outlinePaste`, converts external root-level `p / br… / p` separators into one empty paragraph per standalone break. A break-containing paragraph otherwise acquires an extra visual line. This preserves intentional blank spacing rather than deleting it; inline breaks, nested blocks, explicit empty paragraphs, and unmarked internal `data-pm-slice` HTML are untouched. Our marked copy-out separators are restored to empty paragraphs first, including within internal slices and nested lists, preserving attributes and slice metadata. The upstream Google Docs wrapper cleanup runs first. This rule is based on a reproduced clipboard shape; it is not a promise to normalize arbitrary source HTML.

Native Edit and right-click menus expose **Paste as → Plain text / Markdown**; Plain text uses **⌘⇧V**. Main captures text once per gesture and sends `ClipboardPasteRequest` to that window. Plain text inserts characters literally, preserves spaces and line breaks (CRLF/CR become LF), and does not parse Markdown. Markdown explicitly parses that text with the existing schema, even when the clipboard also has HTML; open slice boundaries let inline content remain in its surrounding sentence. Both modes apply existing paste rules (including table repair and the bullets-only lock of a folder's outline), replace the active selection in one undoable transaction, and leave empty clipboard text as a no-op. In code, text remains literal.

**Normal pasted numbering (YAZ-1429):** external numbered lists become ordinary text with visible labels, so Enter cannot continue or renumber them. `editor/clipboardNumbers.ts` converts HTML ordered-list markers before DOM parsing, honoring numeric start, reverse order and explicit item values while retaining rich inline content, nested blocks and empty parent labels. Foreign `data-pm-slice` context is removed so it cannot recreate automatic numbering. Plain-text-only paste uses the existing remark AST and each item's source position to preserve original labels/punctuation, repeated numbers and task markers; code nodes remain literal. Mixed fake-bullet outlines use the same conversion, paste constraints and undo boundary. Saved ordered Markdown, Number children and explicit Paste as Markdown remain unchanged. Newly copied app HTML carries `data-yaseendocs-clipboard` alongside ProseMirror's slice metadata so intentional internal lists round-trip. This identifier exists only in clipboard HTML, never in note files. A text-only copy has no source identity and follows normal external-paste rules; explicit Markdown is the deliberate override. Existing notes are not migrated. Source-specific CSS counters are outside this normalization contract.

Each Crepe view subscribes through `menu.onPasteAs`, claims only its own focused surface, and unsubscribes on destroy. CodeMirror and ordinary inputs use the private validated native `insertText` fallback, retaining their own caret and edit handling. Read-only Crepe views cannot be changed by either path. No clipboard writes, mode flags, async clipboard reads, new dependency, or changed save serializer are involved in paste handling. `clipboardPaste.test.ts`, native menu, preload bridge, and window IPC tests cover these boundaries; native input undo and the actual source-app clipboard still require a pointed manual check when validating a release.

### Clipboard output (YAZ-938, YAZ-1389, YAZ-1411, YAZ-1443)

Normal Copy/Cut provides readable `text/plain` and rich `text/html` (YAZ-1443). `editor/clipboardPlainText.ts` reads the selected ProseMirror structure rather than Markdown source. Paragraph boundaries contribute one newline and each explicit empty paragraph contributes one additional blank line; repeated/leading/trailing selected empty rows, inline breaks, code whitespace and literal punctuation remain intact. Headings/marks/links copy their visible text without Markdown syntax; links retain their target in HTML and explicit Markdown. Lists retain readable labels, nesting and task state, and table cells use tabs. A single text block selected inside open list wrappers copies its selected words without inventing a bullet; selections spanning parent and nested children keep their hierarchy. No global whitespace collapse or note migration is involved.

Edit and right-click menus expose **Copy as → Plain text / Markdown**. Both write text only, removing rich formats so destinations cannot select HTML instead. Plain text matches normal Copy’s readable text. Markdown uses the existing source serializer, including intentional syntax and empty-paragraph markers. Each Crepe subscribes through `menu.onCopyAs`, claims only its focused selection (including read-only selections), and unsubscribes on destroy. Focused CodeMirror uses its public editor-state selection API, preserving all nonempty ranges and line breaks as literal code in both explicit modes. Preload falls back to selected input/textarea or focused DOM text; password fields and empty selections leave the clipboard unchanged. Main validates the registered live sender, main frame, renderer URL, current target and bounded string before the private `clipboard.writeText` call. No arbitrary clipboard writer is exposed through the renderer API.

For rich HTML (YAZ-1411), the existing clipboard-only DOM serializer reuses paragraph rendering/attributes and marks, sets paragraph margins to zero, and exports each empty paragraph as a standalone BR carrying `data-mdapp-empty-paragraph`. Actual Google Docs testing showed that it drops `p > br` empty paragraphs but preserves a standalone BR as an empty paragraph; our paste adapter restores the marked BR before ProseMirror parsing. The app clipboard identity and ProseMirror slice metadata remain intact for intentional internal lists. No placeholder spaces or invisible characters are introduced. Default HTML, the live editor and the save serializer are unchanged by the plain-text fix. Destination apps can apply their own formatting; real-app review remains useful. Clipboard copy/paste integration tests and native menu, preload and private IPC tests cover these contracts.

## Keyboard (client)

All bindings are `$shortcut` keymaps registered in `createCrepe()` with priority 100 (Crepe's own keymaps are 50), so they run first and fall through (`return false`) when they do not apply. `Mod` = ⌘ on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere (ProseMirror decides by `navigator.platform`). App-level window & tab shortcuts (⌘⇧N / ⌘⇧O / ⌘W / ⌘⇧W / ⌃Tab / ⌃⇧Tab) live in the application menu — see "Menus & shortcuts".

| Keys | Where | Does | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Tab` | caret/selection in list items | indent (sink) the item(s); no-op on a first sibling — never inserts spaces | GRO-2012 `listCommands.ts` |
| `Shift-Tab` | list items | outdent (lift); level 1 → paragraph; following siblings nest under the lifted item | GRO-2012 (Crepe default, kept) |
| `Enter` | end of a parent item | new FIRST child (after the folded subtree when the parent is collapsed) | GRO-2012 |
| `Enter` | empty item (no children) | outdent; at level 1 leaves the list as a paragraph | GRO-2012 |
| `Backspace` | start of an item's first block | join into the previous paragraph / parent item; empty parent: children take its place; non-empty parent: no-op | GRO-2012 |
| `Mod-Enter` | list item(s) whose first block touches the selection | cycle bullet → `[ ]` → `[x]` → bullet, each item from its own state; a `[x]` item's first line renders struck + dimmed (rule 9, CSS only — the file stays `[x]`); outside lists falls through (table exit / CodeMirror exit keep theirs) | GRO-2027 `hotkeys.ts` · YAZ-1514 `bullets.css` |
