| `Mod-ArrowUp` / `Mod-ArrowDown` | caret in a list item, or in a heading section outside lists | fold / unfold that item (`setOutlineFoldAtSelection`, same meta-only transaction as the chevron — persisted, ⌘Z-revertible; text-only leaf or already in that state = consumed no-op; an image bullet folds to its chip, YAZ-1709), or the innermost heading section at the caret (rule 29, `headingHotkeys.ts`); outside both falls through to the native document-start/end jump | GRO-2092 `hotkeys.ts`, YAZ-1140 |
| `Mod-Shift-u` | anywhere | fold every foldable bullet (parents and image bullets, YAZ-1709) and foldable H1-H3 heading section in one metadata-only transaction; both plugins persist through the app state's `folders[root].folds` | `foldAllHotkeys.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-i` | anywhere | unfold every foldable bullet and foldable H1-H3 heading section in one metadata-only transaction | `foldAllHotkeys.ts` |
| `Mod-z` | a fold, a zoom or a heading fold was the latest VIEW action (no doc change since) | revert exactly that one step — an individual fold, a combined bullet+heading fold-all/unfold-all (both exact prior sets in one transaction), or a zoom. All transaction kinds carry `VIEW_ACTION_META` (`viewActions.ts`); each plugin drops its pending undo when a foreign kind fires, while `document-fold` deliberately keeps both halves of the combined action eligible. Otherwise falls through to history's undo. Plugin-appended transactions (e.g. Crepe's trailing paragraph) don't break eligibility | GRO-2075 `outlineFolding.ts`, GRO-2091 `zoom.ts`, YAZ-1140 `headingFolding.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-x` | selection | toggle strikethrough (Obsidian binding; Crepe's `Mod-Alt-x` still works) | GRO-2027 |
| `Mod-u` | selection | toggle underline (`<u>…</u>` on disk) | GRO-2028 `marks/underline.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-h` | selection or caret | toggle the YELLOW highlight (`==…==` on disk): lit if any of the selection carries yellow → remove it, else apply it (edge whitespace excluded); at a caret flips the stored mark for what is typed next; the toolbar's yellow dot is the same command | YAZ-1480 `marks/highlight.ts` |
| `Mod-.` | caret in a list item | zoom into that item (view-only; breadcrumbs appear); outside lists falls through | GRO-2029 `outline/zoom.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-.` | while zoomed | zoom out one level (parent item, or fully at top level); not zoomed falls through | GRO-2029 |
| `Shift-Tab` / `Mod-[` | while zoomed, on the zoomed item or a direct child | no-op (lifting would escape the zoomed subtree); everywhere else outdents as usual | GRO-2029 |
| `↑` / `↓` / `Enter` / `Esc` | while the `[[` link picker is open | move the highlight (wraparound) / insert the highlighted suggestion as plain `[[name]]` text / dismiss leaving the typed text; with the picker CLOSED all four fall through — the picker's keymap is registered before the outliner's so its priority-100 Enter wins the tie only while open (rule 24) | GRO-2191 `wikilink/wikilinkPicker.ts` |
| `Shift-ArrowUp` / `Shift-ArrowDown` | any text selection | **Whole-line selection (YAZ-1734, D1–D7; `lineSelection.ts`, one file, registered before the outliner keymap).** A "line" is the textblock — a paragraph, a heading, a list item's own text — never the visual row (D1). Only the HEAD moves. On a block edge it moves to the same edge of the previous / next VISIBLE textblock, so `⌘←` `⇧↓` and `⌘→` `⇧↑` select exactly one whole line and each repeat adds exactly one more; mid-line it first moves to its own line's near edge (`⇧↓` the end, `⇧↑` the start); an empty block: `⇧↓` takes the start edge, `⇧↑` the end (D2). A press never crosses the anchor and always reaches it, so `⇧↑` undoes exactly one `⇧↓`; collapsed on the anchor, the next press flips past it (D5). Hidden lines — inside a folded bullet's subtree, inside a folded heading section, or outside the zoom (`isHiddenTextblock` asks the three plugins' STATE: `collapsedItemsHiding`, `collapsedHeadingsHiding`, `getZoomedItemPos`; never the DOM) — are skipped, so the range may span hidden text. Hidden text is NEVER deleted: `⌫`, `Delete`, typing and `Enter` over such a range (`deleteVisible`, `visibleTypeOver`, `enterVisible`) remove only the visible pieces by explicit construction (the last block and every whole visible block between go as nodes together with the wrappers they were the only child of; the first block keeps its head and gains the last block's tail — selected from its start it goes as a node and the last keeps its tail), and `Enter` then re-presses itself on the view so the ordinary Enter splits at the caret it left (the keymap hands every handler of one key the same pre-delete state, so merely declining is not enough). `⌘X` stays the ordinary cut on purpose: it MOVES the whole range, hidden kids included, and paste restores them (D6, D7). A `list_item` left without its own paragraph never survives a transaction: `liftHeadlessItems` (`appendTransaction`, every doc change, whatever path) lifts its kids one level up, subtrees intact — a deleted parent's kids move up, folded or not (D3); to delete a folded parent WITH its kids, unfold it first. No visible neighbour (document edge, zoom edge) is a consumed no-op. Falls through to the native pixel-column move only for a node selection or a leaf block (`hr`) in the gap; an image is an inline atom inside a paragraph (ImageBlock off), so an image line is a line. Pinned neighbours (`lineSelection.scenarios.test.ts`): a fenced code block is a line (the head lands at the start of its text); above a table the head never enters it — `prosemirror-tables` normalises the cross-cell selection back to the paragraph above, selected whole; the zoomed item's own text is the visible line above its children; in a folder's outline (bullets-only) a deleted parent row leaves an empty bullet keeping its kids, because that schema cannot drop the row. A selection with no hidden line inside falls through to the ordinary path untouched. | YAZ-1734 `lineSelection.ts` |

14. **Multi-block drag** (GRO-2019, `src/editor/multiBlockDrag.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()`): dragging the block handle while a multi-block selection contains the grabbed block moves the WHOLE selection. A document-capture mousedown expands the selection to whole sibling blocks (`expandedBlockRange`: deepest level containing both ends — sibling `list_item`s inside a nested list, not the outer list) and suppresses Crepe's single-block selection replacement; a capture dragstart hands ProseMirror a fully CLOSED slice (an open slice would splice into the target paragraph) via `dataTransfer` + `view.dragging`; after the drop an `appendTransaction` (on `uiEvent: 'drop'`) deletes the merged empty shell the move-deletion leaves at the origin, walking up wrappers it was the only child of. Drop X-position controls nesting depth (ProseMirror `dropPoint`). A grab OUTSIDE the selection never arms the plugin — Crepe's single-block drag is untouched. Folded parents drag with their hidden children.
15. **Block handle gate** (GRO-2081, `src/editor/blockHandleGate.ts`): while the pointer is inside a guide-line strip band or a fold chevron's box, `.milkdown-block-handle` gets `mdapp-handle-muted` (pointer-events none + invisible) so those clicks land; muting pauses during drags. Band geometry is imported from `guideLines.ts` — one source of truth. Crepe's `blockHandle.shouldShow` config is typed upstream but never consumed; do not reach for it.
16. **Hotkey reference** (GRO-2067, `src/settings/hotkeys.ts` — the Settings › Hotkeys page since YAZ-1679 — a `standalone` section of the dialog, its own page under the nav divider rather than a stretch of the scrolling settings page — which retired the keyboard button beside the settings cog): the page lists every binding, one group per table, from the exported `HOTKEYS`/`VIEW_HOTKEYS`/`WINDOW_HOTKEYS`/`MOUSE_TIPS` lists, and the settings search indexes every key and label so "close tab" finds the table (`WINDOW_HOTKEYS` = the app-menu window & tab shortcuts — ⌘⇧N, ⌘⇧O, ⌘O Switch vault (YAZ-1767), ⌘, Settings (YAZ-1679), ⌘W Close Tab, ⌘⇧W Close Window, ⌃Tab/⌃⇧Tab and ⌘⇧]/⌘⇧[ Next/Previous Tab (the ⌥-click Open Recent tip is gone since YAZ-1914 D2 — ⌥ no longer differs); `MOUSE_TIPS` carries the editor wiki-link click conventions — click = current tab with create-on-missing, Links C GRO-2192 — the SHARED "⌘-click file or link" background-tab line (sidebar I3 + editor links, one convention) and the right-click-for-Open-in-new-window tip, plus the vault right-click tip, YAZ-1798, with Set display name since YAZ-1974; the ⌘O line names ⇧⏎) — update them together with any keymap or menu-accelerator change (`hotkeys.test.ts` pins the set).
17. **URL** (GRO-2069, `src/lib/urlHash.ts`): the open file is mirrored as `#/abs/path.md` via `history.replaceState` (never pushState); on boot a hash file wins over the folder's remembered `lastFile` and may live OUTSIDE the current root (the sidebar's stale-file validation skips out-of-root paths). No file → no hash. The shareable `yaseendocs://` link is separate — see "Links".

18. **Bullet threading** (GRO-2094, `src/editor/outline/bulletThreading.ts` + `.css`): view-only decorations derived from `selection.$from` on every update — `outline-thread-node` on each list_item on the root → caret path (glyph takes the app accent `--accent` via `--list-marker-color` on its `.label-wrapper`; Crepe's frame `--crepe-color-primary` is #333 and would read as black), and inside each NESTED list on the path `outline-thread-seg` on items 0…pathIndex plus `outline-thread-stop` on the path child (segment cut at its glyph centre, elbow into the bullet). Top-level lists have no guide line and get no segments (Logseq-identical). 2px `--accent` `li::before` at the guide-line x with `pointer-events: none` (a pseudo hit-tests as its element; guide-line click-to-fold needs the UL as target). No state, no persistence, never `markdownUpdated`. Drawn only under `.app[data-threading='on']` — the dialog's Bullet threading › "Show" row (default On, `settings.bulletThreading` in the app state); the dialog's Bullet threading › "Line width" (1/2/3px) and "Line colour" (picker + Default = accent) rows feed `--thread-width` / `--thread-color` (GRO-2109). Caret semantics: the path is that of `selection.$from` — a NodeSelection on an item threads to its parent, a multi-item range threads the first item.

19. **Base files** — TOMBSTONE (GRO-2125, deleted in YAZ-844): `.base` is no longer a file the app opens. `BaseHost` (the main-pane host with its view/raw-YAML modes, its own autosave baseline and the "opening a base and doing nothing never writes" invariant) is gone, `Editor` has no `.base` branch: it dispatches supported files to their Markdown/text/PDF/image capability owners, while a `.base` on disk remains unsupported: invisible to the index, not openable in-app, not creatable — it lists in the tree with `kind: null` (YAZ-1577 D4) and clicking it hands it to the OS. What survives is the ENGINE, the views, `ViewsPane` and the schema types (`viewSchema.ts`'s `parseViews` / `serializeViews` / `updateViews`), all of them a folder's internals now: the views live in the folder's `.folder.md`, under `folder_settings`, and render on the folder's page, where the behaviour lives on ("Folders are the pages"). The 1B normalisation note travels with them — `yaml`'s `indentSeq` is document-global, so a views block mixing sequence-indent styles is re-indented to one style on its first REAL save. What the `.base` file's byte-identical round trip becomes here is narrower and stated honestly: every other frontmatter key of `.folder.md` survives, because `writeFolderSettings` goes through the one-key writer — but the settings block itself is rebuilt from the parsed value, so comments and hand-formatting INSIDE `folder_settings` do not survive an edit to it.

20. **Base embeds** — TOMBSTONE (GRO-2145, deleted in YAZ-844): `![[X.base]]` / `![[X.base#View]]` no longer renders anything. The whole `src/editor/baseEmbed/` folder — the `$prose` decoration plugin, the slot store, the `BaseEmbed` widget and `resolveBase`'s shortest-path resolution over `api.tree` — is gone, and with it the read-only embed chrome's only production caller — `ViewsPane`'s `readOnly` and `initialView` props outlived it by one unit and were amputated in YAZ-846 (see "View chrome"). An `![[X.base]]` left in a note is now just an ordinary embed whose target does not exist: the text stays byte-identical, nothing renders under it. A folder's page is where views are read now, and it is not embeddable anywhere else — one folder, one page, always its own notes ("Folders are the pages").

21. **`base` code blocks** — TOMBSTONE (GRO-2146, deleted in YAZ-844): a fenced block with `language === 'base'` is a plain CodeMirror code block again. `src/editor/baseCodeBlock/` — the `$view` replacement for `code_block`, its slot store, the `BaseCodeBlock` widget and the raw-YAML toggle — is gone, `createCrepe` no longer takes `baseCodeBlocks`, and every language now delegates to Crepe's stock block. The text on disk was always the exact code-block text, so nothing about any existing note changed except that the block stops rendering as a view. Views live in a folder's settings now, edited through the toolbar rather than through YAML by hand (rule 19's tombstone, "Folders are the pages").

22. **Typed-page scaffolds & the new-entity flow** — TOMBSTONE (Bible B GRO-2202, deleted in YAZ-836): the registry scaffold, `page_type`, per-type templates, the starter `All <pluralName>.base`, `createType` / `NewTypeDialog` and the sidebar's "New ▸ <type>" submenu are all gone; a new note is born in its FOLDER from that folder's `.template.md` and the caller's seed, and nothing else ("Folders are the pages": Births — `createNote`), and the still-valid vault-wide property declarations moved to `.yaseendocs/properties.json` ("Properties"). The number is kept because rules 23-26 and the `// rule N` citations in the code index into this list; `ensureFolder` is the one helper of `src/views/scaffold.ts` that outlived the type half.

23. **Wikilinks in the editor** (Links A, GRO-2190, `src/editor/wikilink/`): `[[target]]` renders Obsidian-live-preview style via DECORATIONS only (`wikilinkPlugin.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` — never a Crepe feature, the allowlist is untouched; never a schema/serializer change, so the text round-trips byte-identically — `roundtrip.test.ts` pins alias / heading / `#^block` / adjacent / inside-bold / unicode-and-spaces / unclosed-`[[` forms). Caret OUTSIDE the match: the `[[` `]]` brackets (plus `target|` before an alias and each `#`) get `wikilink__syntax` (CSS `display: none`); visible text carries `wikilink` (accent colour); `[[t|alias]]` shows only the alias; `[[t#h]]` shows `t > h` (each post-`#` segment gets `wikilink__sub`, whose `::before` draws the separator). An ID link (YAZ-2293 — the target is a note's `id` and the source resolves it) hides the id as well and shows the note's CURRENT TITLE — its file name without the extension — in its place: the ONE widget decoration here (`idLinkTitle` gives the title; the widget is keyed by it and wears the classes the id text would have worn), where every other decoration only hides or styles characters the document holds. The title is read off the index at each build, so a rename — by anything — changes what the link shows and not one byte of the document. `[[<id>#h]]` shows `Title > h`; a hand-typed `[[<id>|label]]` shows its label, never the title; an id NO note has stays the raw id, dimmed exactly like an unresolved name; and before the index has loaded an id shows raw and undimmed. `linkDisplayText(inner, titleOf?)` is that one mapping, shared with the backlinks snippets (rule 25). Resolved vs unresolved comes from the ONE resolver (`resolverFor`, see "Link resolution" — and, for a target no note answers, the FOLDER of that name: `linkResolver`, YAZ-2290 D10, "Links": Links to folders; such a link is resolved, opens the folder's tab on click and is never created): an unresolvable target dims via `wikilink--unresolved`; an empty target (`[[#h]]`) is a same-file link, never dimmed. A match whose DISPLAY would be empty (`[[Note|]]`, `[[|]]`, `[[#]]`) gets NO decorations at all and stays raw and editable (GRO-2197): hiding every part of it would erase the text from the page and leave no `.wikilink` span for the click handler either. Caret INSIDE or immediately adjacent (selection overlapping the match, boundaries INCLUSIVE): that match's decorations drop — raw editable syntax; this is also why `display: none` hiding is arrow-safe: the caret never crosses hidden text because at a boundary the match is already raw. An id link's title widget sits INSIDE the match (after the `[[`), so the same rule covers it: it is gone before the caret reaches the id, and the raw `[[<id>]]` is what is edited. Excluded: `![[…]]` embeds (they stay plain text — the base-embed widget that used to claim some of them died in YAZ-844, rule 20's tombstone) and code (`code_block` nodes and inline-`code` marks — mirrors the index's `stripCode`). Resolution reaches the plugin through a per-window `WikilinkResolveSource` (`createCrepe({ wikilinks })`): App owns one stable source and `WikilinkIndexBridge` feeds it from `useIndex`; an index update swaps the resolve function and pokes each subscribed editor with a META transaction — decorations recompute live, the editor never remounts, the doc never changes, `markdownUpdated` never fires. Interaction (Links C, GRO-2192, `wikilinkClick.ts` + `createFromLink.ts`, wired as `createCrepe({ wikilinkNav })` — App threads this window's tabs API + its notice setter through `Editor`): collapsed `.wikilink` spans get `cursor: pointer` and navigate on MOUSEDOWN with the default prevented — BEFORE the caret could land in the match and flash the raw syntax, so the reveal stays caret-adjacency/keyboard-only (exactly Obsidian's live-preview model: click navigates; to edit, place the caret adjacent and arrow in). Plain click → the CURRENT tab (`openCurrent` — an already-open path activates its tab); ⌘-click → a BACKGROUND tab (`openBackground` — appended, never activated, focus untouched; already-open → no-op); clicks on REVEALED raw text never navigate (a revealed match has no decoration span — that IS editing); Alt/Shift/Ctrl-modified clicks keep their defaults (future gestures — note ⌥ is now the right-panel key on a folder's Table/Board rows, YAZ-1557, so a future ⌥-click on a link should mean the same). An UNRESOLVED link CREATES its page first (`createFromLink`), then opens by the same gesture — except an unresolved ID link (YAZ-2293), which is NEVER created: an id names a note that existed, so a page called `<id>.md` would be a different note wearing its name, and the click says `That note no longer exists` through the passive link-notice and does nothing else (a RESOLVING id link opens from its title widget, which carries `.wikilink` and sits inside the match, so the hit-test finds it like any segment). For every other unresolved link: `|alias`/`#…` stripped (`linkPageName` — the ONE strip with the decorations); `[[#h]]` (empty target, same-file) is a no-op — the heading jump is GRO-2239; a bare target creates under the "Default location for new notes" setting (C2-, GRO-2240): `current` (default, YAZ-1643) → the SOURCE page's folder — the page the link was clicked or typed in, so a right-panel editor creates beside ITSELF, and a folder's outline as a note in that folder would (its `.folder.md` stands in as the source), never beside the main tab (a source outside the vault → the root), `root` → the vault root, `folder` → `<root>/<newNoteFolder>` — App resolves it (`newNoteBase`) behind a stable ref-backed `newNoteFolderFor(sourcePath)` getter threaded through `Editor` into `wikilinkNav` as `createFolder` (each editor binds its OWN path) and read at CLICK time, so settings changes land live without remounting any editor; a pathed `[[Sub/Page]]` (or `[[/Page]]`) is an EXPLICIT AIM: always root-relative whatever the setting (Obsidian's behavior), with missing parents — the base FOLDER of the setting included — created level by level (scaffold's `ensureFolder`); `.md` appended unless already markdown (mirror of `entryPath`); ALREADY_EXISTS just opens it (the creation race is benign); invalid names (per-segment `validateEntryName`) and create failures surface via the passive link-notice — never a dialog. Two more notices, both GRO-2197: a click landing in the PRE-INDEX window (no resolver yet, so every link still renders resolved and creating anything could shadow an existing note) is still swallowed, but now SAYS so instead of doing nothing at all; and a ⌘-click that CREATES a page names it in a notice, because a background tab is by definition not shown and the gesture otherwise looks like nothing happened. The watcher-driven index refetch then flips unresolved→resolved styling everywhere on its own (the live restyle above). **Right-click on a rendered id link** (YAZ-2293, `wikilinkMenu.ts`, registered with the click plugin on the same source and nav — so only when `wikilinkNav` is given): an id link hides its id, so this menu is where the id can be SEEN and copied — rule 26's `.ctx-menu.ctx-menu--editor` popup (`blockHandleMenu.ts` `renderMenu`), holding the note's name (a folder's reads `<name> (folder)`) and its id as two disabled label rows, then **Copy ID** ("Note ids": Copy ID). An id no note has, and any id before the index loads, has no name row; the id and Copy ID are still offered. The right-button MOUSEDOWN — and a Ctrl-click's left-button one, macOS's other right-click — is swallowed on an id link for the click handler's own reason: left to its default the caret lands in the match, the reveal rule drops the rendered span, and `contextmenu` arrives on raw text. The popup closes on a row click, an outside mousedown, a scroll, Escape, window blur and any doc change, one at a time. A NAME link, a view-only link, revealed raw text and plain text keep the native menu — nothing is prevented or drawn for them.

24. **The `[[` link picker** (Links B, GRO-2191, `src/editor/wikilink/wikilinkPicker.ts` + `.css`): typing `[[` in any non-code textblock opens a suggestion popup over every markdown note in the vault — and, since YAZ-2290 D10, every folder, its rows after the notes', reading `<name> (folder)` and inserted by id — by name when it has none ("Links": Links to folders) —; typing filters, ↑/↓ navigate (wraparound), Enter or click INSERTS PLAIN TEXT — `[[<id>]]` when the note has an `id` (YAZ-2293, **By id** below), else `[[name]]` — (replacing `[[fragment` up to the caret — never a schema/doc-structure change; the rule-23 decorations restyle it on their next pass, raw while the caret still touches it), Esc dismisses leaving the typed text — and that same `[[` stays closed while typing continues (a fresh `[[` reopens). Candidates come from the index through the shared matcher (`links/completion.ts` — the ONE `[[…]]` matcher with EditableCell's link editors). Matching is case-insensitive over the candidate NAME with the typed fragment trimmed at its ENDS (internal spaces stay significant), RANKED exact → prefix → substring and stable within each bucket, capped at 8 AFTER ranking (GRO-2197 — so `[[cac` puts the note actually named `CAC` first instead of whatever the path order happened to reach first). Ranking means the full candidate list is scanned every keystroke, which the per-candidate lowercase precomputed at construction pays for; the 5,000-record smoke in `completion.perf.test.ts` (the `perf` project) is the budget guard: `linkCandidates(records)` gives each note its SHORTEST unambiguous name — duplicate basenames follow the resolver's shallowest-depth rule, so the bare name belongs to the shallowest match and every other duplicate shows AND inserts its root-relative `folder/basename`. **Aliases** (E2, GRO-2214): a note with frontmatter `aliases` also gets ONE row per alias — typed as the alias, shown as `CAC — Customer Acquisition Cost` (the note half is that note's own unambiguous name, so duplicate aliases are folder-disambiguated by construction) and INSERTING the piped `[[Customer Acquisition Cost|CAC]]`, which targets the note and displays the alias. The piped form is exact whoever else claims the alias, so an alias row is unambiguous exactly like a folder-disambiguated duplicate; a row matches on its own name only (never on the note half of its label). Two alias rows are skipped outright (GRO-2197): one whose alias IS the note's own chosen name (it would read `CAC — CAC` and insert the pointless `[[CAC|CAC]]`), and one whose alias contains `[` or `]` (its piped insert would re-parse as the inner link and point somewhere else entirely). **By id** (YAZ-2293, 🔒): a note that has an `id` is offered under exactly the same rows — typed and read by its name, its `folder/basename`, or `Alias — Note` — but EVERY one of them inserts the plain `[[<id>]]`: no name, no pipe, so the link survives any rename or move, and rule 23 shows the note's current title in its place. The names above are then only what a row is TYPED and READ as; a note with no id keeps inserting them. A non-empty fragment nothing matches offers ONE "Create" row: it creates the page right there AND links it by the id it is born with (YAZ-2293) — the picker mints the id (`mintNoteId`), inserts `[[<id>]]` in place of the typed page name (a `#heading` typed after it rides along) and hands the same id to `createFromLink`, which passes it to `createFile({ path, id })` (YAZ-1357, 🔒 D3 revised — Yasin's ruling that a picked Create shows up in the sidebar at once: `createFromLink` under `nav.createFolder()`, the same placement as create-on-click — beside the page being typed in (YAZ-1643), no navigation, a passive `Created "…"` notice). When NO page is born with that id — a create failure, said through the same notice, or a page of that name the index had not shown yet, which is silent because the name resolves — the id in the document goes back to the typed page name, found by its text wherever typing has since pushed it. Without a nav there is no vault to create in, so the row only inserts `[[typed text]]` as-is. A hand-typed dangling link is still created on click (rule 23's Links C interaction); typing `|` is alias entry: the suggestions close and typing continues as plain text. Anatomy: a `$prose` plugin owns the state (session recomputed per transaction from doc + selection; candidate updates poke a meta transaction — same live-update pattern as rule 23) and the row DOM (`role="listbox"`/`option`, mousedown-preventDefault, theme tokens); `SlashProvider` (`@milkdown/kit/plugin/slash`) positions the popup at the caret and toggles `data-show` (its one-char `trigger` cannot express `[[`, so it runs a custom `shouldShow` over the plugin state; closing hides directly — the provider skips meta-only transactions). Keys ride `wikilinkPickerKeymap` (`$shortcut`, priority 100 — see "Keyboard"): every command declines (`return false`) while the picker is closed, so ↑/↓/Enter/Esc are NEVER swallowed then; the Enter tie with the outliner's priority-100 Enter breaks by addition order — `createCrepe()` MUST `use` this keymap before `outlinerKeymap` (`wikilinkPicker.test.ts` pins both directions). Per-window plumbing mirrors rule 23: `createCrepe({ wikilinkCandidates })`, App owns one `WikilinkCandidateSource`, `WikilinkIndexBridge` feeds it `linkCandidates(records)` per ready snapshot. No new GLOBAL hotkeys — the Hotkeys page (rule 16) is untouched.

25. **Backlinks — "Linked mentions"** (Links D, GRO-2193, `src/links/backlinks.ts` + `BacklinksSection.tsx` + `backlinks.css`; decision D of GRO-2096 and the GRO-2193 placement thread, both LOCKED). **Storage**: none — the referencing set is computed CLIENT-SIDE in ONE pass over the Markdown index snapshot the window already holds (`backlinksFor`), through THE shared semantic resolver (`resolverFor`, see "Link resolution"). View-only files never enter that snapshot or resolver and therefore never receive or contribute backlinks. No reverse map in the main process, no new IPC, no new `IndexRecord` field, and no watcher of its own: auto-refresh rides the existing 300 ms-debounced index refetch. A mention is a Markdown record's `links` **or** `embeds` entry resolving to the open Markdown path — an `![[embed]]` mentions its target exactly like a `[[link]]` — with the open note itself excluded, ONE entry per referencing note however many mentions it holds, path-sorted. A FOLDER's page mentions what its settings record links by the rename engine's rule (`referenceTargets`, never a second reading of `folder_settings`): its own link properties (`owner: "[[Alice]]"` in `.folder.md`), a view's `order` entry, a link column's `target`; and any link in a line of its outlines, prose included, as a note's body does (`folderMentionSnippets`). The folder is ONE entry — its `.folder.md` record, sorted by that file's path — and never lists itself. Alias-awareness is free (E2, GRO-2214): a note linking `[[CAC]]` IS a linked mention of the page whose frontmatter aliases it. So is id-awareness (YAZ-2293): a note linking `[[<id>]]` IS a linked mention of the note that id names. Results are memoized per (records array identity, path) in a WeakMap (`resolverFor`'s idiom), so one snapshot costs one pass per open path however many Markdown tab layers are mounted. **Placement**: a collapsible block near the BOTTOM of a Markdown note, INSIDE the editor's scroller — `.editor-host` holds `.page-title` (block ZERO, ⚡ YAZ-888), `.frontmatter-panel` (block ONE, ⚡ YAZ-883), `.editor-mount` (where the Crepe instance lands), the comment stream ("Comments", 🔒 D4), then the section, then "Reviews" (YAZ-2322) — so it scrolls WITH the note, Obsidian's in-document mode. No right panel, no sidebar section; one per Markdown tab. A FOLDER's page carries the same section as its last block, listing the notes and the folders that link to the folder (YAZ-2290 D10, `FolderView`); text/PDF/image dispatch before these hosts and mount none of it. **Header**: "Linked mentions (N)" with the view-group chevron (rotated −90° while collapsed), no hairline above it (YAZ-1680), COLLAPSED by default, N always visible — and N === 0 renders NOTHING at all (quiet over complete: Obsidian's in-document mode keeps an empty block, we do not). **Snippets, on demand**: an entry only mounts while the section is EXPANDED and reads its Markdown file from that mount (`fs:read`) — collapsed costs zero reads, and the index stores no positions. A snippet is the mention's own LINE — one snippet per line however many mentions it holds (GRO-2197) — whitespace-trimmed and windowed to ~120 chars around the FIRST mention with `…` on the clipped sides, at most 5 lines per note. The line reads exactly as the editor renders it: every wikilink on it is shown as its DISPLAY text through the shared `linkDisplayText` (`[[Note|CAC]]` → `CAC`, `[[Note#H]]` → `Note > H`, an id link → its note's current title through `idLinkTitle` and an id no note has → the raw id, exactly as rule 23 draws them (YAZ-2293), embeds and the empty-display forms of rule 23 stay raw), never the raw `[[…]]` source, and EVERY mention of the open note on that line is highlighted (`<mark class="backlinks__match">` per range, so a line mentioning it twice is one row with two marks; a mention pushed outside the window by the ~120-char clip drops, the centred one never does). Code is skipped through the rename engine's length-preserving `maskCode` (the index's `stripCode` mirror), and frontmatter lines scan like any other — the index reads links there too, though only as whole-value exact `[[…]]` strings, so a frontmatter line CAN highlight a mention the index did not count (GRO-2197 audit, deferred). A skeleton bar shows while a read is in flight; a FAILED read leaves the entry standing WITHOUT snippets — never a dialog, never a notice. A FOLDER entry reads nothing: its snippets are the bullets of its outlines that hold the link (`folderMentionSnippets` — the same `mentionSnippets`, over the outline text the index already holds), and none when it links only by `order` or `target`. **Live**: a fresh snapshot recomputes N (and drops the whole section when the last mention goes); an EXPANDED entry re-reads its snippets only when its own record's `mtime` moved — changed files only, so unrelated vault activity costs no reads. **Clicks**: plain → the CURRENT tab (`openCurrent`), ⌘ → a BACKGROUND tab (`openBackground`) — the same model as wiki-link clicks (rule 23) and sidebar rows; entry title and snippet rows behave identically (title = basename, tooltip = the full path; a folder entry is titled by the folder's name and opens the folder's page). **Collapse state** is per-component and in-memory: since every visited Markdown tab keeps its editor layer mounted (rule 4) it is effectively per tab for the session, and it is never persisted — an open backlinks block is not part of a note's identity. **Feed**: the per-window `WikilinkResolveSource` carries the semantic resolver AND the Markdown snapshot it was built from (`update(resolve, records)`, fed by `WikilinkIndexBridge` — one semantic snapshot feed for decorations, the Markdown picker rows and backlinks alike), while the view-only catalog remains a separate navigation-only source; the section subscribes only to the semantic source, so index updates never re-render App or remount an editor.

26. **Block handle menu + manual numbering** (YAZ-726/728/729/793/1329, `src/editor/blockHandleMenu.ts`, `outline/numberChildren.ts`, `outline/numberChildrenRow.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()`): right-click on the 6-dot handle opens a `.ctx-menu.ctx-menu--editor` popup mounted in the editor's own parent (no overlay; closes on outside mousedown, Escape, scroll, window blur and any doc change). Rows are DATA from a `RowProvider` called at open time with the probe target (`blockHandleTarget.handleTargetPos`, shared with multi-block drag) — the plugin never knows what a row does. First row: "Number children" ↔ "Bullet children" flips EVERY direct child list of the hovered `list_item` between `bullet_list` and `ordered_list` in one transaction (one undo step); it REPLACES the child list's marker type, never stacks a bullet and number on one row; grandchildren untouched; disabled (still shown) when the item has no direct child list. List commands NEVER read or write paragraph text — hand-typed same-line `* 1) Setup` is literal text under rule 8. **This is the only editor conversion command that creates numbering**: `createCrepe()` removes upstream's `1. ` `wrapInOrderedListInputRule` and `Mod-Alt-7` `orderedListKeymap`, and configures BlockEdit without the slash-menu Ordered List row; `- ` / `* ` bullet rules stay (E1). Explicit ordered Markdown still loads/persists because it is the command's disk format and valid imported/pasted content. Right-button mousedown/mouseup on the handle are stopped at document capture so Crepe's NodeSelection + focus never fire for a right-click.

27. **Drawing previews in the page** (YAZ-878, third build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852, `src/editor/drawing/drawingPreview.ts` + `.css`, registered in `createCrepe()` only when `opts.drawingPreview` supplies the vault root): an `![[Sketch.excalidraw]]` embed RENDERS AS THE SCENE. **DECORATIONS ONLY** (🔒 the same rule as 23): the embed stays plain markdown TEXT — no schema node, no serializer, no node view — so round trip is byte-identical (`roundtrip.test.ts` plus a guard in the plugin's own test). ProseMirror has no replace-a-range-with-a-widget decoration, so the mechanism is the honest pair the codebase already uses: a `Decoration.inline` over the whole match carrying `drawing-embed__syntax` (CSS `display: none`, `wikilink__syntax`'s exact mechanism) plus a `Decoration.widget` at the match START (`side: -1`, `contenteditable="false"`, `key` = target + state so PM reuses the DOM node and a settled preview never flickers). **REVEAL** is rule 23's, with boundaries **EXCLUSIVE** instead of inclusive: a selection strictly INSIDE the match drops both decorations (raw, editable syntax), while the caret sitting immediately before or after it keeps the preview — because YAZ-877's insert leaves the caret exactly there and an inclusive rule would hide the preview the insert exists to show. Boundary behaviour stays sane either way: an arrow key off an edge steps inside and reveals, and Backspace at the right edge eats the final `]`, un-matching the embed and leaving what a plain-text delete leaves. **ONLY `.excalidraw` TARGETS** (`isDrawingTarget`, case-insensitive suffix): every other embed (`![[img.png]]`), every link, and anything inside a code block or an inline-`code` run is untouched. The target goes to `readAsset` **RAW** — path or bare basename — because that is where the resolution rule lives (YAZ-876); the renderer never joins paths. **STATES**: *loading* is a quiet empty frame (no spinner — nothing to jitter); *ready* is the SVG inside that same frame (`max-width: 100%`, `max-height: 24em`, `cursor: pointer` only when a click handler exists) — one shared frame so the wait never jumps into the render AND an empty drawing, which exports to a near-nothing SVG, is still a visible clickable line; *broken* — missing file, unreadable bytes, not a scene, a render that threw — is a small INERT chip (“Broken drawing”) and the embed text **stays visible and editable**, because a drawing whose file is gone must remain a line the user can fix by hand. Nothing is ever repaired or rewritten. **THE DEPENDENCY** (`@excalidraw/excalidraw`, PINNED 0.18.1, the one addition of this unit): loaded ONLY through a dynamic `import()` on the first preview render (`drawings/renderScene.ts`), so the editor's main chunk never carries it and a note with no drawing embed loads none of it. 🔒 **OFFLINE**: the renderer must NEVER reach a CDN, and Excalidraw falls back to esm.sh whenever `window.EXCALIDRAW_ASSET_PATH` fails — so that path is set to the app's OWN copy of the package's asset folder (`app://yaseen/excalidraw-assets/`) BEFORE the module is imported. The copy is made at build time by `excalidrawAssets()` in `desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` (from node_modules into `out/renderer/`, and served from node_modules by a dev-server middleware) — never committed, and carried into the packaged app by electron-builder's existing `out/**`. Fonts are fetched at all only for scenes containing TEXT; a text-free scene asks for nothing. **REFRESH**: scenes are cached per target for the life of ONE editor mount; a `DrawingFeed` poke (`drawings/drawingFeed.ts` — the `WikilinkResolveSource` subscribe/poke idiom with no state, threaded as `drawingPreview.feed`) drops that target's entry and rebuilds the decorations, which re-read and re-render it. Since YAZ-879 `CrepeHost` creates exactly ONE feed per editor mount and gives it to both the preview plugin and the modal, so a save redraws every preview of that target in place — no remount, no reload, no document change. Without a feed previews still render, they just never refresh short of a remount. **CLICK**: a rendered preview calls `drawingPreview.onOpenDrawing(target)` and swallows the mousedown so the caret never lands in the match; Editor threads it to the modal (rule 28), and a mount that supplies neither wire keeps inert previews. CSS note: every rule in `drawingPreview.css` is scoped under `.milkdown`, because Crepe's own reset carries `.milkdown * { margin: 0; padding: 0 }` at equal specificity and loads later — an unscoped class silently loses all spacing. End-to-end in `desktop/e2e/drawing.spec.ts` steps 2-4 (previews render and the raw text is not duplicated; a drawn scene WITH TEXT renders with its font inlined from a `data:` URL and **zero** http(s) requests leave the app; a corrupted sidecar reopens as the broken chip with the note still byte-identical).

28. **The drawing modal** (YAZ-879, fourth build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852, `src/drawings/DrawingModal.tsx` + `drawingModal.css` + `ExcalidrawSurface.tsx` + `saveDrawing.ts`): clicking a rendered preview opens the scene FULL SIZE, edits it, and Save writes the sidecar back. 🔒 **A MODAL, NEVER AN INLINE NODE VIEW**: the embed is plain text under decorations (rule 27), so there is no node to host an editor in, and a drawing canvas inside the text column would fight the caret for every key it owns. It is React BESIDE the editor — the `PageTitle` precedent: `CrepeHost` holds `openDrawing: string | null`, the preview's `drawingPreview.onOpenDrawing` sets it, the modal is keyed by target and unmounted (never hidden) on close. 🔒 **ONE ENGINE SEAM**: `ExcalidrawSurface` is the ONLY component that mounts `<Excalidraw>`, the only place a scene is serialized, and the only place "changed" is defined; the chrome — overlay, Save, dirty, discard strip, error line — imports nothing but that component and its types, so YAZ-868's swap to yaseendraw rewrites one file. The boundary is pinned as SOURCE in `DrawingModal.test.tsx` (a runtime assertion would pass with a second hidden import). Everything is LAZY: the module through `renderScene.ts`'s one `loadExcalidraw()`, the stylesheet through the surface's own dynamic CSS import — the entry chunk gains only the chrome's own few KB, and a note with no drawing loads neither. ⚡ Every prop handed to the engine is STABLE (memoized `initialData`, ref-backed `onChange`, module-level `UIOptions`): `<Excalidraw>` is memoized and calls `onChange` whenever it renders, so a prop rebuilt per render is an infinite loop (React #185, caught in e2e). The modal keeps the latest snapshot in a REF and only its VERSION in state, so a drag costs one render rather than one per pointer move. **LOAD / SAVE**: `openDrawing` (`drawingScene.ts`) reads through `api.readAsset` with the embed's RAW target and keeps the resolved ABSOLUTE path and the read `mtime`; the save goes to `api.writeAsset` at that explicit path (writes are never fuzzy, 🔒 YAZ-876) with that mtime as `expectedMtime`. 🔒 **THE CANVAS WINS**: `writeProperty`'s CONFLICT dance with the one difference a scene forces — the re-read fetches nothing but a fresh guard (two scenes cannot be merged and the user is looking at theirs), then writes once; a second CONFLICT throws. **SHAPE ON DISK**: the library's own `serializeAsJSON(…, 'local')` — so element cleanup and the `files` filter (an image paste survives) are its rules — with two of ours: `source` stays `yaseen-docs` (pinned as `window.EXCALIDRAW_EXPORT_SOURCE` before the import, beside the font path) and a trailing newline, so a file YAZ-877 created and this saved back differ only in what was drawn. After a successful write the modal pokes the `DrawingFeed` (rule 27) and closes. ⚡ **KEYS ARE THE OVERLAY'S, NEVER `window`'s** — the YAZ-888 lesson `ConfirmRename` learned and paid for in e2e: React flushes mount effects INSIDE the dispatch of the event that opened the component, so a `window` listener hears the very click or keystroke that opened it, and this modal is opened by a CLICK. The overlay takes focus on mount so Esc lands before the canvas exists. **LEAVING** is one gesture with three triggers (Esc, click-away, ✕): clean → close; dirty → an INLINE confirm strip under the title bar ("Discard drawing changes?" / Cancel / Discard), never a second sheet stacked over this one, and Esc while it is up answers the strip. **SAVE IS DISABLED WHEN CLEAN** — the first snapshot the surface emits (at mount, from the loaded scene, before the engine volunteers one) is the baseline, and `getSceneVersion` is the comparison. **FAILURES ARE INLINE, NEVER A DIALOG**: a sidecar that is missing, unreadable or not a scene opens as the error state (message + close, nothing to write — reachable only by a race, since rule 27's broken chip is inert), and a failed write leaves the modal open with its error line and the canvas untouched. **THEME** is App's already-resolved appearance read back off `<html data-theme>` (`lib/theme.ts` `appliedTheme`), once, at open: the setting lives in App and threading a prop down to one transient modal is plumbing the feature has not earned. Known gap for the polish pass: a theme change while the modal is open does not follow. End-to-end in `desktop/e2e/drawing.spec.ts` step 5 (a preview click opens the modal on a real canvas with Save disabled; the rectangle tool is picked on the engine's own toolbar and DRAWN with real mouse drags; Save writes an element into the sidecar — still `yaseen-docs`, still pretty-printed with a trailing newline — the in-page preview redraws from the feed's poke, and reopening the saved drawing is clean and Esc-closes, with the note byte-identical throughout).

29. **Heading folding** (YAZ-1140, `src/editor/outline/headingFolding.ts` + `.css`, keymap `headingHotkeys.ts`): the heading-shaped twin of rule 5, registered in `createCrepe()` right after the bullet plugin. Foldable = an H1–H3 outside every list whose SECTION — its following sibling blocks up to the next heading of the same or a shallower level, or the end of the parent (blockquotes are walked; lists are skipped whole: the bullet chevron owns that gutter) — is non-empty. Each gets a widget `<button class="heading-toggle" aria-expanded>` at the start of the h element's inline content — INSIDE the h element, so it inherits the heading font-size and one `height: 1.3em` rule centres the chevron on the first line at every level — and collapsing adds `data-heading-folded="true"` to every section block (CSS `display:none`; an inner collapsed heading's widget sits inside a hidden block, so nesting compounds for free). Toggles are metadata-only transactions stamped `'heading-fold'` (`viewActions.ts`): never `markdownUpdated`, disk and mtime untouched. Fold keys = `h:` + the rule-5 scheme (`getOutlineFoldKey`) — a third key space beside fold and zoom keys — persisted in the SAME `folders[root].folds` bucket: `Editor.tsx` splits by prefix on load and writes the union of both plugins' latest reports, so neither kind can wipe the other's keys and rename/delete remapping rides the existing store path unchanged. ⌘↑/⌘↓ fold/unfold the innermost section at the caret (declining inside lists — rule 7's bullet handler owns those; ⌘⇧U/⌘⇧I stay bullets-only in v1); ⌘Z joins the view-action protocol, which YAZ-1140 made SYMMETRIC: every view-action plugin (fold, zoom, heading fold) drops its pending panic-undo when any OTHER kind fires, so ⌘Z reverts the single latest view action and a second ⌘Z can never resurrect an older one. CMD+F reveals matches hidden by collapsed sections silently and re-collapses them on its own `revealedHeadings` books (`findInPage.ts`); the block handle mutes over the chevron (rule 15); the bullets-only outline editor computes zero entries and stays inert. End-to-end: `desktop/e2e/headingFold.spec.ts` — chevron → section hidden → `h:` key in `yaseendocs.json` → quit → relaunch → restored, with a bullet fold coexisting in the same bucket.

30. **Inline `<br>` ↔ hardbreak** (YAZ-1452, `src/editor/inlineBreaks.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` right after underline): a `<br>` (any spelling, any case) is the hardbreak node, not an `html` atom — inside table cells it is the only line break GFM has, and Milkdown's `remarkPreserveEmptyLinePlugin` used to delete every one of them on parse (data loss on the next autosave). 🔒 **ORDER IS THE MECHANISM**: our `$remark` plugin runs BEFORE Milkdown's, which is removed and re-registered AFTER ours — never dropped, because `shouldPreserveEmptyLine` resolves its id to keep writing `<br />` for empty paragraphs. **LOAD**: a `<br>` becomes `break`; in a prose paragraph a `<br>` with nothing after it is dropped (CommonMark has no trailing hard break; remark would write a stray `\`), which is also what keeps the lone `<br />` blank-line marker round-tripping. **SAVE**: a hardbreak inside `tableCell` is written as `<br>` (remark's default writes a space there); elsewhere remark's default `\` + newline — so `a<br>b` in prose normalises to the backslash form (rule 6). **CELLS SERIALISE THEMSELVES**: `table_cell` / `table_header` `toMarkdown` feed the cell's inline content directly instead of going through the paragraph runner, because that runner always drops a paragraph's LAST hardbreak (a trailing `<br>` in a cell decayed one per save) and wrote `<br />` for an empty cell; several paragraphs in one cell are joined by a break, an empty cell is written empty. **ENTER AND SHIFT-ENTER IN A CELL** are our shortcut (priority 90 — above Milkdown's 50, below the wikilink picker's and outliner's 100 so an open `[[` picker still takes Enter): both always insert a hardbreak, a new line in the cell as in Google Docs (🔒 YAZ-1462). Milkdown's own Shift-Enter refuses to run inside tables, and its "second Shift-Enter after a break makes a new paragraph" rule split the table. Mod-Enter is still Milkdown's `exitTable`. Contract test: `inlineBreaks.test.ts` (19 cases, one row copied verbatim from the reporting doc).

31. **Highlight** (YAZ-1480, `src/editor/marks/highlight.ts` + the shared `marks/htmlPairs.ts`): ProseMirror mark `highlight` with ONE optional attribute `color`. **On disk**: `color: null` (yellow) is Obsidian's `==text==` — a vendored micromark tokenizer (gfm-strikethrough's attention run with `=`, exactly two: a lone `=` is text, `===` cancels, `\==` escapes) plus stringify `unsafe` rules: any `=` touching another `=` is written `\=` (`a == b` → `a \=\= b`, reads back as the same text; a lone `=` never; URLs never; an odd run escapes one short, `a === b` → `a \=\== b`, still stable). `postProcessMarkdown()` restores `=` inside `[[…]]` so a wikilink target keeps its bytes. `==` on its own line under a paragraph is still a setext heading. Two tight runs on one line pair up (`x==5 and y==6` on disk highlights `5 and y`, as in Obsidian); the typing rule is stricter than the parser (no `=` inside, no word character before), so the same string TYPED stays text and saves escaped. `green`/`blue`/`pink` have no Markdown syntax and store inline HTML `<mark class="highlight-<name>">text</mark>` — rule 11's `<u>` mechanism through the shared pair walk. A bare `<mark>` is read as yellow and written back as `==…==` (rule 6 normalisation); any other `<mark class/style>` is not this mark and passes through as inline HTML, bytes untouched — on PASTE every `<mark>` becomes the mark (unknown class → yellow): a pasted `<mark>` is a highlight. 🔒 A highlight nested inside a highlight ENDS the outer one (Milkdown's `ParserState` keeps one mark set) — lossy on the first save, stable after; a highlight inside a DIFFERENT mark is unaffected. **Toolbar** (`buildToolbar` in createCrepe.ts): four swatches (yellow, green, blue, pink), dots filled with the wash token, added into Crepe's own `formatting` group after Strikethrough — `getGroup('formatting')` THROWS if Crepe ever renames that group; `headingToolbar.test.ts` pins it. 🔒 D5, Bold's toggle rule: a dot is LIT when ANY selected text carries its colour (at a caret: the marks it would type with); pressing a lit dot removes exactly that colour, an unlit one applies it over any other colour; edge whitespace stays outside the mark; where marks cannot apply (code) the command declines and the key falls through. A lit dot is painted as a pressed button (`--crepe-color-selected`). `setHighlightCommand(color)` is the one command behind the swatches, `Mod-Shift-h` (yellow; a `$useKeymap`, so Crepe's `keymapRef` labels the yellow dot and its `aria-keyshortcuts` from it) and the `==x==` typing rule (yellow). Copy-out emits `<mark>`. **Styling**: translucent washes `--mark-bg` + `--mark-bg-<name>` in `:root` (one value per colour, both themes), text colour inherited, selector `.milkdown .ProseMirror mark` so every Crepe surface paints it. Not a Crepe feature (allowlist untouched).

**YAZ-1289 editor-link amendment to rules 23–25:** the editor still mounts these plugins only for Markdown source notes. Rule 23 consults the separate view-only source only when the raw target has a recognized explicit text/PDF/image extension; missing/loading/removed targets and heading/block suffixes remain unresolved and never run create-on-click, while `|display` is presentation only. Rule 24 composes explicit-extension catalog candidates with the Markdown candidates before the same matcher/ranker; recognized view-only target spellings suppress unroutable semantic rows, but semantic aliases whose display text matches remain. `WikilinkResolveSource.resolve` and `.records` stay the original Markdown feed by identity, so rule 25 and every other semantic consumer remain Markdown-only.

30. **Images, first-class** (YAZ-1656 — decisions D1–D8 and the demo learnings are comments on that issue; `src/editor/image/`): `![alt](src)` RENDERS from the vault, a pasted or dropped image is SAVED to the vault, and the picture can be resized, opened full-size and copied. 🔒 **THE ONE NODE VIEW** (`imageView.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` only when `opts.image` supplies the vault root + the note's absolute path): rules 23/27 forbid node views for wikilinks and drawing embeds because those are plain TEXT with no node to view; the commonmark `image` node ALREADY EXISTS, so a node view here changes no schema and no serializer and `![alt|300](src "t")` stays byte-identical (`roundtrip.test.ts`). The rule it establishes: a node view is allowed only on a node the schema already has and never touches the serializer. **SRC** (`imageSrc.ts`): anything with a scheme passes through untouched (`https:`, `data:`; a foreign `app://fs/…` simply 404s); a leading `/` is root-relative; everything else becomes `app://vault/<encoded root>/<ref, each segment decoded ONCE then encoded>?from=<note's root-relative dir>` — decode-once because a markdown src is already percent-encoded (Obsidian writes `%20`), so `case%20a.png` reaches main as `case a.png`, never `case%2520a.png`. Main (`vaultProtocol.ts`) resolves note-relative → root-relative → shortest-path basename, images only. **STATES**: loading is quiet; broken (missing, foreign, escaped, non-image) is a small INERT chip "Broken image: <src as written>" with the markdown still editable — nothing is repaired or rewritten. **WIDTH** is Obsidian's `![alt|400](src)` — alt is already preserved byte-for-byte, so no serializer work — parsed for display (`parseAlt`; `a|b` is not a width) and written back by the resize. On the `<img>` the width is the `--image-width` custom property (`width: var(--image-width, auto)` in `imageView.css`; no property when the alt carries none — the stylesheet's `auto` fallback), NEVER `style.width` — an inline width beats every stylesheet rule, and a stylesheet state must be able to override it without `!important`. **FOLDED CHIP** (YAZ-1709): a bullet holding an image folds like a parent (rule 5); the fold plugin's `data-outline-folded-image` node decoration turns the view into a one-line chip — one fixed text-height box for every image (1.75em wide × (line box − 4px) tall, so it follows the line-spacing setting; `object-fit: cover`; short enough not to grow the row), no label, handles hidden — and a click on the thumbnail unfolds the bullet. An expanded image inside a foldable item shows a corner fold button (`.image-view__fold`, top-left, on hover/selection; absent while broken — the broken chip has no button) rendered dumb by the node view and driven by the fold plugin. The node view itself knows nothing about folding: it sets the width property and renders the button, the stylesheet reads the decorations. **RESIZE**: eight handles (four corners, four edge midpoints), every one WIDTH-ONLY so the aspect ratio can never break (only a width is stored): west handles grow leftward, north/south map through the aspect ratio, a corner follows the axis that moved more; clamp `MIN_WIDTH` 40 px … the editor column; the width is live on the `<img>` during the drag and ONE `setNodeMarkup` on mouseup writes `alt|<w>` (⌘Z is one step); Escape mid-drag cancels (capture-phase, so the editor's Escape→sidebar hand-off never hears it), `window` blur ends the gesture where it stands, a plain click commits nothing, read-only editors have no gesture. `update()` patches width/alt/title in place and rebuilds the `<img>` only on a src change; `ignoreMutation` answers false to PM's `selection` pseudo-mutation. **LIGHTBOX** (`ImageModal.tsx`, rule 28's shape): double-click → a full-window overlay of the FULL-resolution file (`95vw/95vh`, `object-fit: contain`, alt as caption); Esc / backdrop / ✕ close; keys are the overlay's (YAZ-888); focus returns to what had it. It is a GALLERY of every image on the page in document order (folded ones included — they are still in the doc): the node view walks the document at double-click time and hands over `{ images, index }` (no registry of node views, so the list is always current and stateless), the modal owns only the cursor; ← / → and the bottom bar's arrows page, an "n / m" counter sits between them, NO wrap-around (← at the first and → at the last are no-ops, the edge arrow disabled), and a single image shows no bar at all — the old lightbox exactly. **PASTE / DROP** (`insertImage.ts`, wired in `clipboardPaste.ts` through the `imageOptionsCtx` slice, D6): image BYTES on the clipboard or in a drop beat whatever HTML rode along — a "Copy Image" from another app carries the PNG and an `<img src>` only that app can read, and the old behaviour kept the private src and lost the bytes (the reported bug). The file is written FULL-RESOLUTION, never re-encoded, to `assets/images/<note, whitespace → '-'>-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>.<ext from mime>` with `create: true` — never overwritten, a same-second collision retries `-2`, `-3`… — the sibling of `assets/drawings/` and root-relative so moving a note never breaks the link; the inserted node is `![<name>|400](assets/images/…)`, the `|400` (`PASTED_IMAGE_WIDTH`) only when the bitmap is wider than 400 px, so a screenshot lands at a manageable size and a 16 px icon is not blown up. Never in code (a fenced block is a CodeMirror node view that owns its own paste anyway); a drop over code or outside the document is REFUSED rather than passed on, because the next handler is Milkdown's upload plugin, which would inline the file as base64; a selected image becomes a caret AFTER itself before the insert (paste adds, never replaces); the first image file wins when several are dropped; any failure is the host's passive notice and nothing is inserted. HTML with a foreign `<img src>` and NO bytes is left alone (renders the broken chip; foreign URLs are never fetched, nothing is silently deleted). **WHERE IT IS NOT**: a folder's outline editor (its bullets-only lock has no `image` node — wiring options there would write the file and then filter the insert away) and the hover preview card (no vault root in reach) get no `image` options and keep Crepe's stock `<img>`, both commented where they mount. **DEFERRED** (YAZ-1673): `![[img.png]]` embeds (zero in the vault, paste never produces them, would need a second decoration renderer) and a per-vault attachment-folder setting (free later: links are root-relative). End-to-end: `desktop/e2e/imagePaste.spec.ts` and `imageRender.spec.ts` (fixture PNGs encoded at test time by `imageFixtures.ts`).

32. **Code blocks wrap and follow Appearance** (YAZ-2270, decisions D1–D4 are comments on that issue; `src/editor/codeTheme.ts`, wired in `createCrepe()`'s `featureConfigs[CodeMirror]`): every fenced block is Crepe's stock CodeMirror editor with two app settings. **WRAP**: `EditorView.lineWrapping` — a long line wraps at the column edge and breaks mid-word only when it has no space; never a CSS `white-space` override, which bypasses CodeMirror's line measuring. Display only: the markdown is untouched. A diagram wider than the column wraps too (accepted). The `overflow-x: auto` on `.milkdown-code-block` stays as the guard for tables and the rare unbreakable element. **COLOURS**: the app's own theme replaces Crepe's One Dark (built for a dark block, ~2:1 on the light one). Every colour is an `app.css` variable — the `--code-*` syntax tokens plus `--fg`, `--fg-muted`, `--accent`, `--bg-hover` — defined under `:root` and `[data-theme='dark']`, so Appearance re-colours code live with no remount (the `crepeTheme.ts` mechanism). ONE look for every language: no per-language styling (D2, dropped — untagged fences mix code, diagrams and prose). `crepe.ts` no longer defaults a theme, so `@codemirror/theme-one-dark` is not bundled. `codeBlock.test.ts` pins wrapping, the theme, and that every `--code-*` is defined for both appearances and used.

33. **Marks on line breaks** (YAZ-2280, decisions D1–D3 are comments on that issue; `src/editor/breakMarks.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` in place of Milkdown's `hardbreakClearMarkPlugin`, which is removed): a SOFT break is a newline inside a paragraph (`hardbreak` with `isInline`, shown as a space); a HARD break is `\` + newline. 🔒 **ONE RULE**, enforced by one `appendTransaction` on the span a transaction changed: a soft break keeps a mark only while the text on BOTH sides carries that same mark; a hard break never carries one. So a mark added across soft-wrapped lines runs through them as one span (`==two⏎three==`, `**two⏎three**`, `<mark class="highlight-green">two⏎three</mark>`) and the lines keep flowing, and a mark never starts or ends on a newline — Markdown cannot open or close one there, and the mark would be lost on reload with its markers left as text. The rule runs on every document change, not only when a mark is added: un-marking or deleting the text beside a marked break unmarks that break too. Adding a mark never changes a break's kind (the bug this replaced: Milkdown rebuilt each break with default attrs, turning soft into hard). Unchanged: hard breaks split a mark per line (`==a==\⏎==b==`), Shift-Enter inserts an unmarked hard break, and a file that already holds a multi-line mark saves byte-identical. Tests: `breakMarks.test.ts`.

## View modules

TOMBSTONE: this heading was "Bases modules" until YAZ-858.

Everything under this heading serves ONE surface — a folder's page (YAZ-2290; YAZ-844 retired `.base`, Editor rule 19's tombstone). The name no longer stays: YAZ-858 renamed the machinery to match what it serves — `client/src/bases/` → `client/src/views/`, `baseFile.ts` → `viewSchema.ts`, `BaseView` → `ViewsPane`, `.base-*` → `.view-*` — a mechanical rename with zero behaviour change. The property index, the query engine, the view chrome and the cell editors were still BUILT for Obsidian Bases and were not rewritten — they were re-pointed, then re-named. Where an entry below still says "base", it is a tombstone naming something the format took with it (or a stored key kept for compatibility, see `baseGroups` under App state); the surface that mounts them is "Folders are the pages", and the config they read is "Folder settings: `.folder.md`". Nothing here reads or writes a `.base` file any more, and no entry may describe one as current.

### Properties (YAZ-835, under the LOCKED rulings of YAZ-816 — `<root>/.yaseendocs/properties.json`, succeeding the Bible A registry)

**What it is**: vault-wide property DECLARATIONS — the ONE registry concept that survived the type system's deletion (YAZ-836). `{ version: 1, properties: { <name>: { kind, target?, required? } } }`, keyed by BARE frontmatter key — never a canonicalised `note.x`. `kind` ∈ `PROPERTY_KINDS` = text / number / date / checkbox / list / link / multi-link (`shared/types.ts`), `target` is a link/multi-link column's picker constraint (an opaque string here; its meaning is `belongsToBasenames`' — a wikilink to a FOLDER, see "Links": Links to folders), `required` is report-only metadata that gates nothing, exactly as it did in the registry. Read and written in main through the vaultConfig plumbing (`desktop/src/main/properties/`, Electron-free like the vault index); the IPC half is `desktop/src/main/ipc/properties.ts`.

**The version rule, unchanged from the registry** (LOCKED, R2.5): `version: 1` is readable AND mutable; a numeric `version > 1` reads best-effort (known fields consumed, no `error`) but refuses every mutation — writing a v1 shape over a newer file would destroy fields this version does not know. An ABSENT file is empty declarations with NO error, and reading NEVER creates anything: the file is LAZY, the first successful mutation writes the `{ version: 1, properties: {} }` skeleton along with the dotfolder. A MALFORMED file (unparsable JSON, non-object root, missing/non-numeric `version`) reads as empty + an `error` string, and every mutation rejects `INVALID_CONFIG` — the file is never overwritten and never moved aside, deliberately unlike `store.ts`'s `.corrupt-<epoch>` policy: app state is disposable, the user's schema is not.

**Surface**: `getProperties(root)` / `setProperty(root, name, decl)` / `removeProperty(root, name)` / `subscribeProperties(root, listener)`, over channels `properties:get` / `properties:set-property` / `properties:remove-property` / `properties:changed` (`CONTRACT.properties` in `shared/ipc.ts`), reaching the renderer as `window.yaseenDocs.properties.get/setProperty/removeProperty/onChange` (bridge table above). Targeted mutators, never a whole-file PUT (the state door's anti-clobber shape); every mutation is a serialised per-root read-modify-write (the store's write-chain idiom, so two can never interleave reads) that preserves unknown fields at every level for forward compat, and removing an unknown name is a no-op that never creates the file. `subscribeProperties` re-reads through the SAME chain, so notifications deliver in order; main holds ONE subscription per open-vault root (`AppState.windows`, dropped with the last window on it) and broadcasts the fresh declarations to EVERY window, which filter by their own root.

**Validation** (strict at the IPC boundary — a write is config, not content): names must match `PROPERTY_NAME` `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` (snake_case, ONE definition in `shared/types.ts` so no mirror can drift), a declaration must be an object whose `kind` is in `PROPERTY_KINDS`, `target` a string and `required` a boolean — everything else `BAD_REQUEST`. Reading is deliberately laxer than writing: an unknown on-disk `kind` string stays on disk and reads back as `text`. `page_type` is now an ORDINARY name that declares like any other (`properties/index.test.ts`) — the identity rule that reserved it died with the type system.

**The clean break** (LOCKED, YAZ-835): `.yaseendocs/types.json` is NEVER read. No migration, no converter, no read-old-write-new — a vault carrying one simply starts with no declarations, and the file is left untouched on disk. Pinned by a test: *"an old types.json is simply not read — properties.json is the only file this module knows"* (`desktop/src/main/properties/index.test.ts`).

**Not ours** (TOMBSTONE, ⚡ YAZ-815 — ruled by Yasin): `.obsidian/types.json` is another app's file — a different folder, a different owner, a different shape — and we no longer touch it AT ALL. It used to be read fresh per `index(root)` call (never cached), ride `IndexResponse.types` and hold a rung in the typing ladder one BELOW our own declarations; nothing ever consumed that rung, because the one surface that mounts cell editors reads the window's link snapshot instead of fetching an index. A stale foreign artifact feeding a rung with no feed is worse than no rung, so the whole chain — the read, the field, the parameter and the rung — is deleted. `.yaseendocs/properties.json` is the vault's schema; we never wrote `.obsidian/types.json` and now we never read it either.

Files: `desktop/src/main/properties/index.ts`, `desktop/src/main/properties/index.test.ts`, `desktop/src/main/ipc/properties.ts` (+ test), `client/src/views/useProperties.ts` (+ test), `client/src/views/propertiesStub.ts` (+ test — the in-memory `YaseenDocsApi['properties']` the client tests install as `window.yaseenDocs.properties`), `shared/types.ts` (`PROPERTY_KINDS`, `PROPERTY_NAME`, `FOLDER_NAME`, `PropertyDecl`, `PropertiesResponse`).

### Property index (GRO-2127 contract, GRO-2128 index — now in the Electron main process)

The query engine behind a folder's views reads notes through `IndexRecord`, never the filesystem. Transport: bridge method `index(root)` (Desktop D10), no HTTP anywhere — the index (`desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/`) is transport-agnostic (tests call `getIndex` directly); it moved, not rewrote, from the retired server process (GRO-2157).

Files: `shared/types.ts` (`IndexRecord`, `IndexResponse`), `shared/frontmatter.ts` (`splitFrontmatter` — moved from the client, which re-exports it — and `parseFrontmatter`), `shared/noteId.ts` (`isNoteId` — what `id` holds), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts` (`walk`, `scanFile` + pure `extractAliases` / `extractTags` / `extractLinks` / `extractEmbeds`), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/live.ts` (`getIndex`, cache + watcher), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/idSweep.ts` (`sweepIds` — "Note ids": The sweep), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/index.ts` (barrel), `client/src/views/testRecords.ts` (`TEST_RECORDS`).

`IndexResponse { root, records: IndexRecord[], folders: IndexRecord[], generatedAt }` — `records` is every `.md`/`.markdown` file under `root` (dot-entries and `node_modules` skipped, exactly like `tree(root)`), sorted by path; `folders` (🔒 D8, YAZ-2290) is every folder's settings file `.folder.md` — the ONE dot-entry the walk and the watcher let through — as the same `IndexRecord` shape, sorted by path, its `folder` naming the folder it configures, and NEVER among `records` (`getIndex` splits one record map by file name; pinned by `vaultIndex/folderSettings.test.ts`). Images and everything else are never records — nor were `.base` files while the format existed (YAZ-844), which is why retiring it cost the index nothing: no field, no `CACHE_VERSION` bump, no rescan.

| `IndexRecord` field | Value |
|---|---|
| `path` | absolute |
| `id` | OPTIONAL (YAZ-2293): the frontmatter `id` when it is a note id (`isNoteId`, `shared/noteId.ts` — 12 characters of lowercase Crockford base32 with at least one digit) — the note's permanent identity, see "Note ids". ABSENT when the note has none or its `id` is any other shape (someone else's value). The key is not removed from `properties`: it stays there as written, like any other. |
| `name` / `basename` / `ext` | `VSL-v1.md` / `VSL-v1` / `md` (`md` or `markdown`, lower-case, no dot) |
| `folder` | root-relative, `/` separators, `''` at the root (`Content Pillars/1. Agentic Agency`) |
| `size` / `mtime` / `ctime` | bytes / mtime ms / birthtime ms (ctime ms where the platform has no birthtime) |
| `properties` | frontmatter parsed with yaml's default **core** schema: `null`/booleans/numbers typed, dates stay strings (`date: 2026-08-01` → `'2026-08-01'`), `pillar: null` is present with value `null`. `{}` when there is no frontmatter, the block is not a map, or YAML fails — the latter two also set `frontmatterError` (one-line yaml message or `frontmatter is not a map`). Files over `MAX_FILE_BYTES` are stat-only (`{}`, empty lists, no error). The reserved `comments` key is DELETED right after the parse (YAZ-1472, 🔒 D5 — a note's comment stream is content, not a property), so it never reaches a record, the cache, a view column or a link source; see "Comments". |
| `aliases` | frontmatter `aliases` — extra NAMES the note answers to (Links E2, GRO-2214): a list → each string item; a scalar string → ONE alias (never comma-split, the deliberate contrast with `tags`); trimmed, empties and non-strings dropped, de-duplicated (first appearance). Absent / non-string → `[]`. Only the `aliases` key (Obsidian's), never `alias`; nothing is stripped from a value, so `aliases: ["[[X]]"]` is an alias literally named `[[X]]`. |
| `tags` | frontmatter `tags`/`tag` (list → each string item; string → split on commas/whitespace; non-strings ignored; leading `#` stripped) followed by inline `#tags` in order of appearance. Inline rule: `#` preceded by start of text, whitespace or one of `( [ , ;`, then `[A-Za-z0-9_/-]+` with at least one non-digit (`#123` is not a tag), case preserved, nested `a/b` kept whole. Never inside fenced code (``` or ~~~), inline code spans or `http(s)://…` runs. De-duplicated, first appearance wins. |
| `links` | `[[target]]` targets with `\|alias`, `#heading` and `#^block` stripped and trimmed — frontmatter string values (top-level and inside lists) that are exactly `[[…]]` first, then the body. Embeds are excluded, and so is everything under the `aliases` key whatever it looks like (GRO-2214: an alias is one of this note's own NAMES, never an outgoing link). De-duplicated. |
| `embeds` | `![[target]]` targets from the body, same stripping, de-duplicated. |
| `reviews` | Optional. The note's review log (frontmatter `reviews`, YAZ-2322) as `{ at, rating, text }` in time order; absent when never reviewed. Lifted OFF `properties`, like `comments` is dropped. |
| `text` | Optional. `textFingerprint` of the body (YAZ-2322): eight hex characters; a review records the one it saw. Absent when the file was not read (over `MAX_FILE_BYTES`). |

Tags, links and embeds share one code-stripping pre-pass (`stripCode` in `scan.ts`): nothing inside a fenced code block (``` or ~~~) or an inline code span counts, matching Obsidian's metadata cache; `http(s)://…` runs are additionally ignored for tags only. Frontmatter values are never code-stripped.

`TEST_RECORDS` (`client/src/views/testRecords.ts`) is the 8-note `bases-fixture` as records rooted at `/vault` with `size`/`ctime`/`mtime` zeroed; `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/live.test.ts` deep-equals the live scan to it, so client query tests and the main-process index are pinned to one truth.

Cache lifecycle (`getIndex(root)`):
1. First call: walk the root (`isSkipped` pruning — `.folder.md` excepted, YAZ-2290 D8 — markdown only, 32 reads in flight), `scanFile` each file, keep a `Map<path, IndexRecord>`; concurrent first calls for one root share the single scan. A missing / non-directory root rejects with the same `BridgeFailure` codes as the fs bridge (`NOT_FOUND`, `NOT_A_DIRECTORY`, `FORBIDDEN`).
2. The root's shared watcher (`watchers.subscribe`) keeps the map live: `add`/`change` on a markdown path → re-`scanFile` and replace (a file that vanishes before the scan is dropped), `unlink` → delete, `unlinkDir` → delete every record under it; `addDir` changes no record (the sweep gives that folder its `.folder.md`), `ready`/`error` are no-ops. No rescan is ever needed. The id sweep (YAZ-2293 — "Note ids": The sweep) rides both ends: once after the first build's reconcile, over every record and every folder walked, and NOT awaited, and after each `add`/`change` scan lands, over that one record; a write it makes comes back as an ordinary `add`/`change` and rescans the file.
3. Every call FIRST drains the watcher scans still in flight (YAZ-986: the answer is the truth as of the ask — a just-created file can never miss the snapshot its own `add` event provoked, which was YAZ-958's invisible-newborn bug; `liveDrain.test.ts` pins it by holding a scan open across a call), then returns the map sorted by path with a fresh `generatedAt` and resets a 10-minute idle timer; on expiry the watcher subscription is released and the map dropped (the next call rescans). Test hooks: `_evictAll()`, `_setIdleMs(ms?)`.

Persistent cache (GRO-2223, decisions D1–D4 — write GRO-2228, load GRO-2229): the record map additionally persists as one JSON per vault in `app.getPath('userData')/index-cache/<first 16 hex of sha256(root)>.json` (`desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/cache.ts` — Electron-free like `store.ts`: `main/index.ts` injects the dir via `initIndexCache`; the vault itself is never written, and cross-machine sync of the cache is a non-goal — the reconcile makes a foreign cache harmless anyway). Payload `{ version: CACHE_VERSION, root, records: IndexRecord[] }` (the constant is **5** today — see the load rules below); records whose frontmatter cannot round-trip through JSON (non-finite numbers — YAML `.inf`/`.nan` — or self-referential alias cycles) are left out of the write and simply rescan on the next start; the rest of the vault still caches. Persists are debounced ~5 s per root (`schedulePersist` — called from the watcher-incremental mutations, build success, and idle eviction as a natural last flush point) and land through the same `atomicWrite` as everything else, chained per root so writes never reorder; `flushIndexCache()` writes every pending root at once and joins `store.flush()` in the `before-quit` chain. The payload has ALWAYS been exactly those three keys and nothing else — pinned by `liveCache.test.ts` ("the persisted payload is version/root/records and nothing else"). `.obsidian/types.json` never entered it even while it was read per call, which is why ⚡ YAZ-815 deleting that read needed no invalidation and no CACHE_VERSION bump. GC (GRO-2230): `initIndexCache` sweeps the dir once per launch — ANY file (cache JSONs and crashed `.tmp-` leftovers alike) whose mtime is older than 90 days (`GC_MAX_AGE_MS`) is deleted, so vaults never reopened stop accumulating dead files. Every vault open refreshes its file's mtime (build success schedules a persist), so only truly abandoned entries age out, and a wrongly deleted cache is self-healing — exactly one full rescan. Deliberately minimal: no size caps, no LRU, no eviction policy of its own.

THE INVARIANT: the cache is only an accelerator — a wrong, missing, stale or corrupt cache can never produce wrong data. `loadIndexCache(root)` never throws; it answers `{ records, status }` with status `hit`, or degrades: `miss` (no file, or the payload names another root — a hash-prefix collision or a copied cache dir is simply not this vault's cache), `corrupt` (unparsable JSON, wrong shape, or any structurally malformed record — one bad element poisons the whole file), `version-mismatch` (numeric `version` ≠ `CACHE_VERSION`, now **5** — E2 added `aliases` to the record shape, GRO-2214; YAZ-1472 dropped `comments` from `properties`, see "Comments"; YAZ-2293 added `id`, see "Note ids"; YAZ-2322 added `reviews` and `text`). On build, `reconcile(root, files, load)` (`reconcile.ts`) turns the load into the live map: a non-hit is exactly today's full `scanAll`; on a hit it stat-sweeps every walked-and-cached file and reuses a cached record only when path + mtime + size all match (D2 — the record is its own validation key), `scanFile`-ing anything new or differing and dropping per-file stat/scan errors silently (same semantics as `scanAll`). The sweep is a sync `statSync` loop (~27 ms at 10k notes) and the cache load happens before `subscribe`, because the fresh watcher's initial scan floods the 4-thread libuv pool right at build time — async stats queue FIFO behind it and drain at the watcher's pace (~10× slower measured); warm 10k start lands ~99 ms vs ~1040 ms cold (GRO-2229 bench). A corrupt cache file is ignored IN PLACE (one `console.warn` at build) and overwritten by the next persist — deliberately unlike `store.ts`, which moves corrupt user state aside as `.corrupt-<epoch>` for forensics: the cache is derived data whose source of truth is the vault itself, so a corrupt copy holds nothing worth keeping, and move-aside would only accumulate junk for the GC to learn about (GRO-2230).
