RULE: any change to `IndexRecord`'s shape or the scanner's extraction semantics MUST bump `CACHE_VERSION` (`cache.ts`) — an old snapshot must never be read as if it had the new semantics; discard IS the migration (a mismatch degrades to one full rescan, there is no converter), and the fingerprint pin in `cache.test.ts` fails on such a change until the bump lands.

The reconcile also emits the cold-start diff for E1c rename detection (GRO-2242): `getColdStartDiff(root)` → `ColdStartDiff { root, scannedAt, cacheStatus, added, removed, changed }` (= the shared `ColdStartDiffResponse`, shipped to the renderer verbatim as the bridge's `coldDiff(root)`), held on the live entry and dropped with it on idle eviction. `added` carries ON-DISK stats and `removed` the CACHED stats — their (size, mtime) join is the rename signal; `changed` lists paths whose mtime or size moved. Miss semantics are honest: on any `cacheStatus` other than `hit` there is no before-snapshot, so all three lists are EMPTY (never "everything added") and `cacheStatus` says why — consumers must check `cacheStatus === 'hit'` before trusting them. Test hooks: `_resetIndexCache()`, `_setPersistDebounceMs(ms?)`, `_gcDone()`.

### Engine (GRO-2132 function library, GRO-2133 filters / sort / group / limit, GRO-2134 summaries)

`client/src/views/engine.ts` — `runView(def, view, records, { root?, resolve?, declared? }) → ViewResult { rows, groups, summaries, errors, total }`, pure and DOM-free, never throws. `RunOptions.resolve` (🔒 D2, YAZ-819) is the resolver every link in the run resolves through: absent, the engine builds one from `records` — which is the vault whenever the rows ARE the vault — while a folder's view passes only the folder's ROWS and injects the FULL-VAULT resolver, so a link cell pointing outside the folder still resolves ("Folders are the pages"). `RunOptions.declared` is the folder's declared column names (YAZ-1549): `propertyKeys` shows them before any note carries a value, which is what lets a column exist without being stamped into notes (🔒 E1, YAZ-2290). Pipeline: filters → values → sort → limit → group → summaries. A record passes when the base-level `def.filters` AND the view's `filters` both pass; `and` / `or` / `not` nest (`not` = none of the children true); a leaf is truthy per `isTruthy` (an ErrorValue fails). Every filter string is compiled once (`compile()` cache); a compile error or a malformed node makes that whole filter reject every row and adds one `EngineError { where, message }` — `where` is `filters` / `views[i].filters`, with `[j]` per child (`views[0].filters[1][0]`). `Row { record, file: FileValue, values }` — `values` is keyed exactly as the view writes the key (`view.order`, else `propertyKeys`): `file.x` is the file field, `formula.x` evaluates the formula (a compile error → ErrorValue cell + one `EngineError` at `formula.x`; an unknown formula is the evaluator's `unknown formula x` ErrorValue with no report), anything else is the note property (`note.` prefix optional) through `fromYaml`. Sort: `view.sort` in priority order, type-aware (numbers, dates/durations by ms, strings `localeCompare` base-insensitive + numeric, booleans false < true, lists by first element, links by what they READ as (`linkText` — a label, else the title of the note an id link names, else the target; YAZ-2293 D8), mixed types by rank), null / missing / ErrorValue always last whatever the direction, path as the final tiebreak. `limit` applies after sort and before grouping; `total` is the pre-limit count. `groupBy` → one `Group { key, label: render(key, resolve), rows, summaries, fannedOut }` per distinct key (`equals`; the label reads an id link as its note's title while `key` stays the stored value — YAZ-2293 D8), ordered by `groupBy.direction` with the same comparator; null / `''` / empty list / ErrorValue rows go to a trailing `{ key: null, label: 'No value' }` group that is always last; no `groupBy` → `groups: null`. **Two levels** (YAZ-745, YAZ-1097/1098): `groupBy` is an ordered LIST of levels, OUTER FIRST — `groupByLevels` reads the single-object form as a one-element list, so the old spelling stays valid and byte-identical in behaviour, levels past the second are ignored in v1, and a level with no `property` string is not one. With two, the outer level buckets exactly as above and each outer group is then bucketed AGAIN by the inner one: `Group.children` (its inner groups, ordered by the inner level's own direction; a child never has children of its own) and `Group.direct` (the rows sitting straight under the outer) appear on EVERY group of a two-level run — possibly empty, and absent entirely in single-level mode, which is how a consumer tells the two apart — while the outer's own `rows` stay the WHOLE branch, `direct` plus every child's rows, so counts and summaries above it are unchanged. **The merge rule**: an inner key that `equals` its outer's is no child at all, its rows are `direct` — grouping problems by top-function then function, a function that IS its own top shows its rows straight under the section instead of under a header repeating the name — and a "No value" OUTER puts its value-less rows in `direct` too, so it never carries a "No value" child. Everything else applies PER LEVEL and last within its own branch: each outer gets its own trailing "No value" child, and fan-out stays one flag per level (YAZ-671's semantics unchanged — one list anywhere inside marks every inner group). **Fan-out** (YAZ-671 D1): a LIST value joins the entry to one group per DISTINCT non-empty element (`equals`, no resolver — so link identity is the exact target, and two spellings of one note stay two groups), never a combination group; empty elements drop and a list left with none falls to "No value" like `[]` already does. `Group.fannedOut` is true on EVERY group of such a grouping (the "No value" group included) and false otherwise — it is what the group-derived writes read. A fanned-out row is counted in each group it joins, so per-group summaries can sum ABOVE `total` while the footer stays de-duplicated over `kept` (D2, deliberate). `groupKeyOf` keys shift from the rendered list to the rendered element, so collapse state persisted against an old combination group simply never matches again — orphaned, harmless, no migration. `this` is ALWAYS null: the views are a FOLDER's and a folder is no record (YAZ-2290), so the engine takes no "this file" option any more. `propertyKeys(def, view, records, declared?)` is `view.order` if set, else `file.name` + every note key seen or declared (sorted, as `note.<key>`) — bar `id` (YAZ-2293) and `also_in` (YAZ-2290 D2), which are never default columns and which a view can still add; `propertyLabel(def, key)` is `def.properties[key].displayName` (looked up as written, bare and `note.`-prefixed) else the key without `note.`. Budget: 1000 records × 3-clause filter + 2 formulas + sort + groupBy under 50 ms (`engine.perf.test.ts`, the `perf` project) — a SECOND grouping level fits inside it (two levels measured at 4.1 ms), which is why the scalar path stayed a single lookup rather than wrapping every value in a throwaway list.

Link resolution (`makeResolver(files, root?, opts?)`, also `Scope.resolve`): a target matches FIRST by a note's own `id` (YAZ-2293 D5 — `IndexRecord.id`; first, so a note merely NAMED like an id never captures it, and of two notes still sharing an id the first in path order answers), then by absolute path, then root-relative path (with or without `.md` / a leading slash), then bare basename — duplicate basenames resolve to the SHALLOWEST folder (Obsidian's shortest-path rule, GRO-2190), equal depth to the first in path order — and finally, LAST, a frontmatter ALIAS (Links E2, GRO-2214): a real name always beats an alias, whatever their depths, and two notes claiming one alias follow the same shallowest-then-first-in-path-order rule. An alias is a bare NAME, so it never joins a folder path (`[[Costs/CAC]]` does not resolve through the alias `CAC`). Case-insensitive throughout, `[[…]]` / `|alias` / `#heading` stripped. `opts.aliases: false` builds a NAME-ONLY resolver and `opts.ids: false` one that resolves no ids (YAZ-2293 D5) — the rename engine's referencing-set probe passes both (see "Links": Rename), the one consumer that must NOT see aliases or ids. `resolverFor(records, root?, opts?)` is the memoized accessor (WeakMap keyed on the records array identity, then per root, alias mode and id mode): everything reading the SAME snapshot gets ONE resolver — a refetched index is a new array and gets a fresh one; the per-resolver target cache is unchanged. Precisely (GRO-2197 audit — the earlier wording overclaimed; amended by YAZ-819/844): the resolver is one IMPLEMENTATION everywhere and the wikilink surfaces genuinely do share one INSTANCE, because `WikilinkIndexBridge` is the window's single index feed for decorations, the picker and backlinks alike (rules 23/24/25). The three surfaces that used to duplicate that feed — `BaseHost`, `BaseEmbed` and `BaseCodeBlock`, each with its own `useIndex` and therefore its own snapshot array and memo entry — died with the format (YAZ-844), and the ONE surface that runs views today reads the window's `WikilinkResolveSource` instead, so `useIndex` has exactly ONE caller left (`WikilinkIndexBridge`) and one window makes one fetch: the duplicated FETCHES are closed. The duplicated INSTANCE is not, and the reason is worth knowing — the wikilink feed built `resolverFor(records, root)` while the views built `resolverFor(records)` with no root, and the memo is keyed per records identity THEN per root and alias mode — so the same snapshot handed out two resolvers, and a link target written as an absolute `<root>/…` path resolved for the wikilink surfaces but not inside the views. CLOSED in YAZ-846: the root is threaded into every views resolver (`ViewsPane`'s injected `RunOptions.resolve` and the link pickers' in `TableView` / `CardsView` / `ListView`), so one window on one snapshot now holds ONE note resolver instance and one set of semantics. Since YAZ-2290 D10 the wikilink feed's own `ResolveLink` is `linkResolver` (`links/folderLinks.ts`), which wraps that same memoized instance and falls back to a FOLDER when no note answers; the engine's resolver answers notes only. A null root — a mount that does not know one — still degrades to name-and-relative-path resolution, which is the same resolver the engine would have built for itself. It backs the global `file(path)`, `link.asFile()`, and tightens `file.hasLink()`, `link.linksTo()` and link `==` to compare resolved paths — an unresolved side (e.g. `levels.png`, not a note) falls back to the textual `linkTargetsMatch`. Without a resolver `file()` / `asFile()` stay ErrorValues and matching is textual. List helpers `sum() mean() median() min() max()` ignore non-numbers and give null on none.

`client/src/views/summaries.ts` — `summarize(kind, values, custom?)`: built-ins (`BUILTIN_SUMMARIES`, case-insensitive) `Count` / `Empty` / `Filled` / `Unique` on any type (`isEmpty`, `equals`), `Average` / `Max` / `Median` / `Min` / `Range` / `Stddev` (population) / `Sum` on numbers (non-numbers ignored, none → null), `Earliest` / `Latest` / `Range` (→ duration) on dates, `Checked` / `Unchecked` on booleans; a non-built-in kind is looked up in `custom` (= `def.summaries`) and evaluated with `values` bound to the column (`Scope.extra`), else `ErrorValue('unknown summary kind: …')`. `runView` fills `result.summaries[prop]` over the post-limit rows and `group.summaries[prop]` per group from `view.summaries { prop: kind }`.

### View chrome (GRO-2135)

`client/src/views/ViewsPane.tsx` props: `{ parsed, onChange, root, folderPath, records, properties?, onOpenFile, folder }` — `root` keys view state persisted outside the page AND roots the resolver (see "Engine"), `folderPath` is the FOLDER's directory path (it keys the collapsed-group store, YAZ-2290), `records` is the folder's ROWS — the notes in it and its shortcuts — `properties` is the vault-wide declarations for 5B editor inference (typing rung 2, wired in YAZ-846; a `properties.error` renders its own passive "Could not load the vault's property declarations" line, never a fetch failure), and `folder` is the `FolderHost` bundle `FolderView` passes (settings + whole-vault snapshot, records and folders + create + the column doors + background/right-panel open, 🔒 D3 of YAZ-818 — see "Folders are the pages"), REQUIRED since YAZ-846. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-846, the amputation): `readOnly` (the retired embed chrome, which also gated cell editing, column resize, summary picking and group drag in `TableView` / `BoardView` / `CardsView` / `ListView` — gone from all four), `initialView` (which picked the starting tab for `![[X.base#View]]`), `types` (typing rung 3, `.obsidian/types.json` — nothing on this surface ever fed it, and ⚡ YAZ-815 then deleted the rung, the parameter and the read), `indexStatus` / `indexError` (the host's snapshot is always in hand) and the plain 5D `createFromSeed` path all had NO production caller once YAZ-844 retired `.base`, and were deleted rather than carried. `newNote.ts`'s `targetFolder` went with that last one. **Write-through**: every config change is `onChange(updateViews(parsed, mutate))` (re-parsed via `parseViews(serializeViews(…))` so sequential edits inside collections a previous edit created keep working — `doc.setIn` stores plain JS values), exactly one `onChange` per interaction, no Save button; the host turns it into exactly ONE `folder_settings` write through the one door. `onOpenFile` is threaded App (`openFile`) → `Editor` → `FolderView` → `ViewsPane`, and the host feeds `records` (the folder's rows) plus the whole-vault snapshot off the window's `WikilinkResolveSource` rather than a `useIndex` of its own. Component state is transient view state only — the active tab, the search text, the collapsed group keys (mirrored into the main-owned `baseGroups` store, see 4C), the pending optimistic moves (5C) and the last move/create error — everything else lives in the file. The body switches on `view.type`: `table` → `TableView`, `board` (OUR schema extension, D1 — not Obsidian's, and its keys round-trip untouched) → `BoardView`, `cards` → `CardsView`, `list` → `ListView`, `outline` → `OutlineView` (the folder's free-form document, always the FIRST outline view's), anything else (unknown types) → the 4A placeholder row list. One narrowing rides here: while an outline is showing the toolbar offers no Properties, Filter, Preview or Search (`documentView`) — see "Folders are the pages": The outline is a document.

Toolbar (`view/Toolbar.tsx`, `.view-toolbar`): left `view/ViewTabs.tsx` — one `role="tab"` per view (type glyph + name, active tab accent underline). The tabs are EDITABLE (🔒 D0, YAZ-1471, re-ruling 🔒 rule 4 of YAZ-819 and the "view CRUD is not this block's gesture" clause of 🔒 D3 of YAZ-818 — the whole editable half was behind a `readOnly` prop `ViewsPane` set UNCONDITIONALLY from YAZ-846 until YAZ-1471, kept whole and pinned but unreachable; the prop is gone from `ViewTabsProps` and the "…" button never returned, right-click being the one menu gesture). EVERY tab gesture is ONE `ViewsPane.update` — the same `onChange(parseViews(serializeViews(updateViews(…))))` door sort and columns use — and therefore ONE `folder_settings` write; which view is ACTIVE stays session state and switching still writes nothing. **Reorder** (D1): HTML5 drag, the insertion-slot idiom spelled ONCE for both horizontal strips in `client/src/lib/dragSlot.ts` — `insertionSlot(e, i)` (the target's own horizontal midpoint: before it, or after it) and `dropIndex(from, slot)` (past the grab point the slot shifts one left) — shared with the window's tab bar so the two strips cannot drift apart; the strip's empty tail is the END slot (the `e.target !== e.currentTarget` guard), a drop back on the item's own slot writes nothing, the ACTIVE view FOLLOWS its tab (the same move replayed over the indices, never a remembered name), and the in-flight indicators are `view-tab--dragging` on the grabbed tab plus `view-tab--insert-before` / `view-tab--insert-after` on the slot's neighbour. The drag payload is the PRIVATE mime `application/x-yaseen-view-tab` (TabBar's `WORKSPACE_PAGE_MIME` idiom) and never `text/plain`, which a tab mis-dropped on an editor would have ProseMirror insert as text; `dataTransfer` is guarded throughout for jsdom. **The view menu** (D2): right-click on any tab opens the shared `ContextMenuSurface` — Rename (the tab becomes an inline `TextField`, `normalize` committing only a non-empty name NO OTHER view answers to, so a duplicate or an empty draft snaps back and writes nothing, and a tab in rename is not draggable) · Duplicate (`structuredClone` of the whole config inserted right after, named by `freeName` off `<name> copy`, and selected — greyed for an `outline` view, because a folder owns ONE document) · Delete (greyed on the last view — `views` can never be empty — and never immediate: `view/ConfirmDeleteView.tsx` is the in-app sheet, on `ConfirmSheet` like its siblings, with the confirm `--danger` because a view's configuration has no way back, focus starting on Cancel, Esc and click-away cancelling; its copy is the pure `deleteViewMessage(view)`, tested apart). The open menu and sheet hold INDICES, so they are guarded against a list shrunk under them by another window or a hand edit (YAZ-1488): `menuView` / `confirmView` are derived in render and a stale index renders nothing rather than throwing through a block with no error boundary above it. **"+"** (D5): the button at the end of the strip opens an anchored `Popover` type picker over `VIEW_TYPES` (`table` · `board` · `cards` · `list` · `outline`, each labelled by `viewTypeLabel` — the type capitalised; `outline` is offered only while the folder has NONE, for the same one-document reason) — picking a type APPENDS a view named `freeName(<label>)` and mounts it in rename, so the two writes are the append and (only if you type) the name. **Overflow** (D6): `.view-tabs` IS the scroller and wears the shared `.scroll-strip` utility (`app.css`) — no scrollbar, a scroll-driven edge fade — with the active tab `scrollIntoView`n on every activation and labels ellipsized at 180px; the "+" sits OUTSIDE the scroller in `.view-tabs-wrap`, always visible and never masked. Right (YAZ-1397): **Sort** (badge = sort count + grouping-level count), **Properties**, **Filter** (badge = `countRules(view.filters)`, button and badge red for filter errors), the **groups toggle** when grouped, **Preview on hover** for Table/Board, **New**, **Search** (inline input; filters rendered row values case-insensitively without writing), then `N items` or `shown / total items`. While the outline — a document, not rows — is showing, Properties, Filter, Preview and Search are not offered (`documentView`). The groups toggle (YAZ-744) is ONE button with no badge: any group expanded → "Collapse all groups", all collapsed → "Expand all groups"; a click replaces the whole collapsed set for the view — every group the view HAS, the ones an active search hides included — through the same `baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]` entry the per-group chevrons write, never the folder's settings file. Popovers (`view/Popover.tsx`, `.view-popover`) anchor under their trigger, close on Esc / click-away, move focus in on open. `view/TextField.tsx` commits on Enter/blur only (one change per edit), Esc reverts.

The Filter menu (deleted in YAZ-846, RESTORED in YAZ-1218 / YAZ-1226-1229). `view/FilterMenu.tsx` is the builder behind the **Filter** toolbar button: conjunction All / Any / None → `and` / `or` / `not`, property · operator · value rule rows, an Advanced raw-expression input, and the engine's `*.filters` `EngineError`s listed at the top of the popover (`.view-menu__errors`) with the button turned red — the same errors still ride the muted footer note. Its rule ↔ expression model is the pure `view/filterRows.ts` (`ruleToExpr` / `exprToRule` / `inferType` / `operatorsFor` / `toGroup` / `fromGroup` / `countRules`), back beside `view/keys.ts`'s `canonicalKey` (`status` → `note.status`; `file.x` / `formula.x` / `note.x` untouched; non-identifier names as `note["…"]`). What did NOT come back, deliberately (D1; 🔒 Q3, "Folders are the pages": What a folder shows): the This view / All views scope segment — the menu edits `view.filters` ONLY, because a folder's set IS what lives in it and `def.filters` has no persistence door on this surface (a write would round-trip in memory and vanish on the next index rebuild). `inferType` now defers to the typing ladder (D4): the column's assigned kind, else its dominant kind, else the value walk — so the operator list always agrees with the cell editor beside it, `link` / `multi-link` columns included. Five operators take a value CHECKLIST instead of a typed box (🔒 D5, YAZ-1467): single-value columns (text, link, select, `file.folder`) offer **is any of** / **is none of**, written `["a", "b"].contains(<prop>)` and its `!` form — the list literal, never the string `containsAny`, because only the list method compares by equality; list columns (list, tags, multi-select, multi-link, `file.tags`) offer **has any of** / **has none of** / **has all of**, written `<prop>.containsAny("a", "b")`, its `!` form, and `<prop>.containsAll("a", "b")`. `file.links` stays out (link-vs-string equality unverified) and the engine needed no change. A `Rule`'s `value` is therefore `string | string[]`, and moving to or from one of the five resets it to `[]` or `''`. A stored node the builder cannot represent renders read-only as `.view-rule__code` and is never destroyed.

Sort menu (`view/SortMenu.tsx`): `view.sort` rows (searchable property chooser, ASC/DESC toggle, drag grip, remove — an empty list deletes the key) + "Add sort" (`file.name ASC`); beneath, **Group by** edits BOTH levels (YAZ-745): the first chooser is the outer level, a **Then group by** row beneath it (offered only once an outer is set, its options omitting the outer's own key) sets the inner — one level keeps today's single-object form, two write the ordered list, clearing the inner collapses back to the object, "None" on the outer clears both, and an outer moved onto the inner's property drops the inner (the same property twice is not a grouping); rendering of groups is 4C. Properties menu (`view/PropertiesMenu.tsx`): checklist over the shown keys (as written, so `view.order` round-trips) plus every other known key (`view/properties.ts` `allPropertyKeys`: shown + `file.name` + note keys + the folder's DECLARED columns — YAZ-895, so a declared column is offerable before any note carries a value — + formulas, deduped canonically); toggling/reordering writes `view.order` (shown keys only, in order; Table and Board views may hide `file.name`, while Cards and List keep their existing filename/title behavior); reordering is the 6-dot grip at the head of every SHOWN row — `file.name` included, hidden rows gripless — dragged with TabBar's midpoint insertion rule or nudged one step via ArrowUp/ArrowDown on the focused grip, always exactly one `writeOrder` per move so `frozenColumns` keeps following positionally (YAZ-1207; the ↑↓ arrow buttons are gone); for `type: board` views every shown note row additionally carries four card-style toggles (YAZ-1206/YAZ-1217) — bold, underline, hide label, and ⤴ join-to-the-row-above — while the shown `file.name` row carries ONLY ⤴ (the title takes part in layout, never text styling); each click is ONE write to `view.cardStyle[<canonical key>]`, with false flags, emptied entries and an emptied map all deleting themselves; the pencil sets `def.properties[key].displayName` (looked up as written / bare / `note.`-prefixed, written bare for note keys; empty deletes the displayName and an emptied entry/map). A Table-only **Frozen columns** select writes optional `view.frozenColumns`: None deletes it; every other option names one visible column and freezes the contiguous leading prefix through it. The number is positional, never a remembered key set — reorder changes which columns occupy the prefix, restore/new columns append outside it, and hiding clamps and persists it. `frozenColumnCount` is the one tolerant reader: absent, malformed, negative or non-finite → zero; fractions truncate; the effective count never exceeds visible columns. A Board-only **Board → Column width** row is the ONE Board-wide numeric editor, with `px` visible beside it: 280 by default, `min=180`, step 1 and no arbitrary maximum. Only a CHANGED finite draft commits, on Enter or blur, rounded to the nearest integer and then lower-clamped to 180; Enter followed by blur still commits once. Normalized 280 deletes `cardSize`, while every other valid width persists as a NUMBER through ONE `folder_settings` write. Escape, empty or non-finite input, and an unchanged or normalized-unchanged value write nothing. The field displays existing numeric and legacy small/medium/large values through the unchanged `cardWidth` resolver, and open/focus/blur of an untouched value never rewrites it — including a legacy numeric width below 180. Columns are DECLARED here too (YAZ-896/897): every `note.*` row carries a kind select — `auto` (a disabled placeholder, never a choice) while undeclared, meaning the ladder's lower rungs decide — writing `folder_settings.columns[<bare>].kind` through `FolderHost.setColumns`, the declarations' one door beside `Mutate` (ONE settings write, `views` untouched; C1, LOCKED: note VALUES are never migrated or rewritten by a retype — the declaration alone moves, so retyping is always reversible); a declared link/multi-link column additionally shows its per-folder `target` inline (empty deletes the key), and a trailing **+ Add column** (name per `PROPERTY_NAME`, kind, target for link kinds only, duplicates and bad names refused inline with nothing written) declares the column AND appends `note.<name>` to the view's `order` in that SAME single settings write — `setColumns`' optional `views` argument exists precisely so this is never two. Declaring a column writes NOTHING into any note (🔒 E1, YAZ-2290 — "Folder settings: `.folder.md`": No empty keys in notes). For `type: list` views only, a trailing **List** section edits how those properties display — `markerStyle` (Bullet / Number / None select), `indentProperties` (checkbox) and `propertySeparator` (TextField, placeholder `, `) — one write per change, the default value DELETES the key (bullet / unchecked / empty-or-`, `), like SortMenu clearing `sort` / `groupBy`.

Searchable controls (YAZ-1395/1396): `ColumnSearch` matches trimmed, case-insensitive substrings across display labels, written keys and canonical keys. The Properties header stays visible while scrolling, reports match count and hides reorder grips during filtering (clear search to reorder). `ColumnPicker` reuses the search field for Sort, Group by and Then group by; its bounded list expands inside the existing menu, shows labels/keys and the selected option, and supports arrows/Enter, Escape, click-away and normal focus traversal. Typing, canceling or choosing the current canonical value does not write. Existing candidate sources remain: Properties includes declared folder columns; Sort keeps its existing shown/record/formula discovery and missing-current-key fallback. The Filter menu's Property is the same `ColumnPicker` (YAZ-1466), on top-level and nested-group rows alike, built from the shared `view/properties.ts` `propertyOptions` helper Sort also uses. The same component has a `multiple` mode (🔒 D6, YAZ-1467) — the Filter menu's value checklist (trigger labelled `Value`, like the typed box it replaces; `noun="options"` rewords the search box, its label and the no-match line — "Search options…", "No options found."): a click or Enter TOGGLES one value and writes once, the panel stays open until Escape or click-away, and a stored value no record carries still lists itself, ticked. `multiple` defaults off, so Sort's picker is untouched. Toolbar popovers constrain themselves to the viewport and clipping ancestors, resize with their content and allow internal option scrolling without dismissal.

Sort priority (YAZ-1396): drag the same six-dot grip used for columns, or press ArrowUp/ArrowDown while it is focused. The complete rule moves with its direction in one write; cancellation, same-position and boundary moves write nothing. Duplicate-property rules have distinct local UI identities that survive reparse after our edits and are never serialized. A changed external sort invalidates an in-flight drag. Single-rule grips are disabled. Grouping levels are not draggable. The compact sort grid uses a flexible property field, fixed direction width, equal control heights and a separate grouping section; only the new picker removes the native select width cap.

Bulk property visibility (YAZ-1384): Table and Board offer **Select all** and **Unselect all** above the Properties checklist when search is empty. During search, the labels become **Select results / Unselect results** and affect only matching canonical keys, preserving unmatched visibility and shown order. No matches disables both actions. Select all writes the menu’s canonical-deduplicated keys, retaining shown order and appending hidden properties, including declared columns and formulas. Unselect all writes explicit `order: []`, including hiding `file.name`; the existing `writeOrder` path removes frozen columns. Each effective click makes one active-view settings write; satisfied actions are disabled and dimmed. The menu remains available with no visible columns, and Board retains its blank card shells. Selecting all after clearing uses the default menu order and does not restore the previous custom order or frozen columns. Cards and List do not offer these actions.

Body (4A placeholder, view types with no real body yet): one row per result — `file.name` as a button calling `onOpenFile(record.path)`, then the other `order` values `render()`ed and ` · `-joined. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-846, completed in YAZ-861): the two index states that used to replace the body whatever the view type — `.view-view__pending` "Loading the vault index…" and `.view-view__error` "Could not load the vault index: …" — went with `indexStatus` / `indexError`; the one host there is always has its snapshot in hand. The `__pending` class outlived them by one wave, holding a "no views — [Add view]" branch that could not be reached — `readViews` and `folderViewSet` BOTH fall back to `DEFAULT_VIEWS`, so `def.views` is never empty — and YAZ-861 deleted branch, rule and class together, on the FilterMenu's precedent (below): unreachable UI with a live code path behind it is worse than no UI. `.views-pane__error` (renamed from `.view-view__error` with the pane) survives and still carries the create / properties / settings-write failures. A folder with no notes renders an empty body, which is the truth. Tests: `view/keys.test.ts`, `view/Toolbar.test.tsx` (mounts ViewsPane as a folder's views over `TEST_RECORDS` + a views fixture, `testFolderHost()` supplying the required bundle; asserts YAML via `serializeViews` next to the DOM). The tabs are pinned there in four describes (YAZ-1471): **drag to reorder** — a drop on a tab's LEFT half inserts before it in ONE write, the strip's empty tail lands it LAST with the last tab marked `--insert-after`, a drop back on its own slot writes nothing, the ACTIVE view follows its tab both when passed and when dragged, a tab in rename is not draggable, and the payload is the PRIVATE view-tab mime (a `text/plain` name would paste into an editor); **the right-click menu** — opening writes nothing, Rename refuses a duplicate or empty name (the field snaps back, nothing written) and commits a free one in ONE update on Enter OR blur (once, never twice), Duplicate clones after and opens the copy and is disabled for an outline, Delete is disabled on the last view and ASKS first (Escape and click-away both cancel and write nothing), confirming is ONE update, and the selection hands over to the right neighbour (the left one for the last tab); **"+"** — the picker lists every type in menu order and opening writes nothing, Outline is offered only while the folder has none, picking appends in ONE update and mounts the tab in rename (Escape keeps the given name, typing over it is the SECOND update), and the name is the first FREE one off the type label; **overflow** — the ACTIVE tab is scrolled into view on mount and on every activation, and the tablist IS the scroller with the "+" outside it. `view/ConfirmDeleteView.test.ts` pins the sheet's pure copy apart from the sheet (`deleteViewMessage`: an outline names the document that goes with it, every other view its configuration). `FolderView.test.tsx` drives the same arc through the real host: a folder with no settings file shows Table then Board and opening writes NOTHING, its first change writes the whole block into `.folder.md` with the default columns stated, a toolbar change → ONE whole-key `folder_settings` write through the one door, switching a view writes NOTHING, the tabs edit, an open menu or sheet survives the views list shrinking under it (YAZ-1488), an own-write echo arriving after a newer optimistic edit does not take it back (YAZ-1241), and the default view is pinned end to end (a saved START opens there and opening writes nothing, a stale name falls back to the first view silently, `Page → Default view` is ONE whole-key write carrying the name, `First view` clears the key, a rename CARRIES the saved START and a delete CLEARS it in the SAME write, and an external edit that changes ONLY `defaultView` rebuilds the def — D4's proof that it rides in the stamp). `folderSettings.test.ts` pins 🔒 D3's retirement from the read side: a persisted list WITHOUT a board reads back without one (what the card says is what you get, YAZ-1471), a persisted list WITH a board — whatever its name — passes through untouched, and `DEFAULT_VIEWS` is two — Table, then Board, no Outline (🔒 D5a, YAZ-2290) — each read handing back its own copy.

Table (4B, GRO-2136, YAZ-742, YAZ-1151, `view/TableView.tsx`, `.view-table`): `type: table` renders a `<table>` over the post-search rows — sticky header (labels via `propertyLabel`), columns = `propertyKeys` in `order`, widths from `view.columnSize` (px per key, default 150; drag the header's right-edge handle, min 60, live preview, ONE write on mouseup), `view.rowHeight` presets short/medium/tall → 28/44/68px via `--view-table-row-h`. The folder's `.editor-host` remains the ONLY vertical scroller and `.view-table-wrap` remains the table's horizontal scroller. Because native sticky otherwise chooses that horizontal wrapper for both axes, `TableView` listens to the nearest vertical ancestor and translates the existing `<thead>` by `--view-table-header-y`: zero before the table reaches the viewport, the outer scroll delta while rows remain, and clamped at `table height − header height` so it exits with the table bottom. Scroll events coalesce into one animation frame; resize observation refreshes layout changes; unmount removes every listener, observer, pending frame and style. The first effective `view.frozenColumns` real cells in every header/body/summary row are CSS-sticky horizontally, with cumulative `left` offsets derived from those same live widths; resizing a leading column therefore moves every later frozen offset during preview without another state model. Header intersections sit above body cells, frozen cells have an opaque background, and the two axes compose because vertical motion belongs to `<thead>` while horizontal `left` offsets remain on its `<th>` cells; the header remains vertically sticky even when the frozen count is zero. Group headers and window spacers stay ordinary full-width `colSpan` rows — only real property cells freeze — so grouping, keyboard coordinates and vertical windowing keep one semantic table and their existing structure. Inside each group row, the complete compact controls cluster is horizontally sticky at the Table's left padding edge independently of `frozenColumns`; the spanning cell itself remains ordinary, never becomes a data/frozen cell, and is not vertically sticky. A TWO-LEVEL grouping (YAZ-745, YAZ-1100) is still ONE flat display list — an outer header, then its merge-rule `direct` rows, then one inner section per child, whose header cell carries `.view-table__group-cell--nested` — so windowing, `data-cell` coordinates and keyboard navigation are untouched, and collapse-all covers both levels in document order against the store's key-count cap. The inner indent is one 28px step applied to that cluster's sticky `left` (36px against the outer's 8px), NOT to cell padding, precisely because the cluster pins at `left` while the table scrolls sideways: padding would slide the inner label back onto the outer's offset at the right edge, which is the YAZ-1043-46 mechanism honoured once per level. Collapsing an outer folds its whole BRANCH, inner headers included, while the inner section's own collapse survives underneath it — its key being outer-scoped, see "Folders are the pages": Collapsed groups. The `file.name` cell is a `.view-table__link` button → `onOpenFile`; other cells by type — numbers right-aligned (`.view-table__cell--num`), booleans as checkboxes, lists and links/files as chips (`.view-table__chip`, `--link` — an id link's chip reads as its note's title, YAZ-2293 D8, see "Note ids": What a link reads as), ErrorValues as a red `#ERROR` chip with the message in `title`, everything else `render()` (editing: see "Cell editing" below). A secondary click on any non-editing data cell first focuses that exact cell, closes an open hover preview, then opens `PageContextMenu` for the row's exact absolute `record.path` (and its `record.id`, for Copy ID — YAZ-2293); typed editors keep the native menu. With `view.preview: true` (YAZ-1244), resting on a data row opens that page's read-only body after the shared hover-intent delay — the preview is handed the window's link source, so an id link in it reads as a title (YAZ-2293); leaving uses the shared grace window, while drag start and secondary click close it immediately. Summary row pinned in `<tfoot>`: each cell is a click-to-pick kind (None + `BUILTIN_SUMMARIES` + `def.summaries` names) writing `view.summaries[columnKey]` (existing keys matched canonically; removing the last kind deletes the map), value shown via `summarize` over the shown rows. Arrow keys move focus between body cells (`data-cell="row:col"`), Enter on a name cell opens the note — ⌘⏎ a background tab, ⌥⏎ the right panel, the name link's click rule (YAZ-1557, `lib/openGesture.ts`); a BOARD card is the same kind of cell (🔒 D2): every card is `tabIndex=0`, a plain click selects it (the table cell's focus ring), arrows walk ↑↓ through the column's cards in DOM order (nested sections included) and ←→ to the same row of the neighbour column, clamped — DOM-driven off `:scope > .view-board__col`, not a parallel index, because a column is a tree and the DOM already is its reading order — and the title, Enter, or a modifier opens; a title-less card keeps `aria-label={record.name}` and has no role; one click selects an editable property cell and a double-click opens its editor. Above 500 rows only a scroll-positioned slice of `<tr>`s is mounted between spacer rows (no library); rows render from one flat slice so 4C can interleave group header rows. Tests: `view/frozenColumns.test.ts`, `view/TableView.test.tsx`, `view/TableNestedGroups.test.tsx`, and the dedicated `desktop/e2e/freezeColumns.spec.ts` (kept out of YAZ-999's shared folder-column spec) beside `desktop/e2e/nestedGroups.spec.ts` for the two-level render and its per-level sticky label.

**The Table since YAZ-1513** (the column overhaul, YAZ-1537–1551; header drag YAZ-1548; polish YAZ-1549): the `#` GUTTER is the first, always-sticky column (`.view-table__gutter`, fixed width, the frozen offsets starting after it) — 1-based and RESTARTING at every group header, nested sections included, a DISPLAY POSITION and never an id: no `data-cell` and no tabIndex (outside the arrow-key grid), not a drag source or target, nothing to summarise. `ViewDef.rowNumbers?: boolean` decides it, ABSENT = shown, so a shown gutter leaves no key behind — Properties → Table → **Row numbers**, or the `#` header's own right-click, whose only item is "Hide row numbers". The NAME cell renders the page TITLE — `record.basename`, no extension — in every skin through ONE shared renderer (`GroupHeader.tsx` `rowTitle`: the table's name cell, the board's card title, the card's title, the list's primary), while `file.name`'s VALUE keeps its extension for the engine, sort and filter; its label is `defaultLabel` = **Name**. A HEADER RIGHT-CLICK (`view/TableHeaderMenu.tsx`, on the row menu's own surface) offers **Rename column…** (an inline field holding the stored display name with the default label as its placeholder — Enter commits through `setDisplayName`, an emptied field goes back to the default, Esc cancels), **Hide column** (a table may hide `file.name`, YAZ-1007), **Add column to the right…** (the SAME "+ Add column" form, the new key landing after this one) and **Delete column…** (disabled with a tooltip for the undeletable tiers, otherwise the confirm sheet — "Folder settings: `.folder.md`": Delete column). HEADER DRAG (YAZ-1548) reorders a property header by dropping it on another header's left or right half — the Properties list's slot rule — and writes ONE order; the gutter is neither source nor target. `view/columnOrder.ts` `withOrder(view, order)` is THE one order writer, carrying the frozen clamp (YAZ-1007: the prefix is a count over the shown columns, so a shorter order clamps it and an empty order deletes the key): the Properties checklist, reorder and bulk buttons, "+ Add column", the header menu's Hide and Add-right, the header drag and a delete's prune all spell an order through it, so no view can hold an order its `frozenColumns` disagrees with. The PROPERTIES panel is TWO-LEVEL: the list row is grip · checkbox · type icon · label, the key shown only when two labels collide; opening a row is the DETAIL — an editable title (the label, the same `setDisplayName`), the key, the type, options (select / multi-select), the relation, card styling (Board only), "Back to columns", Hide in this view and Delete column…. Every declaration edit reaches disk through the host's doors ("Folder settings: `.folder.md`": The write path), and `FolderView` keeps an optimistic AHEAD copy of `columns` — exposed as `FolderHost.settings.columns`, exactly as `parsed` runs ahead of the index for the views — so a declaration written and not yet echoed back is what the next gesture (a delete's prune, a second add) reads.

Board (4D, GRO-2138, YAZ-1177, `view/BoardView.tsx`, `.view-board`): `type: board` is OUR schema extension (D1) — the view keys are `type: board`, `groupBy` (required for columns), `order` (the card fields) and `cardSize`. The tolerant reader remains the shared `view/cardWidth.ts`: a finite positive number is px, legacy small/medium/large presets are 220/280/340, and anything else is 280. The Properties editor above is intentionally Board-only and writes numeric `cardSize`; it does not alter Cards' `cardSize`, Table's per-column widths, or the global content-width setting. Every outer column gets that one width through `--view-board-col-w`, so columns stay equal and nested child sections inherit their outer column's geometry; there is deliberately no per-column resize drag or preset picker. Every key round-trips byte-for-byte through the parser (`viewSchema.test.ts` board fixture) — a property that mattered for Obsidian interop while `.base` files existed and now simply means a hand-written board survives an edit to any other view. With ONE group level, columns remain the engine's post-search groups on one horizontally scrolling row (a column with no shown cards disappears, "No value" last). With TWO levels, there is still exactly ONE column per outer group; the outer's merge-rule `direct` cards render first, then its child groups stack vertically inside that column. Every outer and child header uses the shared `GroupHeader` content (chevron, typed value, count, summaries over that level's SHOWN cards). Outer collapse folds the whole branch; child collapse hides only that child's cards and persists under the outer-scoped `nestedGroupKeyOf` ("Folders are the pages": Collapsed groups). Cards: when `file.name` is present in `order`, it renders as a `.view-board__title` button → `onOpenFile`; when omitted, the title/open button is omitted and an otherwise-empty order leaves blank draggable card shells; the other `order` properties render as small label/value rows (`propertyLabel` + the table's typed `cellContent` — shared from `GroupHeader.tsx`; a missing value keeps the label with an empty value). A secondary click anywhere on a rendered `.view-board__card` closes an open hover preview, then opens the shared `PageContextMenu` actions in the fixed order Open in new tab → Copy path → Copy ID (YAZ-2293 — offered only when the row's own record carries an `id`) → Reveal in Finder, always targeting that rendered row's exact absolute `record.path` — including hidden titles, direct/inner nested cards, and repeated fanned-out cards — while headers, add controls, placeholders, and the no-group hint retain the native menu; background open leaves the folder's tab active, and failed clipboard/reveal actions report through the passive notice. With `view.preview: true` (YAZ-1244), resting on any rendered card opens that page's read-only body after the shared hover-intent delay — handed the window's link source like the table's (YAZ-2293); leaving uses the shared grace window, while drag start and secondary click close it immediately. Per-property `view.cardStyle` (YAZ-1206, reshaped by YAZ-1217's JOIN model) lays those rows out: a card is `.view-board__line` rows following `view.order`, where each key starts a new line unless its `join: true` continues the line being built — appended right after a muted `.view-board__dash` element (never a schema flag); consecutive joins chain onto one line, join on the first rendered key is a no-op, and the title is UN-PINNED — `file.name` renders at its order position, can be joined onto and can itself join, while ignoring `bold`/`underline`/`hideLabel` (those class the note rows; `hideLabel` drops the label span). Because the flags ride the shared `cardList`, cards inside subgroup sections inherit them unchanged. A writable group header's `+` creates in that exact scope; a nested child seeds BOTH raw note properties, while `file.*` and `formula.*` levels remain read-only. Without `groupBy` the body is a centered `.view-board__hint` whose "Group by…" button writes the first non-file property as `view.groupBy { property, direction: ASC }` through the file (one write; the Sort menu edits it after). Cards drag at their visible level — direct cards between outer columns, child cards between child sections, including across outer columns. An inner target is never a descendant of the outer target, so one drop cannot bubble into a second move. A cross-outer child move updates both raw properties through ONE `writeProperties` whole-file transaction; the ordinary one-key path still uses `writeProperty`. Out of scope: a third grouping level, swimlanes and board card images. Tests: `view/BoardView.test.tsx`, `view/NestedGroupActions.test.tsx`, `writeProperty.test.ts`, and `desktop/e2e/nestedGroups.spec.ts`; card styles and the grip additionally in `view/Toolbar.test.tsx`, `desktop/e2e/boardCardStyles.spec.ts` and `desktop/e2e/propertiesReorder.spec.ts`.

Cards (4E, GRO-2139, `view/CardsView.tsx`, `.view-cards`): `type: cards` (Obsidian's schema; view keys `image`, `cardSize`, `imageFit`, `imageAspectRatio`) renders a responsive grid — `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(var(--view-card-w), 1fr))`, width from the shared `cardWidth` (Obsidian's numeric px, or the legacy small/medium/large presets). Each card: an optional cover, then `file.name` as a `.view-card__title` button → `onOpenFile`, then the view's other `order` properties as label/value rows (`propertyLabel` + the shared typed `cellContent`). The view's `image` names a per-note property; the note's VALUE decides the cover — `#rgb`/`#rrggbb` → a colour block, `http(s)://` → the URL straight into `src`, a wikilink `[[pic.png]]` or plain path → a local vault asset through `api.readAsset` (bridge table above) turned into a `data:` URL, cached per root+ref so a grid never fetches one cover twice. While loading, on any failure, or with no/an unresolvable value the cover is the neutral `.view-card__cover--empty` placeholder (never a broken image); only note properties resolve (`file.`/`formula.` image keys → placeholder); a view without `image` renders no cover element at all. `imageFit` cover|contain and `imageAspectRatio` (number, default 1:1) land as `--view-card-fit` / `--view-card-ratio` on the grid. Grouped results render 4C sections (the shared `GroupHeader` over each group's own grid) with the SAME persisted collapse state as the table/board (`baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]`, never the folder's settings file); search narrows cards and drops empty groups. A lightbox is out of scope; property editing: see "Cell editing". Tests: `view/CardsView.test.tsx`, `desktop/src/main/fs/assets.test.ts`.

List (4F, GRO-2140, `view/ListView.tsx`, `.view-list`): `type: list` (Obsidian's schema; view keys `markerStyle`, `indentProperties`, `propertySeparator`) renders one item per record. The FIRST property in `order` is the primary line (Obsidian: the primary list item is whatever sits on top of the Properties menu): `file.name` — the default when `order` is empty or absent — renders as a `.view-list__title` button → `onOpenFile`; any other first property renders its typed value (`cellContent`) as `.view-list__primary` and file.name is NOT implicitly added. The remaining `order` properties render inline after the primary — `render()`ed, empty values skipped, joined by `propertySeparator` (default `, `) — or, with `indentProperties: true`, as indented label/value rows beneath it (`propertyLabel` + the shared typed `cellContent`). `markerStyle` bullet | number | none draws the item marker (default bullet; number = the ordinal within its list, restarting per group; an unknown value falls back to bullet). Grouped results render 4C sections (the shared `GroupHeader` over each group's own list) with the SAME persisted collapse state as the table/board/cards (`baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]`, never the folder's settings file); search narrows items and drops empty groups. The three keys are edited in the Properties menu's List section (above). Property editing: see "Cell editing". Tests: `view/ListView.test.tsx`.

### Cell editing (5B — GRO-2141 write path, GRO-2142 editors)

`transformFile(path, transform)` (`client/src/views/writeProperty.ts`) is the shared application-level whole-file write boundary: read the current bytes, apply a pure transform, write with `expectedMtime`; on `CONFLICT` re-read and recompute once, then surface a second conflict. A no-op never touches disk. `writeProperties(path, writes)` is its ordered multi-key specialization, applying every `setFrontmatterProperty` transform before the single guarded disk write; `writeProperty(path, key, value)` delegates to it for the ordinary one-key case. This matters when one gesture changes coupled keys — YAZ-1177's cross-outer child drag — because parallel same-file writes can each succeed while the last one silently erases the other. Broken frontmatter (`FrontmatterWriteError`) propagates and nothing is written. `writeFolderValues(path, folderId, writes)` is the same boundary over ONE folder's block of `in` (D19) — every value a folder's views write, and the panel's folder rows. The properties panel's TYPED ROWS (⚡ YAZ-884) call `writeProperty` AS IS for the note's own fields, one key per row edit / add / delete, then apply the same one-key transform to the panel's own copy so its raw fallback cannot fall behind. Its RAW mode (⚡ YAZ-883, "The properties panel") keeps its purpose-built `replaceFrontmatter` flow. ONE file is created by a write (🔒 D1, YAZ-2290): `readForWrite`, the read every `transformFile` starts with, creates a folder's `.folder.md` when it is not there yet ("Folder settings: `.folder.md`"); for every other path a missing file is still `NOT_FOUND`.

Editor inference (`client/src/views/editorType.ts`, locked precedence — `columnTyping` computes the per-column halves once per render, `cellEditor` adds the per-note value on top). The LADDER, top rung first: (1) the FOLDER's own `folder_settings.columns` declaration (its `.folder.md`, or the default columns while it has none), view-scoped — WIRED in YAZ-819 (`columnTyping(key, records, properties, settings)` reading `settings.columns[<bare>]`, threaded `ViewsPane` → `TableView` / `CardsView` / `ListView`), see "Folder settings: `.folder.md`": Typing is per folder; (2) a vault-wide declaration in `.yaseendocs/properties.json` (`PropertiesResponse`, via `useProperties` — see "Properties"), which also carries the link/multi-link `target` that narrows the picker — WIRED in YAZ-846, App-owned (ONE `useProperties(root)` per window) and threaded App → `Editor` → `FolderView` → `ViewsPane`; a corrupt `properties.json` arrives as a `properties.error`, renders its own passive line and the column simply falls through; (3) the note's own YAML value; (4) the dominant value type across the view's records for a note without the key; (5) text, the final fallback. `file.*` and `formula.*` never get an editor (read-only). TOMBSTONE (⚡ YAZ-815, ruled by Yasin): between (2) and (3) sat an explicit `.obsidian/types.json` assignment, delivered as `IndexResponse.types` — an imported artifact ranking BELOW the vault's own schema. It was UNWIRED from the day rung 2 landed, because its data rode `IndexResponse`, which the one surface that mounts these editors deliberately never fetches (🔒 D2 — it reads the window's link snapshot instead), so `columnTyping`'s `types` parameter was passed `undefined` by every caller. A rung with no feed is not a rung: the rung, the parameter, the `IndexResponse` field and the per-call read of another app's file are all deleted. Kinds: text / number / checkbox / date / list / link / multi-link. TOMBSTONE: the type-scoped rung that used to sit above all of these — a `page_type`-keyed per-type schema — died with the type system in YAZ-836.

`view/EditableCell.tsx` is one editable property cell, shared by table cells and card/list property rows: its display (typed like a read-only cell) opens the editor directly in cards/lists, while `TableView` selects the editable `<td>` on a single click and delegates a double-click — including on an empty cell — or Enter on its focused cell to that same `[data-edit]` control. In table edit mode the real `<td>` is the cell-sized surface and sole geometry owner: the shared editor fills its normal containing block with no measured/copied geometry, while cards and lists keep their compact shared-editor layout unchanged. A table checkbox follows the same deliberate gesture and toggles exactly once. Clicks inside an open editor stay with that editor; nested activation is never replayed. Enter/blur commit through the host's `onCommit` — the cell has no writer of its own: a folder's views pass `FolderHost.writeValues` (D19), the properties panel its own (⚡ YAZ-884, "The properties panel") — and Esc cancels; the link editor completes `[[…]]` over the index's basenames through the shared matcher (`links/completion.ts` — one matcher with the editor's `[[` picker, rule 24; GRO-2191), each suggestion READING as the basename and WRITING the note's id — `[[<id>]]` — when it has one, `[[basename]]` when it has none (`basenameCandidates`, YAZ-2293; the multi-link chips editor completes the same way). A stored id link RESTS as its note's title — in the cell, and on the chips editor's chips — while the editor's text and every commit stay the stored form, so an unchanged Enter writes nothing and an edited list writes the stored ids back (YAZ-2293 D8). The `list` editor remains free-form chips: Enter adds the pending text as one item, a comma is literal text, and the committed value is a YAML array. Commits are optimistic: the committed value renders immediately and stays until the index refetch delivers it; a failed write reverts the cell and shows an inline error. Checkboxes are live and commit on every toggle, no edit mode. An untouched chips editor (no chip added/removed, no pending text) never commits — its seed stringifies the list, so a no-op commit would rewrite `[1, 2]` as `['1', '2']` (7B, GRO-2148). The note's own open editor window absorbs the resulting frontmatter-only `change` silently (GRO-2186, see the autosave note under "Bridge API").

### Group drag (5C — GRO-2143)

`view/groupDrag.ts`, shared by the board's columns and the grouped table's sections. `dragKey(view)` is the bare frontmatter key a drop writes — null (drag disabled) unless `groupBy` is a note property. (It was also off under the views' `readOnly`, the read-only embed chrome's prop; that lost its last caller with the `.base` format in YAZ-844 and was deleted in YAZ-846 — see the View chrome tombstone.) `useGroupDrag(key, onMove)` owns the transient HTML5 drag state: `source(path, gk)` spreads on each card/row, `target(group)` on each column/section (rows included — events bubble), the hovered target highlights, a drop on the source group is a no-op, and Esc cancels (real drags fire `dragend`; a document keydown fallback covers jsdom). A drop on ANOTHER group fires `onMove(path, value)` with the target's raw YAML value read off that group's FIRST row — so the written type matches what the group's notes already carry — or `undefined` for "No value", which DELETES the key. **Fanned-out groups SWAP instead** (YAZ-671 D3): because the row sits in several groups, overwriting with a neighbour's whole list would destroy its other values, so `onMove` carries a third `GroupSwap { remove, add }` argument — `remove` is the source group's value (`drag.key`, which is why the drag state carries the group VALUE and not just its `groupKeyOf` string) and `add` is the target's; either is null at "No value". ViewsPane edits the page's list rather than replacing it: elements are matched with the engine's own `equals` over `fromYaml` and NO resolver — the exact comparison that decided the grouping — so a swap can only remove the element that put the row in that group, and every other value survives. Dropping a fanned-out row on "No value" removes just that element and never deletes the key. `dragKey` is now `groupByKey` (one name; the group "+" needs the same key whether or not a drag is in flight). ViewsPane owns the optimistic side: `PendingMove { key, value, prevRaw }` per path, patched into the records BEFORE the engine runs (`applyMoves`) so the card lands in its target group with sort/values/summaries consistent, committed through `FolderHost.writeValues` (D19). Clearing follows 5B's discipline — an entry clears only when the index refetch moves that key's raw off `prevRaw` (our write landing, or a concurrent writer winning), never before. A failed write drops the move (the card snaps back) and flags the card/row inline (`moveError`). Tests: `view/GroupDrag.test.tsx`.

### New note (5D — GRO-2144)

`newNote.ts`, behind the toolbar's **New** button and each group header's "+". `deriveSeed(def, view)` is pure over the filter ASTs (def + view): equality leaves `note.x == <literal>` seed `x` with the literal's YAML type and `file.hasTag("t")` adds to the seed's own `tags` (the note's tags, never a folder's value) — only **and-reachable** leaves count (`or`/`not` subtrees are skipped entirely; seeding those would not, or would anti-, satisfy the view), and non-equality rules are ignored by design (locked kickoff decision). A group "+" additionally seeds the group's property with the raw value of that group's first row ("No value" seeds nothing). Under FAN-OUT (YAZ-671 D4) it seeds `[render(group.key)]` instead — that group's own element as a one-item list, because the first row's raw value is a neighbour's WHOLE list there; `render()` returns a link to its `[[…]]` form, the same one the picker inserts. "No value" still seeds nothing. PLACEMENT is the folder's, always (🔒 D4/E1/E3, YAZ-2290): `FolderHost.create` (`FolderView` `createInFolder`) births the note IN the folder being viewed, from the folder's `.template.md` and the seed and nothing else — no column is stamped empty — so a group "+" still seeds its group ("Folders are the pages": Births). TOMBSTONE (YAZ-846): the plain path — `targetFolder` (the `inFolder` seed under the root, else the page's own folder, else the root) plus `ViewsPane`'s `createFromSeed` — is deleted, function and all, and the folder half of the seed followed it (YAZ-2318): `deriveSeed` no longer reads `file.inFolder`, and `NewNoteSeed` is `{ properties, tags? }`: the values go into the opened folder's block, the tags to the note's top level ("Folder settings": Values). The name is `freeName` over the basenames of the notes that live in the folder (`Untitled`, `Untitled 2`, `Untitled 3`…) — or off a typed name, the board's inline add (YAZ-943), `/` turned to a space. `createNote` (`scaffold.ts`) is ONE atomic content-at-create call — `createFile({ path, content })`, the content being what the seed makes of the template's frontmatter, plus the template's body — no create-then-write second step and no race; an empty seed in a folder with no template still creates the file, holding only the `id` the create door gives it (YAZ-2293, bridge table). Success opens the note; failure shows `.views-pane__error` "Could not create note: …" and opens nothing. Tests: `newNote.test.ts`, `view/NewNote.test.tsx`.


## Document magnification (YAZ-1410)

Each Markdown `CrepeHost` owns a temporary percentage, default **100%**. The control is ONE
segmented pill immediately left of Sync — `− | 100% | +` (YAZ-1710, which retired YAZ-1430's
dropdown arrow: the percentage alone opens the dropdown). `−` / `+` step to the next preset in
that direction, a custom value snapping to the nearest preset that way (117 → 125 or 100); the
end button is disabled at 50 / 400. A step applies at once through the same commit path as a
preset: it closes an open menu, discards its draft (valid or invalid) and does not move focus,
so repeated clicks keep stepping (`stepZoom` in `editor/DocumentZoom.tsx`). The KEYS take a finer
ladder above 200 (`stepZoomByKey`, D16): presets to 200, then 25 at a time to 400, a custom value
snapping to that grid in the step direction (210 → 225 up, 200 down) — 200 → 300 is one click in
the menu but too big a jump under a held key. A labeled Custom input above the presets accepts
whole numbers **50–400**, with an optional `%` suffix, and presets are **50, 75, 90, 100, 125,
150, 200, 300, 400** (D12). Enter or leaving the control applies a valid draft;
Escape cancels it. Invalid input preserves the applied value and keeps the panel open with a
short range message, including after an outside dismissal attempt; focus is not pulled back.
Choosing a preset replaces the draft and closes the list. Typing a partial number never resizes
the document. Explicit completion or Escape returns focus to the trigger; outside dismissal
does not steal focus from the destination.

Note and outline editors use the native text caret through Crepe's `Cursor.virtual: false`
setting. The virtual overlay and its caret-hiding class are absent: its viewport measurements
would scale twice inside CSS zoom. Click/selection coordinates are not remapped. The Cursor
feature stays enabled for gap/drop support. The virtual directional affinity tail and its
extra formatting-boundary arrow step are intentionally removed; native/editor mark behavior
applies. Tests cover the shared factory, preserved selection/typing/undo and boundary keys.

The zoom lives on the CONTENT, never on a shell (YAZ-1710 D14). The scroller `.editor-host` carries
only `--document-zoom`; `app.css` applies `zoom: var(--document-zoom)` to the children of every
block (`.editor-host > :not(.editor-mount) > *`) and to the body's `.ProseMirror` node itself. The
`display: contents` properties panel is one of those children and passes the zoom on to its pieces
through computed style — listing the pieces again zoomed the chip twice (6B1). Each block's column shell — `max-width`,
`margin: 0 auto`, the 48px gutters — stays in unzoomed px, so the five blocks keep one aligned
column and the gutters never grow; bullet indent (`--list-indent: 2.15em`) scales with the text
on purpose (damping was tried and rejected, D13). Crepe appends its floating chrome — toolbar,
slash menu, link preview/edit, block handle — beside `.ProseMirror` inside `.milkdown`, so it is
unzoomed and floating-ui positions it in unzoomed space, the only space it gets right (a
counter-`zoom` rescales the popup's own left/top; a counter-`transform` leaves a z× layout box
that `flip()`/`shift()` cannot keep on screen at 400%). The block handle's probe
(`blockHandleTarget.ts`) therefore scales with `cssZoom(view.dom)`, not with the handle.
The control/status row, tabs, sidebar and dialogs outside the scroller keep their usual size.
Retained editor mounts preserve zoom across tab switches; closing/reopening or another remount
(including path changes) resets it. Separate mounted editors, including right panes, are
independent. Zoom does not change Markdown, autosave, IPC, settings, or persisted app state.
Non-Markdown viewers, Electron's existing application zoom and outline bullet-focus zoom keep
their separate behavior. No Fit mode or persistent preference. `⌘+` / `⌘−` / `⌘0` step this ladder
while focus is inside the note's section and zoom the whole app otherwise (YAZ-1710, see the menu
table). **Caret anchor (D11):** every zoom change — keys or pill — goes through one door,
`changeZoom` in `Editor.tsx`, which snapshots the caret's offset inside the scroller BEFORE the zoom
(when the selection is inside this note's section and visible); a `useLayoutEffect` then scrolls
by exactly what the zoom moved it, so the line being edited does not budge on screen, like a design
tool zooming around the cursor. A caret that was off screen, or none in this note, is brought to
`scrollIntoView({ block: 'center' })`. **Sideways slack (D15):** the scroller is `overflow-x: auto`
with a zero-height `::after` runner `calc(100% + var(--zoom-slack))`; `Editor.tsx` MEASURES
`--zoom-slack` = the deepest bullet's indent at this zoom minus the same at 100% (over
`.list-item > .children`, on every zoom change and on a `ResizeObserver` of the body), so a full
drag parks the deepest bullet where it sits unzoomed and never further — the page still reflows on
screen (panning to a fixed column was tried and rejected); 0 at 100% and for a note with no bullets.

DOM geometry used for pointer hit areas, drag distances and pinned folder-table headers must
convert viewport measurements to local layout units when inside a scaled block (`cssZoom` on the
element being measured — the scroller itself is unscaled since D14).

Milkdown table drag previews and insertion indicators use the version-pinned
`@milkdown/components@7.22.1` archive in `client/vendor`, enforced for all transitive consumers
by the root npm override. The narrow patch converts viewport sizes/offsets to local CSS pixels;
it preserves row/column drop targeting and the existing 100% behavior. Rebuild/provenance
instructions live in `client/vendor/milkdown-components-7.22.1-yaz1410.md`. The shipped runtime
has geometry regression coverage at 50%, 100%, 125% and 200%; upgrades must preserve those tests.
The same archive carries YAZ-2131 4B: Crepe's list-item node view restores the caret it saw at
mount once per editor view per frame (the latest capture of a still-mounted item wins, as the last
of the old per-item dispatches did), never once per list item — N items were N whole-plugin
transactions in one frame. `listItemCaretRestore.test.ts` pins it on the installed runtime.
The same pipeline (`tools/buildMilkdownPatch.mjs`, one entry per package) carries a pinned
`@milkdown/plugin-tooltip@7.22.1` for YAZ-2238: a throttled `TooltipProvider` update is skipped only
when the state matches both the call's `prevState` AND the state its last evaluated update saw, so a
no-op transaction can no longer swallow the update that hides Crepe's floating toolbar (a stale
toolbar after ⌘Z covered the row above and kept the block handle off it). Provenance in
`client/vendor/milkdown-plugin-tooltip-7.22.1-yaz2238.md`; `tooltipThrottle.test.ts` pins it on the
installed runtime.

### Configured empty Board columns (YAZ-1417)

`view.showEmptyColumns` defaults off and is saved per Board view. `boardOptionGroups` inserts configured Select/Multi-select options after engine evaluation, retaining observed unlisted/typed values. `ASC` means property option order and `DESC` reverses it; the Sort menu labels this override explicitly. Within-column card sorting stays independent. Search retains configured destinations when the toggle is on.

Empty destinations carry an exact `optionValue`; drag and New card can therefore write/seed a value without borrowing the first card. Multi-select targets remain fanned out and cross-outer nested moves retain the existing atomic two-property write. Nested groups obey the engine merge rule: an inner value equal to its outer belongs in `outer.direct`, not a duplicate empty child.

Verification lives in `boardOptionGroups.test.ts`, `PropertyDefinitionEditor.test.tsx`, `SelectValueEditor.test.tsx`, `writeFolderColumn.test.ts`, `FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx`, `Toolbar.test.tsx`, and the existing Board/drag/typing/filter/scaffold suites. No new colors, bulk record migrations, duplication/layout customization or AI property features are included.
